# CONTRACT.md — Vector

This is the product Vector is building towards, not a description of what ships today. Every clause has a stable ID, states the behavior a buyer gets, names the acceptance evidence that proves it through a public door (a browser journey, an HTTP route, a command, a signed webhook, a built mobile app), and carries a status judged from the current code and tests: **done** (implemented and exercised by the cited test), **partial** (some of the behavior exists at the cited file, the rest is open) or **missing** (no implementation yet). The status table at the end lists every clause. Difficulty — native apps, provider apps, coding-agent sandboxes, live syncs — is recorded as engineering work in a clause, never as a reason to drop it.

Security, privacy and operational requirements are in BASELINE.md, and no product clause supersedes a baseline clause. Capacity and rate limits in seed.json remain estimates; a stated latency is a target until measured. Clauses marked *Policy* name intended replaceable behavior. Dated evidence distinguishes executed checks from unmet obligations.

## Scope

Vector is a full Linear/Jira-class issue tracker for software teams. A team that runs on Linear, Jira Software or GitHub Issues and Projects — or on the up-and-comers Plane and Huly — can move to Vector and keep doing the whole job: fast keyboard-first issues, triage, cycles, projects, initiatives and roadmaps; roles and private teams; reporting; mobile apps; follow-up automation; and the integrations the category runs on (GitHub, GitLab, Slack, Microsoft Teams, Zendesk, Intercom, Sentry, Figma, Notion and the coding agents). Work flows from report to merged change and on to a verified fix: an accepted issue can be delegated to a coding agent that opens a tested pull request under human review, the deployment is tracked, and the issue closes only when the user-facing problem is shown to be gone.

**Buyer.** A US software team of 5 to 500 people — engineering, product and design, with support and leadership as readers — replacing Linear, a Jira Software project or GitHub Projects. Self-hosted on the buyer's own Cloudflare account, free and open source; money appears only as customer revenue in USD (CUST-005).

**Separation from sibling products.** Vector keeps the market's separation and links to its siblings rather than rebuilding them:

- **Penny** owns the customer feedback board, public roadmap and public changelog (the Canny/Productboard job). Vector receives Penny requests as customer-request links on issues and hands shipped work back to Penny (CUST-001, CUST-003, RELEASE-003).
- **HappyToHelp** owns the support inbox, **Orbit** the CRM, **Spool** error tracking, **Uptime** external checks and **Oncall** paging. Vector links their records to issues and reads their evidence (CUST-001, ERROR-002, PUSH-004) but never replaces them.
- Cross-functional work management for non-software teams (the Asana, monday.com and ClickUp job) is not Vector's product; Vector is for software delivery.
- The code host, CI runners and deploy platform stay GitHub, GitLab, Bitbucket and the buyer's CI; Vector owns work state, relationships and delivery evidence.

**Scope before this contract** was one business workspace of equal operators built around evidence-led intake decisions, issues, work streams, projects, dated cycles, saved views, search, in-app notifications and Linear/Jira file imports, with integrations, AI, roadmaps, roles, chat, mobile and real-time collaboration declared out of scope. Those clauses remain where they still hold, rewritten where the scope changed, and now form one part of the full job.

## Product target

Vector is the issue tracker and software project-management workspace a US software team buys instead of Linear, Jira or GitHub Issues and Projects, wired into the code host, chat and coding agents. This contract describes that product, which is what Vector is being built towards, and marks how close the current code is (see [Status](#status)).

| | |
| --- | --- |
| Category | Issue tracking and software project management |
| Incumbents a buyer switches from | Linear, Jira (Atlassian), GitHub Issues and Projects; up-and-comers Plane and Huly |
| First buyer | A US software team of 5 to 500 people — engineering, product and design, with support and leadership as readers — replacing Linear, a Jira Software project or GitHub Projects; self-hosted on the buyer's own Cloudflare account, free and open source, with money appearing only as customer revenue in USD |

## Public homepage

- **HOME-001 — Public homepage.** The owner-approved public marketing homepage at `/` remains exactly as approved, including its copy and assets and the line “100% free, open source alternative to Linear, Jira and Asana.” That line is product positioning; it does not promise feature parity or importer coverage for every named product. The page presents Vector with its headline, one supporting sentence, and source and docs links, followed by short sections (how Current explains each issue, free and open source, deferral, the rest of the product, trying it beside another tracker, a closing call to action), each drawing the product only from its real labels. The public site's “Run Vector” action remains unchanged and points to `/run`, the installation page. The installed application's root `/` serves the Base Lobby, whose Sign in entry satisfies this clause's sign-in requirement (owner decision, 13 Oct 2026 15:11Z). The page contains no pricing, hosting costs, customer testimonials or invented metrics, reads no installation data and is server-rendered. *Acceptance:* `tests/e2e/core-runtime.mjs` (tests tagged HOME-001). *Status:* done — `tests/e2e/core-runtime.mjs`.

## Workspace, people and permissions

- **OPS-001 — Workspace access.** Every workspace member sees every public team's issues, intake items, cycles, projects, initiatives, labels and shared saved views, and every notification addressed to them, and acts on them as the role matrix (ROLE-001, ROLE-008) allows; team membership organizes work and, for a public team, never restricts access. Roles (ROLE-001), private teams (ROLE-002) and guests (ROLE-003) are the only rules that narrow access, and a private saved view (VIEW-002) is visible only to its owner. Every read, search result, notification, export, webhook, Slack unfurl, mobile payload, API response and agent context applies the same rule server-side. Future mobile payloads inherit this rule through MOBILE-001 and MOBILE-002, and future agent contexts through AGENT-001 and AGENT-005; ROLE-008 owns registered-door coverage. *Acceptance:* the existing equal-access tests plus a role-matrix journey that signs in as Admin, Member and Guest and checks every private route, `/api/v1` route, search and export against ROLE-001–003. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts` current private and invited teams scope native, bearer, search, project, notification and document reads; `tests/worker/search-access.test.ts` scoped search; `tests/worker/removal-boundary.test.ts` next-request revocation; `tests/e2e/admin-access-journey.mjs` rolePrivateCells and privateTeamJourney (Admin, Member and Guest visibility); `tests/e2e/role-access-census.mjs` viewerAccessCensus (addressed Inbox, counted CSV and exact attachment bytes across public, private, membership addition and withdrawal at 1440 and 390); `tests/e2e/integrations/outbound-privacy-journey.mjs` outboundPrivacyJourney, invoked by `tests/e2e/integrations/outbound-native.mjs` (public TLS delivery, private production and queued-event suppression, retry refusal and newly public delivery); `tests/worker/outbound-broken-destination.test.ts` Slack link previews; `tests/worker/project-report.test.ts` private document and specification concealment.
- **SETUP-001 — First workspace review.** Fresh configured owners review their workspace at `/setup` before entering an app route. Signed-out visitors return there after ordinary sign-in. Only the recorded installation owner may complete setup through a same-origin, current-session transaction. Completion survives repeated setup, restart and local restore. Existing configured installations retain completion through the additive upgrade. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/first-run.test.ts` (tests tagged SETUP-001). *Status:* done — `tests/worker/first-run.test.ts`.
- **OPS-002 — Member management.** Members are invited, removed by suspension (OPS-004, ROLE-011), reactivated and erased (ROLE-012) in Settings → Members, and every invitation names the invitee's role (ROLE-001) and, for a Guest, the teams they join (ROLE-003). Owners and Admins invite and remove Members and Guests; only Owners invite, promote to or remove Owners and Admins. The installation owner can be removed only by themselves, and never while they are the last Owner (ROLE-001). Members and Guests cannot remove anyone but themselves. *Policy: `operator.management.v1`; default: Owners and Admins invite and remove; a workspace setting lets Members invite Members (never Admins or Guests).* *Acceptance:* the existing invite and removal tests plus the OPS-002 rows of the role-matrix journey (ROLE-001): a Member's invitation is refused 403 by default and admitted after the setting is enabled, a Member's attempt to invite an Admin is refused 403, and an Admin's removal of a Member succeeds. *Status:* partial — `tests/worker/base-auth.test.ts`, `tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts` (invitations name their role, Members invite Members only while the workspace lets them, only Owners invite, promote or remove Owners and Admins, Admins remove Members and Members remove only themselves; removal is not yet a suspension (ROLE-011), reactivation and erasure are ROLE-011's and ROLE-012's, and native Guest invitations name their allowed teams (ROLE-003)).
- **OPS-003 — Workspace time.** Settings hold one IANA workspace timezone. Every calendar day, due reminder, cycle start and end instant, and seven-day window named in this contract is evaluated in that timezone. Changing it never rewrites stored instants, closed cycle snapshots, or reminders already delivered. *Acceptance:* `tests/e2e/admin-workspace-time-journey.mjs`, `tests/e2e/admin-access-journey.mjs`, `tests/e2e/admin-runtime.mjs` (tests tagged OPS-003). *Status:* done — `tests/e2e/admin-workspace-time-journey.mjs`.
- **OPS-004 — Safe removal.** Removing a member from the workspace — by an Owner or Admin in Settings → Members (role matrix), by the member leaving themselves, or by SCIM deprovisioning (ROLE-004) — suspends them under ROLE-011 and never erases them; erasing their personal data is the separate, explicit action of ROLE-012. Vector rejects the suspension of the last Owner. A suspension revokes the member's sessions and credentials (ROLE-011), transfers each project they lead and each shared saved view they own to the person performing it — or to the longest-standing remaining Owner when they left themselves or SCIM removed them — handles their non-terminal issues as the person performing it chooses (ROLE-011), keeps their name on every comment, activity and audit entry, and writes one activity entry per changed issue and project. A failed step rolls back the whole removal. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/removal-boundary.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/collaboration-runtime.mjs`, `tests/worker/base-auth.test.ts`, `tests/worker/foundation-rollback.test.ts` (tests tagged OPS-004) for session revocation, transfers, unassignment, the last-operator guard and rollback; plus the ROLE-011 suspension journey, in which a removed member's comments and activity still carry their name, and the ROLE-012 journey, in which only the explicit erase anonymizes. *Status:* partial — `tests/worker/removal-boundary.test.ts`, `tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts` (removal revokes sessions, unassigns, transfers and rolls back as one unit, refuses the last Owner and, when a member leaves, transfers to the longest-standing remaining Owner, but today every removal also deletes the member's private views and anonymizes their references at once; suspension that keeps the name is open with ROLE-011 and the separate erase with ROLE-012).

**Role matrix.** ROLE-001, ROLE-008 and every clause naming a permission follow this table, and the role-matrix journey checks every cell. "Own" means the actor created the subject; "team owner" means a team owner of the subject's team (ROLE-007); "member's teams" means teams the actor belongs to or, for a public team, can see; "team setting" means the team's owners, or every member of that team while its *Who manages team settings* permission is All team members (ROLE-009; default Team owners only); "planning setting" means the team's owners, or every member of that team while its *Who manages cycles and templates* permission is All team members (ROLE-009; default All team members); "the project's lead" means the member named as the subject project's lead (PROJ-001). Owners and Admins pass every team-setting and planning-setting row whatever the team's permissions; Guests fail every "team setting" and "planning setting" row whatever they are. The journey checks each "team setting" and "planning setting" cell under both values of the permission. An action with no row here is refused to every role but Owner (ROLE-008). The Viewer column is the missing ROLE-013 target, distinct from Guest: its read access follows public-team visibility and explicit private-team membership, and every work mutation is refused. TEAM-007, TEAM-008, PROJ-017 and the TEAM-006 hierarchy row are also targets, not claims of shipped doors. Until PROJ-017 makes project deletion recoverable, only Owners and Admins may delete a project.

## Teams and workflows

- **TEAM-001 — Team identity.** A team has a name and an identifier prefix, each unique across archived and non-archived teams. The prefix is two to ten uppercase letters and cannot be changed once the team has an issue, so existing identifiers stay resolvable. Every issue belongs to exactly one team. *Acceptance:* `tests/e2e/admin-team-journey.mjs` (tests tagged TEAM-001). *Status:* done — `tests/e2e/admin-team-journey.mjs`.
- **TEAM-002 — Team workflow.** Every team has an ordered list of workflow statuses with at least one status in each of the Intake, Backlog, Unstarted, Started, Completed, Canceled, and Duplicate categories, and status names unique within the team. Owners, Admins and the team's owners (ROLE-007) can add, rename, and reorder statuses, and so can every member of the team while its *Who manages team settings* permission is All team members (ROLE-009); other Members and all Guests are refused. A status never changes category, and no category is added or removed. A team's *default status* in a category is the first status of that category in workflow order, and every clause naming a category as a destination moves the issue there. *Acceptance:* `tests/e2e/admin-team-journey.mjs`, `tests/worker/base-auth.test.ts` (tests tagged TEAM-002) for the workflow rules, plus the TEAM-002 rows of the role-matrix journey (ROLE-001): a team owner renames a status, and a Member who does not own the team gets 403 under the default permission and succeeds once the permission is All team members. *Status:* done — `tests/e2e/admin-team-journey.mjs`, `tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts`.
- **TEAM-003 — Team archiving.** Archiving a team stops new issues, intake, and issue moves from targeting it while preserving its issues, cycles, projects, identifiers, and history; a signal source request naming an archived team creates nothing and returns 409. Unarchiving restores normal use. Teams are never deleted; archiving is the only terminal state. *Acceptance:* `tests/e2e/admin-archive-team-journey.mjs` (tests tagged TEAM-003). *Status:* done — `tests/e2e/admin-archive-team-journey.mjs`.
- **TEAM-004 — Status removal.** Removing a workflow status requires a destination status in the same category and atomically moves every issue to it. Vector rejects removal of a category's last status and records the status mapping in audit history. *Acceptance:* `tests/e2e/admin-team-journey.mjs` (tests tagged TEAM-004). *Status:* done — `tests/e2e/admin-team-journey.mjs`.

## Issues

- **ISSUE-001 — Creation.** An operator can create an issue with a required title and team plus an optional Markdown description (written in the ISSUE-026 editor, with inline images and video under ISSUE-024 and checklists under ISSUE-025), assignee, project, cycle, priority, labels, estimate, due date, expected outcome, customer impact, and values for the custom fields defined for its team (FIELD-001, which carries their types and acceptance). It starts in the team's default Backlog status unless another non-Intake status is explicitly chosen. *Policy: `issue.defaults.v1`; default: unassigned, no priority, no cycle, and no project.* *Acceptance:* `tests/e2e/core-runtime.mjs`, `tests/markdown-rendering.test.tsx` (tests tagged ISSUE-001). *Status:* done — `tests/e2e/core-runtime.mjs`.
- **ISSUE-002 — Stable identifiers.** An issue identifier is its team's prefix, a hyphen, and an integer one higher than the highest ever allocated under that prefix, so a number is never reused after an issue is deleted. The identifier never changes, including when the issue moves teams (ISSUE-012); a prefix therefore records where an issue started, not where it lives. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/issue-deletion-relations.test.ts` (tests tagged ISSUE-002). *Status:* done — `tests/worker/issue-deletion-relations.test.ts`.
- **ISSUE-003 — Priority.** An issue has exactly one priority: Urgent, High, Medium, Low, or No priority. No priority is the default and sorts after Low. *Acceptance:* A test that sets each of the five priorities and checks that No priority is the default and sorts after Low. *Status:* done — `tests/e2e/core-board-journey.mjs`, `evidence/keyboard-board/ISSUE-003/`.
- **ISSUE-004 — Status transitions.** A version-checked operator mutation (ISSUE-006) can move an issue in any direction among the Backlog, Unstarted, Started, Completed, and Canceled statuses of its team's workflow. Vector rejects moving any issue into an Intake status. Leaving Intake uses INTAKE-005, INTAKE-007, or REL-003; entering or leaving Duplicate uses REL-003 or REL-005. Entering Completed records the completion time and entering Canceled records the cancellation time; leaving one of those categories clears the time it recorded and removes no activity. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/issue-session-projection.test.ts` (tests tagged ISSUE-004). *Status:* done — `tests/worker/issue-session-projection.test.ts`.
- **ISSUE-005 — Assignment.** An issue has at most one assignee. Assigning, reassigning, or unassigning it takes effect immediately and records the actor, the previous assignee, and the new assignee. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/removal-assignment.test.ts` (tests tagged ISSUE-005). *Status:* done — `tests/worker/removal-assignment.test.ts`.
- **ISSUE-006 — Concurrent edits.** Every issue carries an integer version that increases by one on each mutation that appends an activity entry under ISSUE-007. An issue mutation carries the version the client last read; if the stored version is higher, Vector rejects the mutation with 409 and returns the current issue and version, changing nothing. Adding a comment, evidence revision, follower, or notification does not change the version and is never rejected as stale. *Acceptance:* `tests/e2e/core-runtime.mjs`, `tests/e2e/collaboration-preferences.mjs`, `tests/worker/dependencies.test.ts` (tests tagged ISSUE-006). *Status:* done — `tests/e2e/core-runtime.mjs`.
- **ISSUE-007 — Activity history.** Creation and every change to title, description, team, status, priority, assignee, project, milestone (PROJ-011), cycle, estimate, due date, labels, expected outcome, customer impact, custom fields, duplicate target, parent, or dependency append an immutable activity entry recording the time, the previous value, the new value, and the actor — the operator, or the named automation when a cycle closure, deferral return, or import acted. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/dependencies.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/admin-label-journey.mjs`, `tests/worker/cycle-closure.test.ts` (tests tagged ISSUE-007). `tests/worker/project-fields.test.ts` also proves one combined project/milestone activity entry when an issue leaves its project. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/dependencies.test.ts`.
- **ISSUE-008 — Comments, threads and resolution.** Any member who can see an issue (a Guest only in invited teams) can add a Markdown comment in the ISSUE-026 editor, with inline images and video (ISSUE-024) and checklists (ISSUE-025). Each top-level comment starts a thread: members reply to it, and replies show under it in order. Any member who can comment can resolve a thread when its question is answered, which collapses it to one line naming who resolved it and when, and can reopen it; a new reply reopens a resolved thread. A comment can also quote highlighted text of the issue description or a document (PROJ-013) as an inline comment, which anchors the thread to that text and shows it in the margin; if the text is later removed, the thread stays in the issue with the quoted text shown as removed. Only a comment's author edits it; an edited comment shows its edit time. Authors delete their own comments; Owners, Admins and the team's owners delete anyone's (role matrix, ROLE-001); the entry records who acted. Deleting a comment removes its body, attachments, links, and search entry, leaves a tombstone and an activity entry in the thread, keeps its replies, and does not remove the follower state it created. A synced Slack or Teams thread maps to exactly one Vector comment thread (SLACK-002). *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/discussion-access.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/collaboration-runtime.mjs`, `tests/e2e/core-runtime.mjs` (tests tagged ISSUE-008) for comment creation, editing, deletion and tombstones; plus a thread journey that replies twice to a comment, resolves the thread and sees it collapse to one line with the resolver, posts a new reply and sees it reopen, adds an inline comment on highlighted description text and finds it anchored in the margin, and is refused 403 editing another member's comment; plus the SLACK-002 fixture in which one Slack thread's replies land only in its mapped comment thread. *Status:* partial — `tests/worker/discussion-threads.test.ts`, `tests/worker/discussion-access.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/core-threads-journey.mjs`; `evidence/comment-threads/ISSUE-008/` (threads with ordered replies, resolve and reopen naming who and when, reopen on a new reply, author-only editing refused 403, and deletion by the author or an Owner or Admin with tombstones that keep replies are implemented; inline comments quoting description or document text are not built; team-owner deletion waits on ROLE-007, Guest limits on ROLE-003, inline images and video on ISSUE-024, and the Slack thread mapping on SLACK-002).
- **ISSUE-009 — Attachments and links.** Operators can attach files and named HTTPS links to an issue or a comment. Removing either records the action; removing a file also deletes its blob under `BASE-DATA-003`. An image or video pasted, dropped or uploaded into a description, comment or document is an attachment under this clause and is shown inline where it was placed (ISSUE-024). *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/discussion-file-admission.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/core-runtime.mjs`, `tests/worker/link-cascade.test.ts` (tests tagged ISSUE-009). *Status:* done — `tests/worker/discussion-file-admission.test.ts`.
- **ISSUE-010 — Deletion.** Deleting an issue first moves it to Recently deleted (ISSUE-020), from which it can be undone or restored intact for 30 days. Permanent deletion happens when that retention ends, or at once when an Owner or Admin chooses "Delete permanently" (for erasure under SEC-003 and DATA-011): it removes the issue's comments, attachments, links, evidence revisions, dependencies, parent link, follower state, notifications, activity, and search entry; its children become root issues, and a duplicate is removed from its canonical issue's list. Vector rejects deleting a canonical issue that still has duplicates. Closed cycle snapshots (CYCLE-005) and audit entries keep only its identifier, and its source idempotency records (INTAKE-002) are retained so a re-delivery cannot resurrect it. *Policy: `issue.can-delete.v1`; default: Owners, Admins and the issue's creator; a workspace setting extends it to every Member of the issue's team; Guests never delete; only Owners and Admins delete permanently (role matrix, ROLE-001).* *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/issue-deletion-cascade.test.ts`, `tests/worker/collaboration-atomicity.test.ts`, `tests/worker/issue-deletion-import.test.ts` (tests tagged ISSUE-010) for the permanent cascade; plus the ISSUE-020 journey and the ISSUE-010 rows of the role-matrix journey: a Member who did not create the issue is refused 403 by default, its creator deletes it to Recently deleted, and a Member's "Delete permanently" is refused 403 while an Admin's succeeds and runs the cascade at once. *Status:* partial — `tests/worker/issue-deletion-cascade.test.ts`, `tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts` (the immediate permanent cascade, which only Owners and Admins run as the role matrix's permanent deletion. Gap, carried as release debt: until ISSUE-020's Recently deleted lands, Members cannot delete issues, their own included, because the only deletion today is irreversible and there is no creator exception; deletion by an issue's creator or, with the setting, its team's Members, Recently deleted and undo are open with ISSUE-020).
- **ISSUE-011 — Estimates.** An issue estimate is absent or an integer from 1 through 100 points. Changing an estimate never rewrites a closed cycle snapshot. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/cycle-closure.test.ts` (tests tagged ISSUE-011). *Status:* done — `tests/worker/cycle-closure.test.ts`.
- **ISSUE-012 — Moving teams.** Moving an issue requires a non-archived target team and an explicit target status in the same category as its current status, including Intake so a misrouted intake item reaches the right team without a decision. The move preserves the identifier (ISSUE-002), uncommits the issue from any cycle (CYCLE-002), keeps its project, assignee, labels, custom-field values and history, and writes one activity entry. Labels and custom-field values are preserved unchanged; the native move does not map them. All of it happens or none of it does. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/bulk-commands.test.ts` and `tests/e2e/core-stream-planning-journey.mjs` exercise team moves, including Intake and Started issues, stable identifiers, cycle uncommit and one activity entry. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/bulk-commands.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/core-stream-planning-journey.mjs`, `evidence/keyboard-planning/ISSUE-012/`.
- **ISSUE-013 — Mentions.** Typing `@` in an issue description or comment offers matching operators, and choosing one records a mention of that operator. Text resolving to no operator is stored and shown literally and mentions nobody. A mention adds a follower (NOTIFY-002) and notifies once (NOTIFY-001); editing text to add a mention notifies only the newly mentioned operator, and removing a mention notifies nobody and withdraws no delivered notification. *Acceptance:* `tests/e2e/collaboration-events.mjs`, `tests/markdown-rendering.test.tsx` (tests tagged ISSUE-013). *Status:* done — `tests/e2e/collaboration-events.mjs`.
- **ISSUE-014 — Labels.** A label belongs to the workspace or to one team, has a name unique among the labels visible in that scope and a color, and can sit in a label group (for example Area, Type) whose labels are mutually exclusive on an issue. Owners, Admins and Members create labels and apply them — Members through the inline Create label in the issue label picker, subject to the workspace label-creation setting, while Settings → Labels is written only by Owners and Admins and Members only read labels — and Guests apply existing labels in their invited teams; Owners and Admins rename, recolor, merge and delete workspace labels, and a team's owners — or all its members while its *Who manages team settings* permission is All team members (ROLE-009) — do so for that team's labels (role matrix, ROLE-001). Merging one label into another relabels every issue and saved-view filter in one change. Deleting a label removes it from every issue and from the filters of saved views naming it, and deletes no issue. An issue carries up to the number of labels in `seed.json → limits`. *Policy: `label.manage.v1`; default: as above; a workspace setting restricts label creation to Owners and Admins.* *Acceptance:* `tests/e2e/admin-issue-journey.mjs`, `tests/e2e/admin-label-journey.mjs`, `tests/e2e/collaboration-issue.mjs` (tests tagged ISSUE-014) for workspace labels; plus a Member journey that opens Settings → Labels read-only and creates through the inline issue picker under both values of the workspace label-creation setting, without gaining workspace label-management controls; plus a journey that creates a team label and a label group, is refused applying two labels of one group, merges two labels and finds every issue relabeled, and the ISSUE-014 rows of the role-matrix journey (a Member's delete of a workspace label refused 403, an Admin's admitted). *Status:* partial — `tests/e2e/admin-label-journey.mjs`, `tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/admin-access-journey.mjs` (Owners, Admins and Members create and apply workspace labels and only Owners and Admins rename, recolor or delete them (role matrix); Settings → Labels is written by Owners and Admins and is read-only for Members. Members create inline in the issue label picker or through session and scoped `/api/v1/labels` doors when the workspace permits it; Owners/Admins set the audited creation permission. Merging relabels issues, projects and saved-view filters atomically with one activity per changed issue. Gap: team labels and groups are absent).
- **ISSUE-015 — Unsent drafts.** An unsubmitted new-issue form is retained in the operator's browser and restored the next time they open new issue there. A draft is not a record: it appears in no list, search result, notification, or export, and becomes an issue only when that operator creates it. *Acceptance:* `tests/e2e/core-draft-journey.mjs` (tests tagged ISSUE-015). *Status:* done — `tests/e2e/core-draft-journey.mjs`.

## Relations

- **REL-001 — Dependencies.** An operator can record that one issue blocks another, across teams, and can remove the link. Vector rejects self-links, a link that already exists in either direction, and any link that would create a directed cycle. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/dependencies.test.ts` (tests tagged REL-001). *Status:* done — `tests/worker/dependencies.test.ts`.
- **REL-002 — Blocked state.** An issue is shown as blocked while any issue that blocks it is outside a Completed, Canceled, or Duplicate category. Blocking never changes the issue's workflow status by itself. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/dependencies.test.ts` (tests tagged REL-002). *Status:* done — `tests/worker/dependencies.test.ts`.
- **REL-003 — Duplicate decision.** Marking an issue as a duplicate requires one canonical issue that is neither itself nor already a duplicate, moves it to the team's default Duplicate status, and preserves its description, comments, evidence, attachments, and activity under the duplicate issue. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/issue-deletion-relations.test.ts` (tests tagged REL-003). *Status:* done — `tests/worker/issue-deletion-relations.test.ts`.
- **REL-004 — Canonical duplicates.** A canonical issue lists every direct duplicate. If it is later marked duplicate, Vector atomically repoints its duplicates to the new canonical issue, so a duplicate chain or cycle is never exposed. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/issue-deletion-relations.test.ts` (tests tagged REL-004). *Status:* done — `tests/worker/issue-deletion-relations.test.ts`.
- **REL-005 — Undo duplicate.** Undoing a duplicate decision removes the canonical link and restores the issue's most recent non-terminal status, or its team's default Backlog status if it has none, while preserving both decision activity entries. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/issue-deletion-relations.test.ts` (tests tagged REL-005). *Status:* done — `tests/worker/issue-deletion-relations.test.ts`.
- **REL-006 — Parent and child.** An issue has at most one parent. An operator can set, replace, or clear it from issue details. Vector rejects self-parenting and ancestry cycles; changing a parent never changes status, team, project, cycle, or assignee, and deleting a parent makes each direct child a root issue. Issue lists can filter to roots or to children of one exact parent (VIEW-001). *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/issue-deletion-preimage.test.ts`, `tests/worker/parent-deletion-custody.test.ts` (tests tagged REL-006). *Status:* done — `tests/worker/issue-deletion-preimage.test.ts`.

## Intake and triage

- **INTAKE-001 — Intake creation.** A registered signal source (INTAKE-011) creates an intake issue with a title, team, observed time, its source ID, source display name, source record identifier, source revision identifier, summary, link, and optional structured measurements and customer reports. An operator creates one with a title and team and any of the same descriptive fields, attributed to that operator instead of a source. Either way it starts in the team's default Intake status and appears in Needs decision (CURR-002) within 5 seconds. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/source-ingest.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/core-runtime.mjs` (tests tagged INTAKE-001). *Status:* done — `tests/worker/source-ingest.test.ts`.
- **INTAKE-002 — Idempotent sources.** Re-delivery of the same source ID, source record identifier, source revision identifier, and normalized payload returns the original result and creates nothing. Re-delivery of an identity whose issue has since been deleted (ISSUE-010) returns 410 and creates nothing. Reusing an identity with a different payload returns 409 and changes nothing. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/source-ingest.test.ts`, `tests/worker/source-erasure-identity.test.ts` (tests tagged INTAKE-002). *Status:* done — `tests/worker/source-ingest.test.ts`.
- **INTAKE-003 — Evidence revisions.** A new source revision identifier for an existing source record appends immutable dated evidence even when revisions arrive out of order. The issue orders evidence by observed time, then receipt time, and shows a field-level change from the preceding observed revision. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/source-ingest.test.ts` (tests tagged INTAKE-003). *Status:* done — `tests/worker/source-ingest.test.ts`.
- **INTAKE-004 — Inspectable assessment.** An intake issue shows the evidence behind its attention reason, impact measurements, likely cause, recommended assignee, suggested priority, suggested labels and likely duplicates. Each assertion names whether it came from a source, an operator, a policy or an agent; an agent assertion also names the model, the records it read and when, and is shown as a suggestion until a person accepts it. TRIAGE-003 inherits the agent provenance sentence and the agent-suggestion triage journey before agent suggestions ship. Vector never presents an inferred assertion as a fact or applies one silently. *Acceptance:* the existing source, operator and policy provenance journey; TRIAGE-003 owns the inherited agent-suggestion journey. *Status:* done — `tests/e2e/core-runtime.mjs` (source, operator and policy provenance; TRIAGE-003 inherits agent provenance and its triage journey).
- **INTAKE-005 — Accept.** Owners, Admins and Members who can see an intake item's team accept, defer and decline it; Guests cannot (role matrix). Accepting an intake issue atomically records the actor, the time, and an optional reason; applies the chosen assignee, priority, cycle, and Started or Unstarted status; and clears any deferral. It leaves Needs decision and reaches its new Current and My work sections within the CURR-008 window. Its role-matrix cells are enforced on every door of this clause and proven by the role-matrix journey (`tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts`), Guest cells with a Guest assigned through ROLE-003's native door. *Acceptance:* `tests/e2e/core-runtime.mjs` (tests tagged INTAKE-005). Role-matrix cells: `tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts`. *Status:* done — `tests/e2e/core-runtime.mjs`.
- **INTAKE-006 — Defer.** Deferring an intake issue (by an Owner, Admin or Member who can see its team; Guests cannot, role matrix) records who deferred it, an absolute return time strictly in the future, and an optional reason, and removes it from Needs decision. It returns there exactly once — within 5 minutes of the return time, or within 5 seconds of a new evidence revision (INTAKE-003), whichever comes first — and the return atomically clears the deferral and leaves the earlier decision visible. The same roles can defer it again or end a deferral early. Its role-matrix cells are enforced on every door of this clause and proven by the role-matrix journey (`tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts`), Guest cells with a Guest assigned through ROLE-003's native door. *Acceptance:* `tests/e2e/core-runtime.mjs`, `tests/worker/issue-session-projection.test.ts` (tests tagged INTAKE-006). Role-matrix cells: `tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts`. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/source-ingest.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/core-runtime.mjs`, `evidence/issue-follow-up/INTAKE-006/`.
- **INTAKE-007 — Decline.** Declining an intake issue — open to Owners, Admins and Members who can see its team, never to Guests (role matrix) — requires a reason, moves it to the team's default Canceled status, and removes it from Needs decision without deleting its evidence or decision history. Its role-matrix cells are enforced on every door of this clause and proven by the role-matrix journey (`tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts`), Guest cells with a Guest assigned through ROLE-003's native door. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/source-ingest.test.ts` (tests tagged INTAKE-007) declines without a reason (refused with no writes) and with one, and reads Canceled with evidence and decision history retained after restart. Role-matrix cells: `tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts`. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/source-ingest.test.ts`.
- **INTAKE-008 — Evidence after a terminal decision.** Evidence received after an issue is Canceled or Duplicate is appended and visible but never reopens, reassigns, reprioritizes, or resurfaces the issue. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/source-ingest.test.ts` (exercises this behavior; the test does not yet tag INTAKE-008). *Status:* done — `tests/worker/source-ingest.test.ts`.
- **INTAKE-009 — Source failure.** A signal source request from an unknown or revoked source, or carrying a wrong signature, returns 401; one whose body fails validation returns 400; one naming an archived team returns 409 (TEAM-003). None of them creates anything. A valid request that cannot be stored durably returns 503 so the source retries. No request is acknowledged before its idempotency record and evidence are durable. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/source-ingest.test.ts` (exercises this behavior; the test does not yet tag INTAKE-009). *Status:* done — `tests/worker/source-ingest.test.ts`.
- **INTAKE-010 — Decision policy.** Suggested assignee, priority, and start state are selected by the intake recommendation policy and are always editable before acceptance. *Policy: `intake.recommendation.v1`; default: every registered source pins its team; the signed source payload may explicitly recommend an assignee through a source-attributed `assertions.assigneeOperatorId` value. An absent assignee recommendation leaves the issue unassigned in that team's triage, with No priority and Unstarted as the acceptance defaults. The recommendation never assigns the intake item before acceptance.* *Acceptance:* A test that delivers two signed items to a registered source's pinned team, one with an explicit assignee recommendation and one without an assignee recommendation, checks both stay unassigned in that team's triage, and checks that assignee, priority and start state recommendations stay editable before acceptance. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/intake-policy.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/core-intake-policy-journey.mjs`.
- **INTAKE-011 — Source registration.** An Owner or Admin registers a signal source (integration management in the role matrix; Members and Guests are refused 403) with a display name, a source ID unique in the workspace, and a signing secret; the `signal.source.v1` adapter normalizes that source's signed request into INTAKE-001. Rotating a secret (by an Owner or Admin) accepts both secrets for a configurable overlap of at most 24 hours and then rejects the previous one. An Owner or Admin revoking a source rejects every later request under INTAKE-009 and deletes no evidence. A secret is shown once at registration or rotation and never again. Its role-matrix cells are enforced on every door of this clause and proven by the role-matrix journey (`tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts`), Guest cells with a Guest assigned through ROLE-003's native door. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/source-ingest.test.ts` (tests tagged INTAKE-011). Role-matrix cells: `tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts`. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/source-ingest.test.ts`.

## Current

- **CURR-001 — Disjoint stream.** Current places an issue in at most one section using this precedence: Needs decision, Now, Next, then Recently done. Canceled, Duplicate, deleted, and actively deferred issues do not appear. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/current-disjoint.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/core-current-disjoint-journey.mjs` place each kind through public routes, assert exact and disjoint membership with overlapping eligibility, exclude canceled/duplicate/deferred/deleted issues, and move Next work to Now through the native status selector at 1440 and 390. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/current-disjoint.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/core-current-disjoint-journey.mjs`.
- **CURR-002 — Needs decision.** Needs decision contains every Intake-category issue whose deferral has expired or is absent and which has not been accepted, declined, or marked duplicate. Every expired deferral returns to Current: none is hidden, and more than one page is reachable through paging or an explicit total and Show more control (for example, “50 shown of 132, show more”), in CURR-007 order. *Acceptance:* A journey that defers, returns and declines intake items and asserts Needs decision membership; it defers 120 issues until all are past due, opens Current and reaches all 120 across pages in CURR-007 order, with the total and remaining issues visible until every issue has been reached. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/current-intake-pagination.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/core-current-intake-pagination-journey.mjs` (every expired deferral is included before bounded return automation drains; Needs decision shows totals and Show more through all 120 items at 1440 and 390, in CURR-007 order).
- **CURR-003 — Now.** Now contains non-terminal issues outside the Intake category that are overdue or in a Started status, excluding anything already in Needs decision. An issue is *overdue* when its due date is earlier than the current calendar day in the workspace timezone (OPS-003). *Acceptance:* A journey with a Started issue and an overdue Unstarted issue at a workspace-timezone day boundary. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/current-now.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/core-current-now-journey.mjs`.
- **CURR-004 — Next.** Next contains non-terminal issues in an Unstarted status that are committed to their team's active or next cycle (CYCLE-001) or due within the next seven calendar days, excluding anything already in a higher-precedence section. *Acceptance:* A journey with active-cycle and due-in-seven-days Unstarted issues. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/current-next.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/core-current-next-journey.mjs`.
- **CURR-005 — Recently done.** Recently done contains issues moved to a Completed category in the previous seven calendar days, ordered by the completion-time exception in CURR-007. *Acceptance:* A journey that completes issues across an eight-day window and checks the seven-day cut-off and completion-time order, including identifier ties. In the same Current view, two in-progress issues of different priority follow the normal CURR-007 order while two done issues completed at different times appear newest completion first even when their priority, due date or last update would put them in the opposite order. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/current-recently-done.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/core-current-recently-done-journey.mjs`.
- **CURR-006 — Explainable attention.** Every issue in Current shows one primary inclusion reason — Awaiting decision, Started work, Overdue, Cycle commitment, Due within seven days, or Completed within seven days — and every applicable context reason from Customer escalation, Unresolved dependency, Unassigned urgent work, Project risk, and New evidence since the most recent intake decision. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/dependencies.test.ts` (tests tagged CURR-006). *Status:* done — `tests/worker/dependencies.test.ts`.
- **CURR-007 — Ranking.** Within every Current section except Recently done, the default order sorts by priority (Urgent first, No priority last), then by due date (overdue first, then earliest first, then issues with no due date), then by most recent update, and finally by stable identifier, so the order is total and stable. Recently done (CURR-005) instead sorts by completion time, newest first, with stable identifier as the tiebreak; priority, due date and last update do not affect that section. *Policy: `current.ranking.v1`.* *Acceptance:* A ranking test over priority, due date, update time and identifier ties, plus completion time and identifier ties in Recently done. One Current view shows two in-progress issues of different priority in normal ranking order alongside two done issues completed at different times in newest-completion-first order, with priority, due date and last update opposing the done issues' completion order. *Status:* done — `tests/e2e/core-stream-controls-ranking.mjs`, `evidence/current-controls/CURR-007/`.
- **CURR-008 — Freshness.** A decision or issue mutation that changes an issue's Current membership, section, or reasons is reflected in an already open Current view within 5 seconds without an operator-initiated reload, and in the next response to any other request. *Acceptance:* `tests/e2e/core-current-journey.mjs` (tests tagged CURR-008). *Status:* done — `tests/e2e/core-current-journey.mjs`.
- **CURR-009 — Stream controls.** Operators can filter Current by team, project, priority, item type, assignee, and attention reason. The default includes all teams and all operators and shows Recently done. *Acceptance:* A browser journey applying each Current filter and its reset. *Status:* done — `tests/e2e/core-stream-controls-journey.mjs`, `evidence/current-controls/CURR-009/`.

## My work

- **WORK-001 — Personal queue.** My work contains every non-terminal issue assigned to the signed-in operator outside the Intake category, grouped disjointly in this precedence: Waiting for issues blocked under REL-002, Now for those the CURR-003 rule would place in Now, and Next for every issue still remaining, each group ordered by CURR-007. An issue assigned to the operator while in an Intake status appears in Current's Needs decision, not here. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/dependencies.test.ts` (tests tagged WORK-001). *Status:* done — `tests/worker/dependencies.test.ts`.
- **WORK-002 — Personal defaults.** Completed, Canceled, Duplicate, and actively deferred issues never appear in My work; an operator reads their finished work from Current's Recently done filtered to themselves (CURR-005, CURR-009). When no issue qualifies, the view states the operator is caught up and offers new issue and Current as next actions. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/dependencies.test.ts` (tests tagged WORK-002). *Status:* done — `tests/worker/dependencies.test.ts`.
- **WORK-003 — Cycle context.** My work shows one row for each team that has an active cycle containing an issue assigned to the operator, giving that cycle's name, start and end dates, and progress, with completed and total committed scope labeled in the unit CYCLE-003 used. Inherited from ROLE-007: My work's team rows follow team membership when this clause changes them. *Acceptance:* A journey with an active cycle containing the operator's issue that checks the cycle row's dates, progress and unit. *Status:* done — `tests/e2e/core-stream-controls-journey.mjs`, `evidence/current-controls/WORK-003/`.

## Projects, initiatives and roadmaps

- **PROJ-001 — Project record.** A project has a name unique in the workspace, a Markdown description, exactly one lead who is a workspace member, an optional start date, an optional target date that is on or after the start date when both are set, a priority (Urgent, High, Medium, Low or No priority, as ISSUE-003), project labels (workspace labels marked for projects, filterable by PROJ-007), a status (PROJ-016), an optional color (`#rrggbb`, canonical lowercase), an optional icon (one Unicode emoji, default none), and values for the custom fields that apply to projects (FIELD-001). Its status is one of the project statuses of its primary team or the workspace (PROJ-016), each in one of the categories Backlog, Planned, Started, Paused, Completed and Canceled, and a new project starts in the default Planned status unless another is chosen. Start date, target date, priority and labels are set in the project page, the projects list, the roadmap timeline (INIT-003) and `/api/v1`, and each change is a project activity entry. Linear project start and target dates, priority and labels import onto these fields (DATA-014). Owners, Admins and Members create projects and edit these fields; Guests see the projects their invited teams contribute to and cannot create or edit one (role matrix). *Acceptance:* `tests/e2e/admin-project-cycle-journey.mjs` (tests tagged PROJ-001) for name, description, lead, target date and native lifecycle; `tests/worker/project-fields.test.ts` and `tests/e2e/core-project-fields-journey.mjs` set start and target dates, High priority and two enabled workspace labels through the project page, projects list and scoped `/api/v1` routes, refuse a target before the start with 400, filter by label, sort by priority and check one activity entry per changed planning field at 1440 and 390; `tests/worker/project-fields-upgrade.test.ts` and `tests/worker/data-http.test.ts` retain those fields through populated upgrade and archive restore. Color and icon save, clear and read through page/list and scoped `/api/v1`, with one activity entry per changed field; the named PROJ-001 worker test and browser journey prove this at 1440/390. FIELD-001/002 carry project custom-field values and their surfaces; PROJ-014 carries primary teams; PROJ-016 carries configurable project statuses; INIT-003 carries roadmap planning-field edits; DATA-014 carries Linear project dates, priority and labels; ROLE-003 carries Guest visibility and create/edit refusals. Each obligation is accepted under its inheriting clause before that capability ships. Its role-matrix cells are enforced and proven by the role-matrix journey (`tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts`). *Status:* done — `tests/worker/project-fields.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/core-project-fields-journey.mjs`, `tests/worker/project-fields-upgrade.test.ts`, `tests/worker/data-http.test.ts`.
- **PROJ-002 — Manual lifecycle.** A project's status (PROJ-016) is changed only by a person — an Owner, Admin or Member; Guests are refused (role matrix). Completing or canceling all of its issues never completes or cancels the project. Its role-matrix cells are enforced on every door of this clause and proven by the role-matrix journey (`tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts`), Guest cells with a Guest assigned through ROLE-003's native door. *Acceptance:* `tests/e2e/admin-project-cycle-journey.mjs`, `tests/e2e/admin-access-journey.mjs` (tests tagged PROJ-002). Role-matrix cells: `tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts`. *Status:* done — `tests/e2e/admin-project-cycle-journey.mjs`.
- **PROJ-003 — Project membership.** An issue belongs to at most one project. Removing an issue from a project or archiving a project never deletes the issue; deleting a project detaches all of its issues but deletes none of them. *Acceptance:* A journey that removes, archives and deletes projects and checks no issue is deleted, including canceling and confirming deletion through the browser at 1440px and 390px. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/project-membership.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/core-project-membership-journey.mjs` (the project administration form has a Delete project control with cancellation and explicit confirmation; confirmed deletion detaches issues and preserves their identities and content. Gap PROJ-003: deletion is currently irreversible and must remain Owner/Admin only; the Member lead cell applies only after PROJ-017 supplies recoverable deletion, Undo and restore. This clause's membership-preservation acceptance does not require Member lead deletion).
- **PROJ-004 — Progress.** Project progress counts issues: completed project issues divided by all project issues outside the Canceled and Duplicate categories. The interface shows the completed and total counts beside the percentage and reports no progress when the denominator is zero. *Acceptance:* `tests/e2e/core-runtime.mjs` (tests tagged PROJ-004). *Status:* done — `tests/e2e/core-runtime.mjs`.
- **PROJ-005 — Health.** Health is reported only for projects whose status is in the Started category (PROJ-016); a project in any other category, Paused included, reports no health and is excluded from health filters and counts. A *remaining issue* is a project issue outside the Completed, Canceled, and Duplicate categories. A Started project is Blocked, At risk, On track, or Unknown, evaluated in that order. The default reports Blocked when at least one remaining issue exists and either an Urgent remaining issue has an unresolved blocker (REL-002) or every remaining issue is blocked; At risk when its target date is in the past, a remaining Urgent issue is unassigned, or its remaining estimated points committed to any team's active cycle exceed that cycle's capacity; On track when a target date in the future, every applicable capacity, and every remaining active-cycle estimate exist and no earlier rule matches; and Unknown otherwise. Remaining scope exactly equal to capacity is not At risk. *Policy: `project.health.v1`.* *Acceptance:* A health test covering each Blocked, At risk, On track and Unknown rule and the equal-capacity edge. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/project-health.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/core-project-health-journey.mjs`.
- **PROJ-006 — Risk explanation.** Every Blocked or At risk project shows the exact issues and measurements that triggered its health, including unresolved dependencies, unowned urgent work, target-date exposure, and committed scope against capacity. *Acceptance:* A journey that opens a Blocked and an At risk project and finds the triggering issues and measurements. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/project-risk.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/core-project-risk-journey.mjs`.
- **PROJ-007 — Portfolio.** The Projects view shows the count of projects in each health and lifecycle state and can filter by lifecycle, health, lead, team, target date, and label. Counts and rows use the same active filters. The team filter selects contributing teams; target-date bounds are inclusive and exclude projects without a target date when either bound is set. The label filter selects workspace labels marked for projects (PROJ-001). *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/project-fields.test.ts` and `tests/e2e/core-project-fields-journey.mjs` apply lifecycle, health, lead, contributing-team, inclusive target-date range and project-label filters individually and together, checking matching health/lifecycle counts and rows at 1440 and 390; the browser journey also sorts by priority and edits a project from the list. `tests/e2e/admin-access-journey.mjs` retains empty-portfolio coverage (tests tagged PROJ-007). *Status:* done — `tests/worker/project-fields.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/core-project-fields-journey.mjs` (lifecycle, health, lead, contributing team, target-date range and label filters, including combined filters, use the same rows for health/lifecycle counts; native 1440/390 journeys also exercise priority order and list editing).
- **PROJ-008 — Historical truth.** Later issue edits update live project progress and health but never rewrite a closed cycle snapshot shown in that project's history. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/cycle-closure.test.ts` (tests tagged PROJ-008). *Status:* done — `tests/worker/cycle-closure.test.ts`.
- **PROJ-009 — Project archiving.** Archiving a project removes it from the default portfolio and stops new issues from joining it while preserving its lifecycle, issues, history, and closed-cycle snapshots. Operators can include archived projects in filters or unarchive one. *Acceptance:* `tests/e2e/admin-project-cycle-journey.mjs` (tests tagged PROJ-009). *Status:* done — `tests/e2e/admin-project-cycle-journey.mjs`.

## Cycles

- **CYCLE-001 — Time box.** A cycle belongs to one team and has a name unique within that team, a start instant, an end instant after the start, and an optional capacity in estimate points, all interpreted in the workspace timezone (OPS-003). Vector rejects a cycle whose dates overlap another cycle of the same team, so a team's *active cycle* — the one whose start is at or before now and whose end is after now — is unique, and its *next cycle* is the cycle of that team with the earliest start at or after the active cycle's end. Owners, Admins and the team's owners create, edit, complete and delete its cycles, and so do all its members while its *Who manages cycles and templates* permission is All team members, the default (ROLE-009); Guests never do (role matrix). Creation enforces team ownership and both ROLE-009 planning values, proven by the role-matrix journey (`tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts`); cycles have no edit, completion or deletion door yet, and those cells are checked when such doors exist. *Acceptance:* `tests/e2e/admin-project-cycle-journey.mjs` (tests tagged CYCLE-001). Role-matrix cells: `tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts`. *Status:* done — `tests/e2e/admin-project-cycle-journey.mjs`.
- **CYCLE-002 — Commitment.** A non-terminal issue can be committed to at most one cycle, and only to a cycle of its own team. Anyone who can edit the issue commits, moves or uncommits it — Owners, Admins, Members who can see its team, and Guests in an invited team (role matrix). Committing, moving, and uncommitting record both cycle identifiers and update both cycles atomically. Its role-matrix cells are enforced on every door of this clause and proven by the role-matrix journey (`tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts`), Guest cells with a Guest assigned through ROLE-003's native door. *Acceptance:* `tests/e2e/admin-cycle-list-journey.mjs`, `tests/e2e/admin-issue-journey.mjs` (tests tagged CYCLE-002). Role-matrix cells: `tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts`. *Status:* done — `tests/e2e/admin-cycle-list-journey.mjs`.
- **CYCLE-003 — Progress and capacity.** Cycle progress is completed committed estimate points divided by all non-Canceled, non-Duplicate committed estimate points when every included issue is estimated; otherwise it uses issue counts and labels the result `by issue count`. With no included issues it shows `No committed work` and no percentage. Capacity risk is Unknown unless capacity and every remaining estimate exist, At risk when remaining points exceed capacity, and Within capacity otherwise. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/cycle-closure.test.ts` (tests tagged CYCLE-003). *Status:* done — `tests/worker/cycle-closure.test.ts`.
- **CYCLE-004 — Closure and rollover.** Within 5 minutes of a cycle's end instant Vector closes it exactly once however many times the closing job runs, and by default moves committed issues in an Unstarted or Started status to the team's next cycle (CYCLE-001) and uncommits committed issues in an Intake, Backlog, Completed, Canceled, or Duplicate status. A closed cycle's name, dates, capacity, and snapshot cannot be changed, and a closed cycle is never reopened. *Policy: `cycle.rollover.v1`.* *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/cycle-closure.test.ts` (tests tagged CYCLE-004). *Status:* done — `tests/worker/cycle-closure.test.ts`.
- **CYCLE-005 — Closure snapshot.** Closing a cycle records committed and completed issue IDs, each issue's project ID at close, estimate totals, capacity, progress, additions and removals during the cycle, and the close time. The snapshot is immutable. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/cycle-closure.test.ts` (tests tagged CYCLE-005). *Status:* done — `tests/worker/cycle-closure.test.ts`.
- **CYCLE-006 — Missing next cycle.** If rollover is required and no next cycle exists, the cycle still closes, its unfinished issues become uncommitted, and one visible failed-operation entry names the affected issues; creating a later cycle does not move them. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/cycle-closure.test.ts` (tests tagged CYCLE-006). *Status:* done — `tests/worker/cycle-closure.test.ts`.

## Search, views and commands

- **SEARCH-001 — Global search.** Search covers issue identifiers, titles, descriptions, expected outcomes, customer impact, comments, evidence summaries, project names, label names, and saved-view names visible to the operator, and returns within 500ms. Issues of archived teams and projects are excluded unless the operator asks for them. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/search-access.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/dev-runtime.mjs`, `tests/worker/search-exact-admission.test.ts` (tests tagged SEARCH-001). *Status:* done — `tests/worker/search-access.test.ts`.
- **SEARCH-002 — Exact identifier.** An exact issue identifier lookup reads the primary record and returns it even while full-text indexing is delayed or unavailable, subject to the archive choice in SEARCH-001. On a successful full-text read, that exact issue ranks first, without duplicating it or dropping other matching issues, projects or accessible saved views; results remain bounded. Primary identity, archive choice and operator/session/workspace admission are rechecked before returning results, including after an index failure. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/search-exact-admission.test.ts`, `tests/worker/search-access.test.ts` (tests tagged SEARCH-002). *Status:* done — `tests/worker/search-exact-admission.test.ts`.
- **SEARCH-003 — Index freshness.** Created or changed searchable text appears in full-text results within 10 seconds. Database triggers maintain the full-text entries in the same transaction as searchable source changes, so a committed source change cannot leave its corresponding entry behind. If an index query fails, the exact primary-record lookup remains available under SEARCH-002; other searches are reported unavailable with the last successful index time; Vector never presents a known index failure as a complete empty result. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/search-access.test.ts` (tests tagged SEARCH-003). *Status:* done — `tests/worker/search-access.test.ts`.
- **VIEW-001 — Issue controls.** Issue lists can filter by status, status category, priority, assignee, team, project, cycle, label, due date, blocked state, and parent (all issues, root issues, or children of one exact parent identifier); sort by newest, oldest, Urgent-to-No-priority, workflow status order, due date, or last update; and group by none, status, assignee, priority, project, cycle, or team. Completed issues are hidden by default outside Current. Grouped pages order by the group key before the selected sort and stable identifier. Only groups with rows on the page are rendered, with counts for the complete filtered result; a group may continue onto another page. The issue-list HTTP response exposes page-present `groups` with full filtered counts and `allGroups` with the complete filtered grouping aggregate. Positive offsets require the response `revision` for the same query and workspace calendar date; invalidated or missing identity returns HTTP 409 `pagination_invalidated` without mixed rows, and browsing restarts visibly at offset zero. *Acceptance:* `tests/e2e/dev-runtime.mjs` (tests tagged VIEW-001). *Status:* done — `tests/e2e/dev-runtime.mjs`.
- **VIEW-002 — Saved views.** Anyone signed in can save a named combination of filters, sort, grouping, and list or board layout as a private view, then update, duplicate, or delete it; Owners, Admins and Members can also save a view as shared with the workspace or a team or switch it to shared, and a Guest's views are always private (role matrix). A private view is visible and editable only by its owner; anyone who can see a shared view's teams can open or duplicate it (a Guest only when it covers only their invited teams), but only its owner can update or delete it, and ownership changes only under OPS-004. Deleting a view never changes an issue. Its role-matrix cells are enforced on every door of this clause and proven by the role-matrix journey (`tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts`), Guest cells with a Guest assigned through ROLE-003's native door. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/views-removal.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/core-keyboard-journey.mjs`, `tests/worker/search-access.test.ts` (tests tagged VIEW-002). Role-matrix cells: `tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts`. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/views-removal.test.ts`.
- **VIEW-003 — Control persistence.** The filters, sort, and grouping an operator last applied to Current, My work, the Projects portfolio, the issue list, and the board are stored for that operator and restored on their next visit from any browser, and one visible control resets them to the defaults. A saved view's layout belongs to that view. Opening a saved view never changes the stored controls of the list or board it was opened from. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/views-removal.test.ts` (tests tagged VIEW-003). *Status:* done — `tests/worker/views-removal.test.ts`.
- **VIEW-004 — Bulk edit.** An operator can select several issues in a list and set status, assignee, priority, project, cycle, or labels on all of them in one operation, up to the batch limit in `seed.json → limits`. The operation is atomic: if any selected issue fails ISSUE-004, ISSUE-006, or CYCLE-002, nothing is written and the response names the failing issues by identifier. A successful operation writes one activity entry per issue and is undone as one action by Undo or Command/Ctrl-Z within the ISSUE-020 window. *Acceptance:* A browser journey selecting issues with X and setting status in one operation, plus a failing selection that writes nothing and names the failing identifiers. *Status:* done — `tests/e2e/core-keyboard-journey.mjs`, `tests/e2e/core-undo-journey.mjs`, `tests/worker/issue-undo.test.ts`, `evidence/issue-undo/VIEW-004/`.
- **VIEW-005 — Team board.** The board shows one explicitly selected team's workflow at a time; it never guesses a team in a multi-team workspace. It displays every configured status as a column in that team's workflow order and places the matching issues from the current filtered page in those columns, ordered by the selected derived sort. Each card has a labeled, keyboard-operable status selector. A status change uses the existing version-checked issue mutation (ISSUE-004, ISSUE-006); intake and duplicate decision rules cannot be bypassed, and a stale conflict shows the current server issue and refreshes the board without applying the rejected move. At 390px, a labeled column selector lets the operator choose which status column to view. Board pages explicitly request `pageSize=100` and `grouping=status` through the same issue-list URL and API; ordinary list requests default to 50 and the accepted page sizes are bounded to 50 or 100. Status counts use the same SQL filters as cards across the complete result, with page counts distinguished from totals. Paging reaches cards beyond the first page; completed work remains hidden until explicitly requested. *Acceptance:* A board journey at 1440px and 390px that changes status from a card and recovers from a stale conflict. *Status:* done — `tests/e2e/core-board-journey.mjs`, `evidence/keyboard-board/VIEW-005/`.
- **CMD-001 — Command palette.** The command palette searches issues, projects, saved views, and available commands from one input. The up and down arrows move the highlight, Enter opens the highlighted record or performs the highlighted command, and Escape closes the palette without acting. *Acceptance:* `tests/e2e/core-palette-journey.mjs` exercises search, arrow-key highlight, Enter and Escape, alongside `tests/e2e/core-keyboard-journey.mjs`. *Status:* done — `tests/e2e/core-palette-journey.mjs`, `evidence/keyboard-board/CMD-001/`.
- **CMD-002 — Keyboard navigation.** Outside editable controls, Command/Ctrl-K opens the palette, C opens new issue, G then M opens My work, G then C opens Current, G then P opens Projects, and Escape closes the topmost transient surface. Every command also has a visible, keyboard-operable control. *Acceptance:* `tests/e2e/core-keyboard-journey.mjs` (tests tagged CMD-002). *Status:* done — `tests/e2e/core-keyboard-journey.mjs`.
- **CMD-003 — Focus and selection.** The up and down arrows move a visible focus indicator through the rows of the active list, Enter opens the focused row, X adds or removes it from the selection VIEW-004 acts on, and Escape returns focus from an open detail pane to the row it was opened from. Changing a filter, sort, or grouping keeps the focused row focused while it remains in the list and focuses the first row when it does not. *Acceptance:* A keyboard journey moving focus with arrows, selecting with X, opening with Enter and returning focus with Escape. *Status:* done — `tests/e2e/core-stream-planning-journey.mjs`, `tests/e2e/core-keyboard-journey.mjs`, `evidence/keyboard-planning/CMD-003/`.

## Notifications

- **NOTIFY-001 — Inbox events.** Vector creates an in-app notification, visible within 5 seconds, for assignment of an issue to the member, a direct mention (ISSUE-013), a comment on an issue they follow, the return of an intake item they deferred, the resolution of the last blocker on an issue assigned to them, a due reminder (NOTIFY-005), a triage item they are responsible for (TRIAGE-001), a project or initiative update they follow (PROJ-010), a delegated agent's question or completion (AGENT-002), a review request or status change from Git (GITHUB-005) and an SLA warning (AUTO-004). Members who follow an issue (NOTIFY-002) — its creator, assignee, commenters, mentioned members and explicit followers — are also notified when it is completed, when it is canceled or marked duplicate, when its status changes to another status (one notification per change, grouped with any other change made in the same mutation), when its priority is raised to Urgent, when it becomes blocked by a newly added blocker or a blocking relation on it is added or removed, when its due date changes, and when a resolved thread they took part in is reopened (ISSUE-008). Each event notifies each follower once, never the actor (NOTIFY-003), and a completion caused by automation or Git (GITHUB-003) names that automation as actor. The inbox is always delivered; email, Slack, Teams, mobile push and desktop follow NOTIFY-006 and NOTIFY-007. A thread-reopened event reaches current issue followers who authored its top-level comment or any reply, excludes the reopener, and emits once for each actual resolved-to-open transition; reopening an already open thread emits nothing. Explicit reopen and reply-reopens share this rule. Its inbox label and per-type mute are available; it creates no email by default (NOTIFY-006/NOTIFY-007 own channels). TRIAGE-001, PROJ-010, AGENT-002, GITHUB-005, GITHUB-003 and AUTO-004 inherit their unbuilt events and inbox obligations before they ship. *Acceptance:* the existing collaboration journeys for the original events plus one journey per added event, including: member A creates an issue, member B completes it, and A finds exactly one completion notification naming B within 5 seconds while B finds none; B cancels a second issue A follows and A gets one cancellation notification; B moves a followed issue from Todo to In Progress and raises it to Urgent in one edit and A gets one grouped notification naming both changes; B adds a blocker to it and A gets one blocking notification; a resolved thread's participating follower gets one reopened-thread notification naming the reopener while a nonparticipant, the actor and a repeated reopen get none. GITHUB-003 owns the inherited merged-PR acceptance cell. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/follower-events.test.ts`, `tests/worker/follower-last-blocker.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/core-follower-journey.mjs`, `tests/worker/source-ingest.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/collaboration-events.mjs`, `evidence/issue-follow-up/NOTIFY-001/`.
- **NOTIFY-002 — Followers.** Issue creators, assignees, commenters, and directly mentioned operators follow the issue automatically; an operator can follow or unfollow it explicitly without changing anyone else's following. *Acceptance:* `tests/e2e/collaboration-events.mjs`, `tests/worker/collaboration-atomicity.test.ts`, `tests/worker/discussion-access.test.ts` (tests tagged NOTIFY-002). *Status:* done — `tests/e2e/collaboration-events.mjs`.
- **NOTIFY-003 — No self-notification.** An action never notifies its actor. One event creates at most one notification per recipient and issue even when the recipient qualifies through several follower rules. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/due-reminders.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/collaboration-pagination.mjs`, `tests/e2e/collaboration-reads.mjs` (tests tagged NOTIFY-003). *Status:* done — `tests/worker/due-reminders.test.ts`.
- **NOTIFY-004 — Read and mute.** Opening a notification marks it read, an operator can mark all of theirs read in one action, and the interface shows their unread count. An operator can mute an issue or an event type, which suppresses only their own later matching notifications and alters neither activity history nor another operator's notifications. *Acceptance:* `tests/e2e/collaboration-preferences.mjs`, `tests/worker/collaboration-final-admission.test.ts`, `tests/worker/collaboration-mail.test.ts` (tests tagged NOTIFY-004). *Status:* done — `tests/e2e/collaboration-preferences.mjs`.
- **NOTIFY-005 — Due reminders.** Within 5 minutes of the configured daily reminder time — 09:00 in the workspace timezone by default — Vector notifies the assignee once for each incomplete issue assigned to them that is due that day, and once more on the first run after it becomes overdue. Changing a due date makes the new date eligible and never repeats a reminder already delivered for the same issue and date. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/due-reminders.test.ts` (tests tagged NOTIFY-005). *Status:* done — `tests/worker/due-reminders.test.ts`.
- **NOTIFY-006 — Assignment and mention email.** A qualifying assignment or direct mention creates at most one durable email intent per event, recipient and issue in the same transaction as its source mutation. With configured transactional mail and the recipient's enabled preference, Vector initiates delivery immediately after commit through Base mail. Mutes, actor exclusion and active-recipient checks apply to each qualifying cause and are rechecked before submission. Mail contains only the recipient address, issue title, identifier, readable actor and authenticated canonical app link; it includes no body, evidence, attachment or tracking. Accepted and development outcomes are terminal; uncertain or interrupted claims are never automatically resent. Definite rejection or rate limit permits finite scheduled retries, with failed and uncertain outcomes visible to the recipient. Erasure removes addressed delivery state and anonymizes retained actors; a request already in flight cannot be recalled. *Acceptance:* `tests/e2e/collaboration-delivery-ui.mjs`, `tests/e2e/collaboration-disabled.mjs`, `tests/e2e/collaboration-preferences.mjs` (tests tagged NOTIFY-006). *Status:* done — `tests/e2e/collaboration-delivery-ui.mjs`.

## Code host and delivery

- **GITHUB-001 — Signed PR links.** A current registered repository receiver verifies exact GitHub HMAC bytes before parsing an allowlisted pull_request delivery. Native issue identifiers in the PR title or body link stable repository/PR identity, safe HTTPS URL, title and open/closed/merged state. Unknown identifiers record diagnostics. A PR event changes issue status only through the team's Git automation rules (GITHUB-003), recorded as automation activity. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/github-links.test.ts` for signed linking and state, plus GITHUB-003's status journey. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/github-links.test.ts` (signed PR linking from identifiers in the title or body; branch names and status rules belong to GITHUB-003).
- **GITHUB-002 — Durable latest PR state.** Delivery replay identity and native version/configuration fences commit with PR/link history. Conflicting replay refuses, older state cannot regress newer state, and merged state stays terminal. Current authority gates both fresh and retained results. Safe histories are portable; credentials require destination re-entry and restored historical deliveries never replay. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/github-links.test.ts` (tests tagged GITHUB-002). *Status:* done — `tests/worker/github-links.test.ts`.

## API and platform

- **API-001 — Native scoped API.** Named reveal-once Base tokens admit only the explicit `/api/v1` routes under route-selected scopes: issue list/detail and description history use `issues:read`; issue create/version-checked update, description restore and description checklist mutations use `issues:write`; comment creation and author-only comment checklist mutations use `comments:write`; metadata uses `metadata:read`; project list/detail use `projects:read`; and project create/update use `projects:write`. The project list applies portfolio filters and returns matching counts; create and update accept native project fields, validate dates and record planning-field activity. Project lists currently return the complete workspace list without pagination (API-003). Token expiry, revocation, active issuer/workspace and resource authority are checked on every request; bearer tokens never admit documents, generated functions, private files or token administration. Existing native mutation and notification rules apply; no deletion API or product role is added. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/integration-api.test.ts`, `tests/worker/dev-credentials.test.ts` (tests tagged API-001). *Status:* done — `tests/worker/integration-api.test.ts`.
- **API-002 — Token authority and usage.** Settings exposes safe metadata/usage and revocation. Issuance and each mutation retain current authority inside the same SQL transaction. Usage-write failure denies admission. Removal revokes issued tokens. Stale writes and authority races publish no unauthorized changes; committed native notification intent remains durable after a response denial. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/integration-authority.test.ts`, `tests/worker/integration-api.test.ts` (tests tagged API-002). *Status:* done — `tests/worker/integration-authority.test.ts`.
