- **WEBHOOK-001 — Durable native delivery.** Registered public HTTPS destinations receive minimal native created/updated/completed issue events under stable business identities. Base delivery attempts, backoff, cooldown and accepted/rejected/uncertain results survive restart. Ambiguous sends never retry under fresh IDs; explicit reconciliation requires receiver evidence. Mapped imports and restore create no delivery events. Until Settings has verified the hosting account’s domain inventory or an enforcing service is bound, outbound is visibly unconfigured and native events are retained. The built-in Base transport refuses private and account-owned destinations, pins TLS for non-Cloudflare answers, and uses its explicit all-Cloudflare platform-fetch route with the re-resolution and destination-zone TLS limits in docs/INTEGRATIONS.md. Failed sends never switch transports. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/outbound-webhooks.test.ts`, `tests/worker/issue-deletion-webhooks.test.ts` (tests tagged WEBHOOK-001). *Status:* done — `tests/worker/outbound-webhooks.test.ts`.

## Import, export and portability

Local portable archive commands use the landed Base D1/R2 engine. Mapped CSV/JSON imports, Linear's documented workspace CSV importer with optional owner-produced API-file enrichment, and Jira Cloud's supported All Fields CSV profile use Base import-kit through the same reviewed native adapter in Settings and the terminal. A reviewed run holds up to 4,000 source rows in a file of up to 4 MiB and publishes in staged, individually atomic batches sized by measured SQL admission (docs/MOVE-IN.md). The run is the unit of review and rollback, not the size limit of a move: a whole-workspace move is one migration (DATA-017) made of as many runs as the source needs, with relations resolved across all of them, one reconciliation report and a tested size of 50,000 issues. Linear and Jira are documented-format, synthetic-only profiles; generator evidence covers 2,000 Linear issues and 300 Jira issues. No actual owner export has been supplied, and hosted importer acceptance or capacity is not claimed. Authenticated HTTP restore into a pristine bootstrap uses Vector admission and a real coordinator with the unchanged Base engine. Local Worker HTTP proof includes exact native records/files and fresh historical-owner sign-in after atomic session retirement; hosted HTTPS acceptance remains separate. See docs/ARCHIVES.md.

- **DATA-001 — Export contents.** The portable export contains a manifest, original attachment bytes, and every operator, team, workflow status, issue, comment, attachment metadata, link, relation, evidence revision, intake decision, project, cycle and snapshot, label, saved view, notification, custom field, non-secret setting, idempotency record, failed operation, and audit entry. It never contains a session, signing, source, or provider secret. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/data-http.test.ts`, `tests/worker/data-archive-failure.test.ts`, `tests/worker/data-http-collision.test.ts` (tests tagged DATA-001). *Status:* done — `tests/worker/data-http.test.ts`.
- **DATA-002 — Mapped import preflight.** Separately from portable restore, an Owner or Admin (role matrix; Members and Guests are refused 403) can preflight an import of issues supplied as CSV rows or as a JSON array of objects with the same field names, before anything is written. The fields are title, description, team, status, priority, assignee, project, cycle, labels, estimate, due date, expected outcome, customer impact, source ID, and source record ID. The preflight validates the mapping, the timestamps, and every team, status, assignee, project, cycle, label, duplicate, and dependency reference, and reports every row that would fail with its row number and reason, and every nonempty value it would not import. One preflight covers the whole file of a run, up to the measured run limits in docs/MOVE-IN.md. Its role-matrix cells are enforced on every door of this clause and proven by the role-matrix journey (`tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts`), Guest cells with a Guest assigned through ROLE-003's native door. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/linear-import.test.ts`, `tests/worker/issue-deletion-import.test.ts` (tests tagged DATA-002). Role-matrix cells: `tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts`. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/linear-import.test.ts`.
- **DATA-003 — Atomic mapped import.** An Owner or Admin (role matrix) applying a successful preflight records an import-run identifier and publishes the run in batches, each written atomically, preserving supplied creation and update times. A failed batch writes none of that batch and leaves earlier batches of the run in place for resume (DATA-006). Its role-matrix cells are enforced on every door of this clause and proven by the role-matrix journey (`tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts`), Guest cells with a Guest assigned through ROLE-003's native door. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/data-import-atomic.test.ts` (tests tagged DATA-003). Role-matrix cells: `tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts`. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/data-import-atomic.test.ts`.
- **DATA-004 — Idempotent mapped import.** Reapplying the same import-run identifier and source record identifiers creates no duplicates and returns the first run's result. A mapped import creates normal activity and one audit entry for the run, and no operator notifications. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/data-import-atomic.test.ts`, `tests/worker/imported-person-erasure.test.ts`, `tests/worker/issue-deletion-import.test.ts` (tests tagged DATA-004). *Status:* done — `tests/worker/data-import-atomic.test.ts`.
- **DATA-005 — Exact portable restore.** `rer import --target local` restores a portable DATA-001 archive without synthesizing activity, audit entries, notifications, or new timestamps; its records are equivalent after export, import, and export as `BASE-DATA-001` requires. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/data-archive-failure.test.ts`, `tests/worker/data-http-collision.test.ts`, `tests/worker/data-http-concurrency.test.ts` (tests tagged DATA-005). *Status:* done — `tests/worker/data-archive-failure.test.ts`.
- **DATA-006 — Staged import run.** Only an Owner or Admin starts, resumes or repeats a run (role matrix). One reviewed run of up to the measured limits in docs/MOVE-IN.md (tested at 2,000 issues; larger sources are split into the runs of one migration under DATA-017, never truncated) previews once and publishes in staged batches under one run identifier. Status says truthfully how many of its batches are applied and whether the last batch failed. An interrupted run (a stopped client or a failed batch) resumes from its last committed batch, and every source record lands exactly once. Issues already published are visible and marked as part of an unfinished run. Applying the same reviewed run again returns that run with no new rows; a different file under the same source name conflicts. No notification, mail or webhook is sent for an imported issue. Its role-matrix cells are enforced on every door of this clause and proven by the role-matrix journey (`tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts`), Guest cells with a Guest assigned through ROLE-003's native door. *Acceptance:* `tests/e2e/data-staged-cli.mjs` (tests tagged DATA-006). Role-matrix cells: `tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts`. *Status:* done — `tests/e2e/data-staged-cli.mjs`.
- **DATA-007 — Reconciliation report.** For a finished or partial run, Settings and the terminal give one report and one CSV: source rows read against Vector issues written, by team, status, project, cycle and assignee; every omitted nonempty field with its count; every unresolved or ignored reference with its row; what the source file does not contain at all; and a mapping of each source ID to its Vector identifier. An imported issue shows its source ID, and searching for that source ID finds it. *Acceptance:* `tests/e2e/jira-public-journey.mjs` (exercises this behavior; the test does not yet tag DATA-007). *Status:* partial — `tests/e2e/jira-public-journey.mjs`. *Gap:* tests/e2e/jira-public-journey.mjs registers no tests and no push/pull_request Verify job or named scheduled job reaches it.
- **DATA-008 — Linear API enrichment file.** A JSON file the owner produces with Linear's GraphQL API (docs/LINEAR-API-FILE.md) merges with a Linear CSV run by source ID. Comments become imported comments with their original time and a read-only author name; attachment links become named HTTPS links that are never fetched; blocks and duplicate relations become dependencies and duplicate links when both issues are in the run, and are reported otherwise. Attachment bytes are reported as not in the file. A malformed file (an unsafe link scheme, an unknown source ID, or a comment for an issue outside the run) is refused with row-level reasons and nothing written. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/linear-api-import.test.ts` (tests tagged DATA-008). *Status:* done — `tests/worker/linear-api-import.test.ts`.
- **DATA-009 — Jira CSV preflight and loss review.** A Jira All Fields file is reviewed before any write through the same staged import service. Source IDs remain distinct from mutable issue keys; repeated headers retain their positions and exact original bytes. All date-bearing cells, including comments and attachment metadata, determine one calendar pattern; ambiguous, mixed or impossible dates are refused. Offsetless instants require an explicitly supplied source offset. Every unsupported occurrence remains visible as an omission; mappings must be confirmed and row errors fixed before apply. Documented profile, synthetic-only; no real export supplied. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/jira-import.test.ts`, `tests/worker/jira-loss-review-fixture.ts` (tests tagged DATA-009). *Status:* done — `tests/worker/jira-import.test.ts`.
- **DATA-010 — Jira staged apply and reconciliation.** The reviewed Jira CSV imports preserved issue times (Resolved for completion, or an explicitly confirmed fallback), due dates, estimates, exact labels, confirmed status/priority/operator/sprint mappings, in-file parents and Blocks, canonical Duplicate links and original-author/time comments. Stable source IDs remain authoritative; old keys are visible and searchable. Outside-file and Relates targets are omissions and never bind a same-key native issue. Attachment bytes are never fetched; named HTTPS links require explicit opt-in. Preview, CLI review and stored report share one loss review and configuration fingerprint. Staged apply/replay, SQL reconciliation and whole-run rollback use the existing lifecycle; later native edits refuse the entire rollback without changes. Historical imports dispatch no notifications, mail, webhooks or agents. The public route requires a current Owner or Admin session (role matrix) and same origin; API tokens are not admitted. Its role-matrix cells are enforced on every door of this clause and proven by the role-matrix journey (`tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts`), Guest cells with a Guest assigned through ROLE-003's native door. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/jira-apply.test.ts` (tests tagged DATA-010). Role-matrix cells: `tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts`. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/jira-apply.test.ts`.
- **DATA-011 — Erasing an imported person.** An Owner or Admin (role matrix; Members and Guests are refused 403) can erase a person that imports carried, by exact email and/or up to five exact display names, after a counts-only preview and a typed confirmation. One D1 batch removes the person from every import run, applied or still pending: records that hold them become permanent tombstones that never publish or replay, runs labeled with their email are retired, originals holding them are withdrawn, imported comments they wrote read as deleted with no author name, reconciliation reports lose those cells, and development mail naming them is dropped. Issues already written stay; a pending run still finishes, without the erased records and without links to them. A current or suspended member, or an open invitation, is refused here; a member's native and imported records are erased together by ROLE-012. The erasure journal is append-only, and an archive made before the erasure is refused on restore when a later archive's journal is supplied. Its role-matrix cells are enforced on every door of this clause and proven by the role-matrix journey (`tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts`), Guest cells with a Guest assigned through ROLE-003's native door. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/imported-person-erasure.test.ts`, `tests/worker/base-refresh-upgrade.test.ts` (tests tagged DATA-011). Role-matrix cells: `tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts`. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/imported-person-erasure.test.ts`.

## Target clauses

### Target: Workspace, people and permissions

- **ROLE-001 — Workspace roles.** Each member has exactly one role: Owner, Admin, Member or Guest today; ROLE-013 adds the read-only Viewer target. Owners and Admins invite and remove members, create teams, manage integrations, API tokens, workspace automations, agents and workspace settings; Owners change any role, Admins change Member and Guest roles (Guest assignment follows ROLE-003) and never to or from Owner or Admin; Members create and change work in the teams they can see, manage the teams they own (ROLE-007) and, where a team's permissions allow it (ROLE-009), that team's settings, cycles and templates; Guests act only inside the teams they were invited to (ROLE-003). The installation owner is an Owner and the last Owner cannot be demoted or removed. Every role change is audited (ROLE-005) and takes effect on the member's next request, including open sessions and API tokens. Each action's permitted roles are exactly those in the role matrix below, every clause that names a permission states the same rule, and *operator* in any clause is resolved by ROLE-008. The role-matrix journey carries the cells it inherited from INTAKE-005, INTAKE-006, INTAKE-007, INTAKE-011, PROJ-002, CYCLE-001, CYCLE-002, VIEW-002, DATA-002, DATA-003, DATA-006, DATA-010, DATA-011: Guest accept, defer, end-deferral, decline and project lifecycle changes are refused 403; a Guest commits only in invited teams (404 elsewhere) and cannot share views; another member cannot edit a shared view they do not own; Member/Guest source registration, rotation and revocation, import preflight/apply/start/resume/repeat/rollback and imported-person erasure preview/confirmation are refused 403 with no writes, while Owners/Admins succeed. CYCLE-001 creation/edit/completion/deletion also checks team owners and both ROLE-009 planning permissions, with Guests refused. A row with no door yet is checked by the clause it names when that clause builds the door. *Acceptance:* a browser and HTTP journey in which an Owner promotes a Member to Admin and demotes them, an Admin's attempt to promote to Admin or Owner is refused 403, the demoted member's next settings request returns 403, and last-Owner demotion is refused; plus a role-matrix journey that signs in as an Owner, an Admin, a Member who owns a team, a Member who does not, and a Guest, attempts every row of the role matrix through the browser and through `/api/v1`, and gets exactly the allowed (2xx) or refused (403, or 404 where the subject is invisible under ROLE-002) result in each cell, checking every "team setting" and "planning setting" cell under both values of the team's ROLE-009 permission. *Status:* partial — `tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts` (the registered matrix, private identifier 404 cells and next-request Viewer refusals), `tests/e2e/admin-access-journey.mjs` (workspace roles, rolePrivateCells and viewerRoleJourney). Owners alone change leadership roles; Admins manage Member/Guest/Viewer assignments; current sessions and retained bearer tokens use the current stored role. *Gap:* Complete coverage remains ROLE-008; unbuilt matrix doors inherit their source clauses. Private 404 evidence is this clause's own case, separate from ROLE-002.
- **ROLE-002 — Private teams.** An Admin can make a team private. Its issues, comments, attachments, cycles, triage, project memberships and search entries are visible only to its members and to Owners; a project or initiative that spans a private team shows other viewers only the counts they may see, never titles. Making a team public or private is audited and re-evaluates notifications, followers, Slack channel routes and webhooks. Inherited from WORK-004: after a member loses access to a private-team issue, it is absent from Created, Subscribed and Activity; the private-team journey checks every tab. Inherited from SLACK-003: private-team content posts only to channels an Admin bound to that team; its acceptance journey verifies that project and saved-view bindings cannot bypass this rule. SLACK-003 channel posts and SLACK-004 direct messages for private/restricted items use only a generic notice without actor or text, or a DM to a permitted explicitly mapped member; its acceptance proves this before private teams ship. Inherited from ROLE-007: a Member's self-join of a private team is refused 403, and once its team owner or an Admin adds them they see its issues; the private-team journey checks both. *Acceptance:* a journey that creates a private team, proves a non-member gets 404 on its issue by identifier, sees no search hit, no Current entry and no export rows, then is added and sees all of them. Inherited from WORK-004: after a member loses access to a private-team issue, it is absent from Created, Subscribed and Activity; the private-team journey checks every tab. Inherited from SLACK-003: private-team content posts only to channels an Admin bound to that team; its acceptance journey verifies that project and saved-view bindings cannot bypass this rule. SLACK-003 channel posts and SLACK-004 direct messages for private/restricted items use only a generic notice without actor or text, or a DM to a permitted explicitly mapped member; its acceptance proves this before private teams ship. For private-team creation (ROLE-002), the inherited TEAM-008 duty is quoted verbatim: "Creating a private team still requires Owner/Admin privacy authority (ROLE-002)." For private-team join requests (ROLE-002), the inherited TEAM-007 duty is quoted verbatim: "A private-team request exposes no issue, search hit or membership until approved, and a hidden private team remains 404." *Status:* partial — `tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts` (current private and invited teams scope native, bearer, search, project, notification and document reads; privateVisibilityJourney), `tests/worker/search-access.test.ts` (exact/full-text and options scope), `tests/worker/outbound-broken-destination.test.ts` (privateChannelRouting: current team-only channels, generic notices and membership-gated DMs), `tests/e2e/admin-access-journey.mjs` (privateTeamJourney), `tests/e2e/admin-team-journey.mjs` (privateTeamRequest and privateAdminMetadata at 1440/390). *Gap:* Private reads, membership withdrawal, identifier/search/Current/Created/Subscribed/Activity, counted CSV, exposed requests and team-only generic Slack delivery are implemented. The existing calendar, dashboard, Insights, template and reference journeys exercise their own private/Viewer cells. Browser attachment bytes and webhook privacy transitions remain unproved; their current network-edge and HTTP checks do not establish every-door browser acceptance. Unbuilt Slack unfurls inherit SLACK-005, mobile payloads MOBILE-001/002, documents PROJ-013 and agent contexts AGENT-001/005. Non-member Admins see private-team metadata, owners, membership, counts and privacy controls in Settings without content access; saved definitions re-evaluate current visibility without edits. Ruled archive behavior: Admin archives must exclude unreadable private-team rows and carry only an excluded-team count; Owner archives carry everything. BLOCKED ROLE-002(c): Base portability per-export row scope and manifest count are unavailable in rer 0.2.77; the safe Admin refusal remains until Base supplies them.
- **ROLE-003 — Guests.** ISSUE-023 inherits the Guest cell: copying requires source visibility and creation permission in the destination, and Guests may copy only within invited teams, through every browser and API door. A Guest (a contractor, agency or customer engineer) is invited to named teams only, sees only those teams' issues and projects, cannot see the member directory beyond those teams, cannot create API tokens or integrations, and is labeled as a guest wherever they appear. Before Guests ship, inherit INTAKE-005/006/007, PROJ-002, CYCLE-002 and VIEW-002: accept, defer, end-deferral, decline and project lifecycle changes are refused 403; cycle commitment is allowed only in invited teams and refused 404 elsewhere; shared-view creation is refused, and view visibility is limited to invited teams. INTAKE-011 and DATA-002/003/006/010/011 source management, import management and erasure are refused 403 with no writes. CYCLE-001 cycle management is refused under both planning permissions. PROJ-001 inherits Guest project visibility only through invited contributing teams and 403 refusal of Guest project creation and every project edit before Guests ship. Inherited from ROLE-007: a Guest's access follows the teams an Admin added them to. *Acceptance:* a guest journey that reaches only the invited team and is refused every other team, settings and token route. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts` ([ROLE-003 OPS-001 OPS-002] real Guest invitations and assignments scope native, bearer, search, project, notification and document reads to current invited teams), using `tests/worker/role-guest-journey.ts`: native invitation and role assignment, current invited-team reads across issues/streams/projects/reports/search/options/directory/views/cycles/notifications, queued mail, bearer API, exports/Settings/tokens refusals, hidden relations and Guest labels; the registry-driven every-role-matrix-row case keeps all inherited write cells with a real assigned Guest. `tests/e2e/role-ui-support.mjs` (exerciseGuestRoles), called by `tests/e2e/admin-access-journey.mjs`: named-team invitation/assignment, Guest issue/project/cycle/Account and labels at 1440/390 with axe/overflow checks. Private-team permissions remain ROLE-002. Guests cannot read indivisible historical aggregates or frozen snapshots containing currently hidden references; visible live facts are used by their live reports, and stored historical records are unchanged.
- **ROLE-004 — Single sign-on and provisioning.** Owners can require SAML 2.0 or OpenID Connect sign-in through Okta, Microsoft Entra ID or Google Workspace, restrict sign-up to verified company domains, and provision and deprovision members through SCIM 2.0; deprovisioning — SCIM `active: false` or `DELETE` — suspends the member under ROLE-011, revoking their sessions and credentials within one request and keeping their name on their work, and never erases them (ROLE-012); SCIM `active: true` reactivates the same member. *Acceptance:* a journey against a local SAML/OIDC identity-provider fixture at the network edge that signs a member in, then a SCIM `DELETE` that revokes their session within one request and leaves their name on their comments, and a SCIM `active: true` that reactivates the same member ID. *Status:* missing.
- **ROLE-005 — Audit log.** Owners and Admins can browse, filter and export as CSV a workspace audit log of sign-ins, role and membership changes, suspensions, reactivations and erasures (ROLE-011, ROLE-012), team privacy changes, integration and token changes, automation and agent runs, exports, imports and deletions, each with actor (person, automation, integration or agent), time, action and subject. Entries survive subject deletion with an opaque reference and are never editable. *Acceptance:* a journey that performs one action of each kind and finds each in the audit view and the CSV. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts` ([ROLE-005] native actions populate an immutable workspace audit view and exactly filtered CSV): existing sign-ins, role/membership changes, erasures, integration/token changes, automation, imports/exports and deletions, actor/time/action/opaque subject, all filters, exact CSV, retention and no edit/delete door; `tests/e2e/admin-audit-journey.mjs` (exerciseAuditView, exerciseAuditAccess): Settings view/all filters/CSV and denied Member/Guest page/API through real role assignment, at 1440/390 with axe/overflow checks. Team privacy changes inherit ROLE-002; suspension/reactivation doors inherit ROLE-011, separate full-person erasure ROLE-012, agent runs AGENT-001 and the automation-run framework AUTO-001. Existing automation and imported-person erasure doors are audited now. Previously erased legacy actors retain explicitly unknown opaque references; new events retain actor provenance.
- **ROLE-006 — Apps and agents as actors.** Integrations, automations and coding agents act under their own named identities with explicit scopes, never as a person. Their changes show the app or agent name with the person who installed or delegated to it, and they can be paused or revoked by an Admin at once. *Acceptance:* a journey that installs an agent identity, sees its change attributed to it in activity and audit, revokes it and sees its next API call refused. *Status:* missing.
- **ROLE-007 — Team membership and team owners.** CYCLE-007 inherits the cycle-schedule role rule: team owners, or all team members while the planning permission is All team members, may set their own team's schedule; workspace Owners and Admins may always act. Every team has a member list and one or more team owners. Owners and Admins create teams and become their first team owner; TEAM-008 carries the future workspace setting that also permits Members to create teams; a team owner or an Admin adds and removes the team's members and team owners and sets the team's two permissions (ROLE-009), which decide whether all team members or only its team owners manage the team's settings (TEAM-002, TEAM-005) and its cycles and templates. Members join and leave open public teams themselves from the team directory; TEAM-007 carries invite-only public teams and join requests; a private team (ROLE-002) is joined only when its team owner or an Admin adds the member; Guests are added to a team only by an Admin (ROLE-003). Team membership decides triage rotations (TRIAGE-001), team notifications and subscriptions (NOTIFY-009), My work's team rows, a project's contributing teams (PROJ-014) and, for private teams and Guests, access; for a public team it never restricts what a workspace Member can see (OPS-001). Leaving or being removed from a team keeps the member's assigned issues assigned and listed for the team owner to reassign; it never deletes work. A team's last team owner cannot leave until another is named, and an Admin can always act as team owner. Every change is audited (ROLE-005). CYCLE-001 inherits team-owner cycle creation, edit, completion and deletion permission: Owners/Admins and team owners succeed; other team members follow ROLE-009 and Guests are refused, checked through browser and HTTP doors. The acceptance's private-team cells (refused 403 joining a private team; added by its team owner, then seeing its issues) are ROLE-002's, and membership's consumers switch in their own clauses: triage rotations (TRIAGE-001), team notifications and subscriptions (NOTIFY-009), My work's team rows (WORK-003), contributing teams (PROJ-014), and private-team and Guest access (ROLE-002, ROLE-003). Cycles have no edit, completion or deletion door yet; CYCLE-001 carries those cells with its doors. *Acceptance:* a journey in which a Member joins and leaves a public team from the directory, is refused 403 joining a private team, is added to it by its team owner and then sees its issues, a Guest's self-join is refused, and the last team owner's attempt to leave is refused until another owner is named. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/admin-team-journey.mjs`.
- **ROLE-008 — Operator and permission coverage.** ISSUE-023 inherits the Guest cell: copying requires source visibility and creation permission in the destination, and Guests may copy only within invited teams, through every browser and API door. Where a clause says *operator*, it means a signed-in person of the workspace, of any role, acting in their own session or through a token they own. The word grants nothing by itself: an operator may take an action only when the role-matrix row naming that action's clause allows their role (and, for a team row, their team ownership and the team's ROLE-009 permission), and only on subjects they can see (OPS-001, ROLE-002, ROLE-003). Every state-changing door — each mutation route under `/api` and `/api/v1`, each private server function, each Settings form and each terminal command that writes — is registered against exactly one role-matrix row. A door with no row is refused to every role but Owner and the refusal is audited (ROLE-005), so a new feature cannot ship open to Members or Guests by omission. Settings pages are reachable as the matrix's "Open Settings pages" row states, and every other Settings page and route returns 403. Integrations, automations and agents act under their own identities and scopes (ROLE-006), never under a person's row. Every browser, HTTP, API and command door of INTAKE-005, INTAKE-006, INTAKE-007, INTAKE-011, PROJ-002, CYCLE-001, CYCLE-002, VIEW-002, DATA-002, DATA-003, DATA-006, DATA-010, DATA-011 is registered and tested under the inherited ROLE-001 matrix cells: Guest intake decisions and project lifecycle refused; Guest commitment scoped to invited teams; Guest view sharing refused and edits owner-only; source/import/erasure management Owner/Admin only; CYCLE-001 management follows team ownership and both ROLE-009 values. Refusals change nothing. *Acceptance:* a coverage check that enumerates the registered mutation routes, server functions, Settings forms and write commands and fails when any door has no role-matrix row or a row names a clause that does not exist; the role-matrix journey (ROLE-001), driven from the same registry so a new row is a new set of checked cells; and a Guest journey that requests every Settings path and gets 403 everywhere but Account. *Status:* done — `tests/e2e/role-door-coverage.mjs` assertRoleDoorCoverage (actual Hono mutations, reachable Settings forms, private server functions, each import command and valid contract references; invoked by `tests/e2e/admin-runtime.mjs`); `tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts` every role-matrix row with a door gives each person exactly their cell, through native and bearer doors, including unregisteredAndSettingsJourney in `tests/worker/role-door-journey.ts` (Owner fallback, publicly visible refusal audit, every Guest Settings read and management write), both team permissions and inherited intake, project, cycle, view, source, import and erasure cells; `tests/e2e/role-ui-support.mjs` exerciseGuestRoles (Account-only Settings at 1440 and 390).
- **ROLE-009 — Team-managed permissions.** CYCLE-007 inherits the cycle-schedule role rule: team owners, or all team members while the planning permission is All team members, may set their own team's schedule; workspace Owners and Admins may always act. Each team has two permissions, each either *All team members* or *Team owners only*, set by its team owners or an Owner or Admin. *Who manages team settings* governs workflow statuses (TEAM-002, TEAM-004), team settings (TEAM-005), team labels (ISSUE-014), team custom fields (FIELD-001), triage responsibility, routing rules, request forms and email intake (TRIAGE-001, TRIAGE-002, TRIAGE-004, TRIAGE-005), Git automation rules (GITHUB-003), the team's project statuses (PROJ-016), and the team's automation rules, SLAs, auto-close and auto-archive (AUTO-002, AUTO-003, AUTO-004). *Who manages cycles and templates* governs creating, editing, completing and deleting cycles (CYCLE-001), the cycle schedule (CYCLE-007), and the team's issue and project templates (ISSUE-016, PROJ-012). Owners and Admins pass both permissions whatever their value; Guests fail both whatever their value; a sub-team (TEAM-006) inherits its parent's values unless it sets its own. A change is audited (ROLE-005) and takes effect on the next request. *Policy: `team.permissions.v1`; default: *Who manages team settings* is Team owners only, *Who manages cycles and templates* is All team members.* CYCLE-001 inherits cycle creation, edit, completion and deletion under both planning permissions: non-owner Members succeed under All team members and are refused 403 under Team owners only; Owners/Admins and team owners succeed, Guests are refused under both. Objects not built yet follow the permission in the clauses that build them: team labels (ISSUE-014), team custom fields (FIELD-001), triage responsibility, routing rules, request forms and email intake (TRIAGE-001, TRIAGE-002, TRIAGE-004, TRIAGE-005), Git automation rules (GITHUB-003), the team's project statuses (PROJ-016), automation rules and SLAs (AUTO-002, AUTO-004), TEAM-005's remaining settings, issue and project templates (ISSUE-016, PROJ-012) and sub-team inheritance (TEAM-006); the acceptance's template and routing-rule cells are ISSUE-016's, PROJ-012's and TRIAGE-002's. Cycles have no edit, completion or deletion door yet; CYCLE-001 carries those cells with its doors. *Acceptance:* the ROLE-009 rows of the role-matrix journey: under the defaults, a Member who does not own the team creates a cycle and a team issue template (2xx) and is refused a routing rule and a status rename (403); after the team owner sets *Who manages cycles and templates* to Team owners only and *Who manages team settings* to All team members, the same Member is refused the cycle and the template and admitted the routing rule and the rename; a Guest in the team is refused all four under both values; and each permission change appears once in the audit log. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/admin-team-journey.mjs`.
- **ROLE-010 — Sign in with Google and passkeys.** Beside the magic link (BASE-ACCESS-001), an Owner can offer "Continue with Google" and passkeys on the sign-in page without requiring either. Google sign-in is OpenID Connect with Google as the identity provider: Settings → Sign-in shows this installation's exact authorized redirect URI and JavaScript origin to enter in a Google Cloud OAuth client, the Owner pastes its client ID and secret, and Vector runs a test sign-in before turning it on. A Google sign-in admits only an email Google reports as verified that already belongs to a member or an open invitation, or that is in a domain the Owner allows for sign-up (ROLE-004); it links to the existing member by that email, stores no password, and never creates a second account for the same person. Once an Owner offers passkeys, every member, Guests included, can register one or more passkeys (WebAuthn, on the device or a security key) under Account and sign in with any of them; removing a passkey, or suspending the member (ROLE-011), revokes it. Enabling, disabling and each sign-in by either method are audited (ROLE-005). The magic link stays available unless an Owner requires SSO under ROLE-004, which then overrides both methods. The mobile apps (MOBILE-001) offer the same methods in the system browser. Viewers may register and remove only their own passkeys and cannot offer or withdraw sign-in methods. *Acceptance:* a journey against a Google OpenID Connect fixture at the network edge that enables Google sign-in, signs an invited member in with a verified email and finds the same member record, is refused for an unverified email and for an address outside the members, invitations and allowed domains, and then signs the same member in by magic link with Google still on; plus a browser journey with a virtual WebAuthn authenticator that registers a passkey, signs out, signs in with it and finds a session, and an Admin's attempt to enable Google sign-in refused 403. *Status:* missing.
- **ROLE-011 — Suspend and reactivate members.** Suspension is the default offboarding: Settings → Members' Suspend action (Owners and Admins; only an Owner suspends an Owner or Admin), a member's own Leave workspace, and SCIM `PATCH` with `active: false` or `DELETE` on the user (ROLE-004) all produce the same suspended state, which takes effect on the member's next request. It revokes every session on every device, every personal API token (API-001), every OAuth grant they authorized (API-004) and the MCP sessions under them (AGENT-005), every passkey (ROLE-010), every mobile, desktop and Web Push registration (MOBILE-002, MOBILE-005), and their linked Slack, Teams and GitHub identities (SLACK-001, SLACK-004, TEAMS-001, GITHUB-006), so a suspended member's SLACK-004 link is deleted, no Slack direct message reaches them and a signed Slack action from their account is refused; magic-link, Google and SSO sign-in for that email are refused while they are suspended. A suspended member no longer counts in the member count or any seat limit, cannot be mentioned, assigned, added to a team or project, or delegated work, and receives no notification or digest; their team memberships and team ownerships are kept but inactive, and a team left with no active team owner is managed by Admins until one is named (ROLE-007). Their name stays, labeled "Suspended", on every comment, description revision, activity entry, audit entry (ROLE-005), project update and customer request they wrote and on their profile, which Owners and Admins open from the member directory's Suspended filter to see their issue activity; the directory lists suspended members apart from active ones. In the same dialog the person suspending chooses whether the member's non-terminal issues stay assigned (each flagged "Assignee suspended" in lists, Current and the Members page for reassignment) or are unassigned; projects they led and shared views they owned transfer as OPS-004 states; their private saved views, favorites and drafts are kept hidden and return on reactivation; and coding-agent sessions they delegated (AGENT-001) are paused and listed for an Admin to reassign or cancel. Reactivation — by an Owner or Admin from the Suspended filter (only an Owner reactivates a former Owner or Admin), by SCIM `active: true`, or by inviting the same email again — restores the same member record and ID with its role, history, team memberships and private views; it never restores a revoked session, token, OAuth grant, passkey, push registration or linked identity, so the member signs in afresh. Suspension, leaving and reactivation each write one audit entry with the actor and the method (Settings, self or SCIM), and neither suspension nor any SCIM call anonymizes anything (ROLE-012). The portable export (DATA-001) carries each member's state with the suspension time and actor, and a restore (DATA-005) brings a suspended member back suspended, with no session or credential. Viewers may leave the workspace themselves but cannot suspend or reactivate another member. *Acceptance:* a browser and HTTP journey in which an Admin suspends a member who wrote two comments and completed an issue, choosing to keep assignments: the member's next page request and their API token are refused 401, their open issue is still assigned and flagged, both comments and the completion activity entry still show their name marked "Suspended", the mention and assignee pickers no longer offer them, and the member directory's Suspended filter lists them with their activity; the same Admin's attempt to suspend an Owner is refused 403; a SCIM fixture `PATCH /scim/v2/Users/{id}` with `active: false` on a second member produces the same state, and a later `active: true` reactivates them; the Admin reactivates the first member, who signs in by magic link to the same member ID with both comments, their team memberships and their private view intact while the old API token stays refused; an export and restore of a workspace with one suspended member brings them back suspended; and the audit log holds one entry per suspension and reactivation. Inherited from SLACK-004: the suspended member's Slack link is deleted, a later mention of them produces no Slack direct message and a signed Slack action from their Slack account is refused. *Status:* partial — `src/core/features/settings/removal.ts`, `tests/worker/removal-boundary.test.ts` (removal revokes sessions and invitations, transfers led projects and owned shared views, and unassigns issues; the suspended state, kept names, the assignment choice, token, OAuth, passkey and push revocation, and reactivation are absent).
- **ROLE-012 — Erase a member's personal data.** Erasure is a separate, deliberate action, offered only on a suspended member (an active member is suspended first, ROLE-011), to Owners and Admins (only an Owner erases a former Owner or Admin), from the member's profile or `/api/v1` — for example to honor a departed employee's or contractor's deletion request under the CCPA. It shows a counts-only preview (comments, revisions, activity and audit entries, project updates, customer requests, imported records under their email, and the external copies Vector cannot recall) and runs only after the actor types the member's email to confirm. One transaction then replaces the member's name, email, avatar and profile with "Former member" and an opaque reference on every comment, revision, activity entry, project update, customer request and audit entry, keeping the content and the audit trail; deletes their private saved views, favorites, drafts, notifications, passkeys and preferences; applies DATA-011 to every imported record under their email; and runs SEC-003 for their Slack, Teams, GitHub and agent identities, listing the external copies it cannot recall. An erased member can never be reactivated, a later invitation of the same email creates a new member, and the erasure writes exactly one audit entry with the actor and an opaque subject reference. The erasure journal is append-only, so restoring an archive made before the erasure is refused when a later archive's journal is supplied (as DATA-011). Suspension, leaving and SCIM deprovisioning never erase. *Acceptance:* a journey that suspends a member with two comments and an activity entry, finds their name still on all three, previews erasure and finds the counts, is refused when the typed email does not match, then erases with the correct email and finds the comments and the entry kept under "Former member", the member's private view gone, an imported comment under their email anonymized, exactly one new audit entry, and reactivation refused 409; plus an erasure attempt on an active member refused 409, an Admin's erasure of a former Admin refused 403, and a Member's erasure refused 403. *Status:* partial — `src/core/features/settings/removal.ts`, `src/core/features/settings/removal-history.ts`, `tests/worker/removal-boundary.test.ts` (today's removal anonymizes the member's references in comments, activity and audit and deletes their private views as part of every removal; a separate erase action on a suspended member, its preview, the typed confirmation and the single audit entry are absent).

- **ROLE-013 — Read-only Viewers.** A Viewer is a stakeholder who reads public teams' work and shared views, and private-team work only after an Owner or Admin explicitly adds them. Unlike a Guest, a Viewer never creates or changes work, including issues, comments, reactions, labels, projects, documents, memberships, integrations or API tokens. Owners and Admins invite Viewers and change Member, Guest and Viewer roles; only Owners change a role to or from Owner or Admin. The Viewer can open Account and the read-only directory and Labels, manage their own sign-in methods and leave the workspace. Reads, search, exports, notifications, API and agent context apply the same visibility; changing to Viewer removes write authority on the next request. *Acceptance:* A browser and HTTP journey that invites a Viewer, signs them in, reads a public issue, project and shared view, refuses every work mutation (including issue edits, comments, reactions, label application and project edits) with 403 and no writes, shows no private-team search result or identifier lookup until an Owner or Admin adds them, and proves a Guest still creates work only in invited teams while the Viewer remains read-only there. Repeat with an open session after a Member is changed to Viewer; the next mutation is refused. Account sign-in, sign-out and own passkey management still work. For team creation by Viewers (ROLE-013), the inherited TEAM-008 duty is quoted verbatim: "Guests and Viewers never create teams or change this workspace permission." Scope: Viewers only. For team joining by Viewers (ROLE-013), the inherited TEAM-007 duty is quoted verbatim: "Guests and Viewers never self-join or request." Scope: Viewers only. ROLE-013's inherited Viewer duties are TEAM-007's and TEAM-008's only, quoted above. *Status:* partial — `tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts` (viewerVisibilityJourney: real invitation, native/bearer registered work-mutation census with unchanged storage, private reads, Guest contrast, role transitions and own leave), `tests/worker/project-fields-upgrade.test.ts` (populated 0095 upgrade), `tests/worker/data-http.test.ts` (Viewer archive restore), `tests/e2e/admin-access-journey.mjs` (viewerRoleJourney at 1440/390). Migration 0097 stores Viewer in the role and invitation CHECK constraints; the door registry refuses work mutations, including a Member's next request after conversion. ROLE-013 stays partial under ct 25 Oct 04:20Z. `tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts` ("current private and invited teams scope native, bearer, search, project, notification and document reads; Viewers remain read-only", worker-2, loading viewerVisibilityJourney): each of 136 native and 26 bearer work/administrative write doors returns 403 with all 245 snapshotted tables unchanged (request-budget counters excluded); bearer-use audit append is checked separately with prior audit rows unchanged. The same case positively reads workspace Labels and the issue label. `tests/e2e/viewer-role-journey.mjs` ("Owner invites a Viewer through named-team Settings; accepted sign-in reads public work without write controls at 1440 and 390" and "Viewer signs in and signs out through native account controls at 1440 and 390 and loses admission", administration through `tests/e2e/admin-runtime.mjs`) positively reads Labels on the issue and in Settings and performs native sign-in and sign-out at both widths, with overflow, serious/critical axe and hydration assertions. One-off persisted writes at native project PATCH, native integration destination POST and bearer project PATCH each fail the per-door storage comparison at `tests/worker/role-viewer-journey.ts` line 66; the normal journey passes. The earlier silent-pass report was not reproduced; the existing comparison already catches these writes. `tests/worker/role-viewer-census.ts` supplies explicit bodies and seeded identities for the original 136 native and 26 bearer mutations plus three Slack settings mutations and the OAuth callback (140 native state-changing doors in total), including raw file upload, own comment and description checklists, import review/apply and erasure confirmation; resource schemas validate the administration, initiative, release and view inputs. Both destination restore doors use active completed-restore fixtures with positive Owner grant/review controls and unchanged Viewer refusals. *Gap:* Unresolved: (b) agent contexts apply Viewer visibility: a duty of AGENT-001/005 (missing, unbuilt), no Viewer assertion yet; (c) own passkey management for Viewers has no asserting test (carried by ROLE-010, missing). ROLE-013 re-proposes DONE only after PROJ-013 and agent-context Viewer assertions exist, or when ROLE-010 and those clauses name the inheritance in both documents and are asserted.

### Target: Teams and workflows

- **TEAM-005 — Team settings.** Each team chooses whether it uses triage, cycles and estimates; its estimate scale (points 1–100 as ISSUE-011, Fibonacci, exponential or T-shirt sizes mapped to points); its default issue template; its triage responsibility (TRIAGE-001); its Git automation rules (GITHUB-003); and its auto-close and auto-archive periods (AUTO-003). Turning a feature off hides it for that team without deleting history. Inherited from ROLE-009: these settings change under the team's *Who manages team settings* permission; Owners and Admins always may and Guests never (role matrix). *Acceptance:* a settings journey that switches a team to T-shirt estimates and off cycles, then sees the issue editor and Current reflect it while closed cycle snapshots stay unchanged. *Status:* missing.
- **TEAM-006 — Sub-teams.** A team can have sub-teams up to three levels deep (for example Platform → Infrastructure, Developer Experience). A sub-team keeps its own identifier prefix, members and issues, inherits its parent's workflow, labels, custom fields, cycle cadence and Git rules unless it overrides them, and the parent team's issue lists, boards, cycles view, insights and triage can include sub-team issues with one toggle. Linear team hierarchies import as sub-teams. Moving a team under another parent never changes issue identifiers. Inherited from ROLE-009: a sub-team keeps its parent's two permissions unless it sets its own. Creating or reparenting a sub-team and changing inherited settings or overrides requires the team-setting permission on both child and parent; creation also requires TEAM-008. Owners/Admins always may; Guests/Viewers never (role matrix). *Acceptance:* a journey that creates a parent with two sub-teams, finds sub-team issues in the parent's list with the toggle on and absent with it off, and overrides one sub-team's workflow without changing its sibling's; through browser and HTTP, check Owners/Admins, team owners and other Members under both team-setting values on child and parent, refuse a Member missing permission on either side and every Guest/Viewer, and check TEAM-008 on creation. *Status:* missing.

- **TEAM-007 — Team join permissions.** Team membership is separate from visibility: a public team can allow open joining or require an invitation; private teams always require approval (ROLE-002). Members join open public teams themselves, request membership only where the team has explicitly exposed a request action, and otherwise are added by an Owner, Admin or team owner. A request grants no membership or read access; a hidden private team remains invisible. Owners, Admins and team owners set the membership mode and approve requests or add Members; only Owners/Admins add Guests or Viewers. The refusal for Viewers is delivered with ROLE-013; the refusal for Guests remains this clause's. Leaving preserves assigned work and the last-team-owner guard (ROLE-007). Public-team reads remain workspace-wide regardless of membership mode (OPS-001). *Policy: `team.join.v1`; default: public teams allow open joining, private teams are invite-only.* *Acceptance:* A browser and HTTP journey that lets a Member join an open public team, switches it to invite-only and refuses immediate self-join with 403, records a visible join request without granting access, and lets a team owner approve it once; an ordinary Member cannot approve or add another member. The private-team request and hidden-team points are delivered with ROLE-002. Owners/Admins can add members, Guests/Viewers cannot self-join or request, and changing membership mode is audited once. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/admin-team-journey.mjs` (teamJoinPermissions and teamJoinReaderCells at 1440/390: public open/invite-only, private exposed request with no issue/search/membership, once-only approval, hidden 404, Owner/Admin assignment, Member team-owner refusal to add Viewer, Guest/Viewer join/request refusals, membership-mode audit). Private/Viewer cells use this receiving clause's own journey; ROLE-002/013 retain independent source-clause evidence.
- **TEAM-008 — Who may create teams.** Owners and Admins set the workspace permission *Who may create teams* to *Owners and Admins only* or *Members too*. Owners/Admins always create teams; Members create public teams only under Members too and become the new team's first team owner (ROLE-007). Private-team creation authority is delivered with ROLE-002. The refusal for Viewers is delivered with ROLE-013; the refusal for Guests remains this clause's. Enforcement is identical through browser, HTTP and API, takes effect on the next request and records each permission change in audit (ROLE-005). *Policy: `team.create.v1`; default: Owners and Admins only.* *Acceptance:* A browser and HTTP journey that refuses a Member's team creation with 403 under Owners and Admins only, lets an Admin enable Members too and then lets the same Member create a public team and become its first team owner, refuses the Member's attempt to change the workspace setting, and refuses Guest and Viewer creation under both values. Return to Owners and Admins only and refuse creation on the Member's next request, without affecting their existing team; record each setting change once in audit. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/admin-team-journey.mjs` (teamCreationPermission and privateTeamCreationCells at 1440/390: both workspace values, current native and scoped API refusal for Guest/Viewer, next-request Member refusal, first team ownership, Owner/Admin private creation and one audit per change). Private/Viewer cells use this receiving clause's own journey; ROLE-002/013 retain independent source-clause evidence. The Member-created response and subsequent state assert `isPrivate === false`; D1 asserts `is_private = 0` in the Worker case and each 1440/390 teamCreationPermission iteration.

### Target: Issues

- **ISSUE-016 — Issue templates.** Owners and Admins create, edit and delete workspace templates. A team's templates are created, edited and deleted by Owners, Admins and the team's owners, and by all its members while its *Who manages cycles and templates* permission is All team members, the default (ROLE-009). Guests start issues from a team's templates in their invited teams but never create one (role matrix). A template prefills title pattern, Markdown description, status, priority, labels, assignee, estimate, project, custom fields and sub-issues. New issue, the command palette, Slack (SLACK-002), email intake (TRIAGE-005) and request forms (TRIAGE-004) can start from a template; applying one never overwrites fields already typed. Linear and Jira templates are importable where the source export carries them. *Acceptance:* a journey that creates a bug template with two sub-issues, creates an issue from it through the browser and through `/api/v1`, and finds both sub-issues linked; plus the ISSUE-016 rows of the role-matrix journey: a Member's workspace template is refused 403, a non-owner Member's team template is admitted under the default and refused 403 under Team owners only, and a Guest's template is refused 403. *Status:* missing.
- **ISSUE-017 — Reactions.** Members react to issue descriptions and comments with emoji; reactions show who reacted, never notify by default, sync with Slack thread reactions (SLACK-002) and are included in export. Owners, Admins and Members may react, Guests may react only where they may comment, and Viewers are refused 403 with no write. *Acceptance:* a journey that adds and removes a reaction, sees the count update in a second session within the SYNC-001 window, and finds it in the archive; plus a Slack Events fixture in which a `reaction_added` on a synced thread's message adds that reaction to the mapped comment, and a reaction added in Vector calls `reactions.add` once on the Slack message. *Status:* partial — `src/core/features/reactions/`, `tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts` (viewerVisibilityJourney and registered matrix cells), `tests/e2e/collaboration-runtime.mjs` (description/comment add/remove, names, second-session five-second refresh and no notification at 1440/390), `tests/e2e/data-roundtrip.mjs` (portable reaction rows). *Gap:* Slack thread reaction synchronization awaits SLACK-002.
- **ISSUE-018 — Description history.** Every saved description revision is kept with author and time, including the single revision each checklist tick writes (ISSUE-025); members can compare any two revisions and restore one, which writes a new revision and activity entry rather than rewriting history. *Acceptance:* a journey that edits a description three times, compares revisions one and three, restores revision one and finds four revisions with the restore attributed. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/description-history.test.ts`; `evidence/issue-writing/ISSUE-018/`.
- **ISSUE-019 — Sub-issue progress.** A parent issue shows its sub-issues in order with status, assignee and a completed-of-total count; members create, reorder and convert sub-issues inline. An optional team rule completes the parent when every sub-issue completes and reopens it when one reopens, recorded as automation activity. *Acceptance:* a journey that creates three sub-issues, completes them, and sees the parent count and the optional auto-complete rule act once. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/subissues.test.ts`, `tests/worker/subissues-access.test.ts`, `tests/worker/subissues-import.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/core-subissues-journey.mjs`.
- **ISSUE-020 — Undo and Recently deleted.** Every member action that changes issues — a deletion, a property or status change, a bulk edit (VIEW-004), a move between teams or cycles, a duplicate decision — shows an Undo control for 10 seconds, and Command/Ctrl-Z outside an editable control undoes the member's most recent such action within that window, writing the reversal as a new activity entry under ISSUE-006 version checks (a stale undo shows the conflict and changes nothing). A deleted issue leaves every list, board, search result, Current and My work at once and appears in Settings → Recently deleted (and the team's Recently deleted list) for 30 days with who deleted it and when; members permitted by `issue.can-delete.v1` and its deleter restore it from there or by Undo. Restore brings back the same identifier, title, description, every comment and thread, attachment bytes, links, evidence, relations (blocks, related, duplicate, parent and children whose links were not since replaced), followers, labels, project, cycle (if still open, otherwise uncommitted), customer requests and activity, and appends one restore activity entry. During retention, an exact identifier lookup or link answers "deleted — restorable" to members who could see it, source re-delivery returns 410 (INTAKE-002), and webhooks receive one deleted event and, on restore, one restored event. After 30 days the scheduled purge runs the ISSUE-010 cascade exactly once, after which the identifier answers 404 and is never reused (ISSUE-002). The purge only completes a deletion a member started and could undo during retention; it is not automatic deletion under BASE-DATA-002, which holds unchanged: Vector never purges an issue, file or comment that no person deleted. *Policy: `issue.deleted-retention.v1`; default: 30 days, configurable by Owners from 7 to 90.* Inherited from ROLE-001: only Owners set the Recently deleted retention; Owners and Admins delete any issue to Recently deleted and restore any, a Member deletes their own issues (any issue in their teams when the setting is on) and restores the issues they deleted, and Guests do neither (role matrix). *Acceptance:* a journey that deletes an issue carrying two comments in a thread, a blocking relation and a parent link, restores it by Undo within 10 seconds and by Recently deleted after a reload, and finds the same identifier, comments, relations and one restore activity entry each time; deletes it again, advances the workspace clock 30 days, runs the purge twice and finds the issue gone with 404 by identifier and no reuse of its number; and presses Command/Ctrl-Z after a bulk priority change and finds every issue's previous priority back with one activity entry each. *Status:* partial — `tests/worker/issue-undo.test.ts` (single-issue Undo restores status timestamps, assignment, labels, cycle and team through native commands; parentUndoScenario in `tests/worker/parent-undo-scenario.ts`, extended by parentUndoBlockers in `tests/worker/parent-undo-blockers.ts`), `tests/e2e/core-undo-journey.mjs`, `tests/e2e/core-undo-properties.mjs`, `tests/e2e/core-undo-parent.mjs` (undoParentJourney and undoPrivateParentJourney in `tests/e2e/core-undo-parent-member.mjs`, reached by core Undo and keyboard journeys in the push runtime), `evidence/issue-undo/ISSUE-020/` (Native property, bulk, team and cycle, duplicate and parent-link set, change and clear changes offer session-bound, version-checked Undo for ten seconds with attributed reversal activity. Parent Undo restores the original edge and position, fences child and affected parent state and ordering, restores exact pre-action parent status timestamps including manual completion, permits authorized parent commands under unreadable ancestors without disclosing them, and refuses expiry, replay, later work, foreign sessions and withdrawn access without storage changes or hidden identifiers; older stored actions remain readable. Recoverable issue deletion, Recently deleted, complete rich restoration, retention configuration and purge, deleted and restored events, source replay fences, intake-decision Undo, blocking and related-link Undo and other unselected action families remain owed. ISSUE-010 Member deletion remains restricted until recovery ships).
- **ISSUE-021 — Issue types.** A team can enable issue types (default Bug, Feature, Task and Epic; renameable, with icons) so an issue has at most one type; a type can carry its own template (ISSUE-016), required custom fields (FIELD-001) and request form (TRIAGE-004), and lists, boards, filters, insights and SLAs (AUTO-004) can use it. An Epic-type issue can parent other issues (REL-006) and roll up their progress (ISSUE-019). Jira issue types import to types by explicit mapping (DATA-013), and Linear or GitHub issue types import where the source carries them (DATA-012, DATA-014). Turning types off keeps each issue's last type in history. *Acceptance:* a journey that enables types on a team, creates a Bug whose required Severity field is refused when empty, groups a list by type, and a Jira fixture whose Story and Bug issues import to the mapped types. *Status:* missing.
- **ISSUE-022 — Issue start date.** An issue has an optional start date, which must be on or before its due date when both are set. It is set in the issue editor, as a list and board property (VIEW-008), by bulk edit (VIEW-004), by dragging on the timeline (VIEW-007) and through `/api/v1`; it filters and sorts like the due date, appears in exports (DATA-001) and the API, and each change is an activity entry (ISSUE-007). Jira's Start date field and a GitHub Projects date field mapped to it import onto it (DATA-012, DATA-013). *Acceptance:* a journey that sets an issue's start date in the browser and through `/api/v1`, finds a start date after the due date refused 400, filters a list to issues starting this week, finds the issue drawn from start to due on the timeline, and finds one activity entry per change. *Status:* done — `tests/e2e/timeline-journey.mjs` (timelineJourney: browser creation and editing, native API changes, invalid intervals, starts-this-week filtering, list and board properties, bulk edits, timeline interval and both endpoint drags, one activity entry per change, Undo and stale refusal at 1440 and 390), invoked by `tests/e2e/calendar-month-journey.mjs` (calendarMonthJourney) in Verify; `tests/worker/integration-api.test.ts` (Base scoped token authority drives native routes, usage, stale writes and revocation without HTML or file admission: API date validation, week filtering and chronological sorting); `tests/worker/jira-import.test.ts` (Jira loss review counts every source concept and refuses unconfirmed mappings, omissions, ambiguous identities and dates before staged native writes: Start date, mapped date import and rollback); `tests/worker/data-http.test.ts` (native archive HTTP requires current owner, origin and job identity; authenticated pristine bootstrap restores atomically and requires fresh historical owner authority: start and due dates); `tests/worker/issue-copy.test.ts` (native and scoped API copies preserve fields and ordered descendants, exclude discussion and map cross-team values with a loss report); `tests/worker/issue-undo.test.ts` (single-issue Undo restores status timestamps, assignment, labels, cycle and team through native commands; native Undo reverses one atomic action and refuses stale, replayed, expired or foreign actions).
- **ISSUE-023 — Copy issue.** From an issue's menu, the command palette (CMD-001) and `/api/v1`, anyone who can create issues in a team copies an issue into it. Its role-matrix cells, Guests copying only within invited teams, are enforced on every door and proven by the role-matrix journey (`tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts`) with a Guest assigned through the native ROLE-003 role door. The copy gets a new identifier and the original's title, description, priority, estimate, labels, project, custom-field values; its cycle when it stays in the same team; and, when chosen, copies of its sub-issues and its links. It never copies comments, activity, attachments (except the files embedded in its description, which ISSUE-024 copies so the copy renders them), followers or customer requests, starts in the team's default Backlog status, and both issues record "copied from" / "copied to" in activity. Copying into another team must map statuses, labels and custom-field values for the destination team and report any value it drops. Destination statuses map to the first Backlog status; today labels and the untyped custom-field map have workspace scope and map by identity. Cross-team cycle commitments are omitted and reported. Typed and team-scoped custom-field definitions (FIELD-001) and team labels (ISSUE-014) must participate in this mapping before those capabilities ship. Embedded-file copying remains an inherited obligation of ISSUE-024 before embedded files ship. Native team moves (ISSUE-012) select an explicit same-category status and preserve labels and custom-field values unchanged. *Acceptance:* a journey that copies an issue with two labels, a custom-field value and two sub-issues through the browser with sub-issues included and through `/api/v1` without, finds new identifiers with equal fields, no comments copied, and the copied-from entries on both issues. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/issue-copy.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/issue-copy-runtime.mjs`.
- **ISSUE-024 — Inline images and video.** A member puts a screenshot, photo or screen recording into an issue description, a comment, a template (ISSUE-016) or a document (PROJ-013) by pasting it from the clipboard, dragging and dropping it onto the editor, choosing `/file` or `/image` from the slash menu (ISSUE-026), or pressing Command/Ctrl-Shift-U, one file or several at once (role matrix: anyone who can edit that text). Each file uploads under ISSUE-009 as an attachment of that issue or document — listed in its attachments with uploader and time — while a placeholder with upload progress holds its place at the cursor; on completion the placeholder becomes an embed that references the attachment by ID, and a refused or failed upload (a type or size outside `seed.json → limits`) leaves a visible error in its place and stores nothing. Images (PNG, JPEG, GIF, WebP) show inline where they were placed with no click to load: at most the editor's width, with their stored intrinsic size reserved so the text does not jump, GIFs animated, and editable alt text; clicking one opens a full-size viewer with zoom, arrow keys through the issue's images, Escape to close, and Download. Videos (MP4, WebM, MOV) show as an inline player with a poster frame, play, pause, seek, full screen and Download; seeking works because the file route answers HTTP `Range` requests with 206; a video whose codec the viewer's browser cannot play shows its name, size and Download in place of the player, never a broken element. `video/mp4`, `video/webm` and `video/quicktime` join `seed.json → limits.attachmentMimeTypes`, and a video may be up to 250 MB, uploaded in resumable parts (R2 multipart) so a dropped connection resumes rather than restarts. On a new issue not yet created, files stage with the member's draft (ISSUE-015) and attach when the issue is created; discarding the draft deletes them; staged files whose draft is gone are listed under Settings → Account → Unattached uploads, where the member attaches or deletes them, and they are never deleted automatically (BASE-DATA-002). Removing an embed from the text keeps the attachment (removing the file is an ISSUE-009 action), and an embed whose attachment was removed shows "File removed"; restoring an older revision (ISSUE-018) that embeds a removed file shows the same marker. An embed resolves only to an attachment of the same issue or document; copying an issue (ISSUE-023) or starting from a template copies the embedded files into the new issue so it renders them. The same embeds are captured, pasted and shown inline in the mobile apps (MOBILE-001), whose camera and photo picker remove location metadata before upload unless the member keeps it; synced Slack and Teams threads carry them both ways (SLACK-002, TEAMS-001); notification email links the issue and never attaches or hotlinks the file (NOTIFY-006); the API (API-003) returns embeds with their attachment IDs; and the portable export (DATA-001) keeps each embed as an archive-relative reference to the original bytes, so a restore (DATA-005) shows the same images. *Baseline reconciliation (BASE-INPUT-002, BASE-INPUT-003).* Inline display applies only to the installation's own uploads of the allow-listed image and video types, referenced by attachment ID. Each is still served by the authorized same-origin file route with its declared content type, `Content-Disposition: attachment`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Cache-Control: private, no-store` and the session and team checks BASE-INPUT-003 requires. The page shows it through an `<img>` or `<video>` element, which a browser renders without acting on the download disposition, so opening the file's URL directly still downloads it and BASE-INPUT-003 holds unchanged. The application CSP allows `img-src` and `media-src` only from `'self'` and `blob:` (the local preview of a file being pasted). SVG, HTML and every other type are never shown inline. Every image reference that is not one of the installation's own attachments of the same issue or document — an external URL, another issue's attachment, another origin — is a remote image and keeps BASE-INPUT-002's load-on-request button. A member who cannot see the issue (a Guest outside the team, a non-member of a private team under ROLE-002) gets 404 from the file route, so a copied embed URL reveals nothing. *Acceptance:* a browser journey that pastes a PNG from the clipboard into a new issue's description, creates the issue, drags an MP4 into a comment and reloads: the image shows inline with no load button and opens in the full-size viewer, the video plays and seeks (its `Range` request answered 206), both files appear in the issue's attachments, the file route still answers with an attachment disposition and `nosniff`, an external `https://` image in the same description still shows its Load image button, an SVG upload is refused before storage, and a second member outside a private team gets 404 for the PNG's URL; the same issue at 390px and in the iOS and Android simulator builds (MOBILE-001) shows the PNG inline; copying the issue (ISSUE-023) shows the PNG in the copy from the copy's own attachment; and a DATA-001 export restored under DATA-005 shows the same PNG inline. *Status:* partial — `src/core/features/discussion/routes.ts`, `tests/worker/discussion-file-admission.test.ts` (PNG, JPEG, WebP and GIF attachments upload and are served same-origin with an attachment disposition and `nosniff`; paste, drop, the upload command, inline display, the viewer, video types, `Range` responses and resumable upload are absent, and `src/core/features/markdown/ui/markdown.tsx` refuses image paths on the installation itself).
- **ISSUE-025 — Checklists.** A description, comment, template or document can hold checklists: typing `[]` or `- [ ]` at the start of a line, pressing Command/Ctrl-Shift-7, or choosing `/checklist` (ISSUE-026) starts one, and items nest and reorder like list items. They are stored as GitHub-flavored Markdown task-list items (`- [ ]`, `- [x]`), so they pass unchanged through the API, export, copying as Markdown, and imports from GitHub, Linear and Jira descriptions. Items show as checkboxes that can be ticked and unticked in place without entering edit mode — in a description or document by anyone who can edit the issue (role matrix; a Guest in invited teams), in a comment only by its author, as ISSUE-008 limits comment edits; everyone else sees a read-only checkbox. Each tick is one version-checked mutation (ISSUE-006) that writes exactly one description revision (ISSUE-018), attributed to the member, and one activity entry naming the item ("checked 'Repro on iOS'"). A tick sent against a stale version applies when that item's text is unchanged in the current version and is otherwise refused 409 with the current text; during a live editing session (SYNC-003) a tick merges like any other edit. An issue whose description has a checklist shows its completed-of-total count ("2/5") as a list and board property (VIEW-008) and in the API; ticking never changes the issue's status by itself. The mobile apps (MOBILE-001) tick items; a synced Slack or Teams comment (SLACK-002) shows a checklist as text (☐ and ☑), and ticks are made in Vector. *Acceptance:* a browser journey that types `[]` to start a three-item checklist in a new issue's description, saves, reloads, ticks one item without entering edit mode, and finds the box ticked, one new description revision attributed to the member whose Markdown has `- [x]`, one activity entry naming the item, and "1/3" on the issue's list row; a Member who did not write a comment finds its checklist read-only and their tick refused 403 through `/api/v1`; two sessions that tick different items from the same stale version both succeed with two revisions; and the same tick in the iOS and Android simulator builds records one revision. *Status:* partial — `tests/worker/description-history.test.ts` — description history and checklists preserve native and scoped authority through revision, restore and concurrent edits; `tests/e2e/comment-checklist-journey.mjs` — commentChecklistJourney, reached by `tests/e2e/core-threads-journey.mjs` in the planning runtime; `tests/markdown-rendering.test.tsx` (browser description and author-only comment ticks, independent stale description and comment ticks, attributed revision and item activity receipts, current-text conflicts and list and board and API counts are implemented; templates, documents, native mobile, live editing, synced Slack and Teams text projection and the complete role and private-team matrix remain open).
- **ISSUE-026 — Editor formatting and slash commands.** Descriptions, comments, templates and documents share one editor that writes GitHub-flavored Markdown: three heading levels, bold, italic, strikethrough, inline code, links, bulleted, numbered and check lists (ISSUE-025), quotes, dividers, tables (insert, then add and remove rows and columns), collapsible sections, and fenced code blocks with a language picker, copy button and syntax highlighting for at least TypeScript, JavaScript, Python, Go, Rust, Java, Kotlin, Swift, Ruby, PHP, C, C++, C#, SQL, shell, YAML, JSON, HTML, CSS and diff. Typing `/` opens a slash menu for every block above, `/file` and `/image` (ISSUE-024), mentions (ISSUE-013) and issue links. Markdown shortcuts (`#`, `-`, `1.`, `>`, `---`, three backticks) and keyboard shortcuts (Command/Ctrl-B, -I, -K for a link, -Shift-7 for a checklist, -Shift-8 for a bulleted list, -Shift-U to upload) apply as typed. Pasting rich text from Google Docs, Notion, Confluence or a web page keeps its headings, lists, links, tables and code as Markdown and drops scripts, styles, forms and remote tracking images, because raw HTML is never rendered (BASE-INPUT-002). Pasting a Vector issue URL makes an issue link, and pasting a GitHub pull request, Figma or Loom URL makes a link that previews under its integration (GITHUB-001, DESIGN-001, DOCS-001). The same Markdown shows the same way in the browser, the mobile apps (MOBILE-001) and exports, and as sanitized text in notification email. *Acceptance:* a browser journey that uses the slash menu to insert a heading, a quote, a divider, a 2×2 table and a TypeScript code block, saves, reloads, and finds each one rendered (the code highlighted, the table a table) with stored Markdown that parses as GitHub-flavored Markdown to the same blocks; pastes an HTML fixture with a heading, a list, a link and a `<script>` and finds the first three as Markdown and no script; and `tests/markdown-rendering.test.tsx` checks GitHub-flavored tables, strikethrough and task lists render, while raw HTML still does not. *Status:* partial — `tests/markdown-rendering.test.tsx`, `src/core/features/markdown/editor/`; `evidence/issue-writing/ISSUE-026/` (the shared browser editor has slash and formatting commands, shortcuts, safe rich paste, tables, collapsible sections, syntax highlighting and code copy; raw HTML is not rendered. Current project-template description, milestone description and default-issue description now use the shared editor, with safe GFM previews and native application. The template journey checks rich-paste tables and highlighted code, rejects active content and tracking images, exercises table row and column editing, and retains controlled drafts across limits, cancellation and stale saves. Document-specific formatting parity proof, standalone ISSUE-016 issue-template adoption, file and image commands with the upload shortcut, provider previews, native mobile editing and remaining cross-surface rendering, export and notification mail duties remain open; template and document checklist mutation remains held under ISSUE-025, and PROJ-012 cadence and issue-template dependencies are unchanged).
- **ISSUE-027 — Issue references and backlinks.** Typing an issue identifier such as `VEC-123` in a description, comment, project update or document (PROJ-013) links it when it names an issue the reader can see, with its title and status on hover; an identifier that names no visible issue stays plain text. When a subject first references an issue, the referenced issue gets one activity entry, "Mentioned in VEC-456" (or in the project, update or document), linking back, and later edits of the same subject add none; removing the reference keeps the entry and marks it removed. A reference from an issue in a private team (ROLE-002) shows in the referenced issue's activity only to people who can see the referencing issue.
