Old identifiers kept by DATA-018 resolve the same way, and the mobile apps (MOBILE-001) link them too. *Acceptance:* a journey that writes "see VEC-123" in VEC-456's description and in a comment, finds both rendered as links with VEC-123's title on hover, finds exactly one "Mentioned in VEC-456" entry on VEC-123 after editing the description again, finds an unknown identifier left as text, and finds a reference from a private-team issue absent from a non-member's view of VEC-123. *Status:* partial — `tests/worker/description-history.test.ts` (referenceScenario through the description/history registration), `tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts` (ROLE-003 invited-team visibility), `tests/e2e/collaboration-references.mjs` (exerciseReferences), `tests/worker/data-http.test.ts` (portable restore), `tests/e2e/data-roundtrip.mjs`. *Gap:* Project updates (PROJ-010), documents (PROJ-013), mobile links (MOBILE-001), DATA-018 aliases and historical/provider backlink backfill remain. `tests/e2e/collaboration-references.mjs` (referenceAccessCells at 1440/390 and HTTP) exercises private-source backlinks for Member/Viewer before membership, after addition and after withdrawal. Additional implementation evidence: `tests/worker/project-report.test.ts` (documentReferenceScenario), `tests/e2e/project-documents-journey.mjs` (documentReferenceAccessJourney), and `migrations/core/0112_document_issue_references.sql` cover document-body links and retained backlinks with current source and target visibility, repeated edits, removal and Member/Viewer access withdrawal. Main’s remaining-gap judgment is retained pending independent re-judgment; document-comment producers remain open.
- **ISSUE-028 — Drafts across devices.** A new-issue draft and an unsent comment are saved to the member's account as they type and are offered on their other browsers and in the desktop and mobile apps (MOBILE-001, MOBILE-005); a Drafts list shows each open draft with its team and last edit, opens it and discards it. A draft stays private to its author — in no list, search result, notification or API response for anyone else — and is removed on every device once it is submitted or discarded on one; when two devices edit one draft, the later save wins and the other device shows the newer text before its next save. Suspension keeps a member's drafts hidden and erasure deletes them (ROLE-011, ROLE-012). ISSUE-015's in-browser restore still applies while a device is offline. *Acceptance:* a journey that types a new-issue draft with a title and two labels in one browser, opens new issue in a second browser signed in as the same member and finds the draft, submits it there and finds it gone from the first browser's Drafts list; plus a second member whose search and `/api/v1` never return the draft. *Status:* missing.

### Custom fields

- **FIELD-001 — Custom field definitions.** Owners and Admins define workspace custom fields, and a team's owners — or all its members while its *Who manages team settings* permission is All team members (ROLE-009) — define fields for that team (role matrix, ROLE-001), each with a name unique in its scope, an optional description and one type: text, number (with optional unit and decimals), single-select, multi-select (options with colors, reorderable, archivable), date, person (a workspace member), URL or checkbox. A field applies to issues, projects or both, can be required for chosen issue types (ISSUE-021) or templates, and can be restricted to chosen teams. An issue or project shows its applicable fields in the detail pane; values are validated by type on every door (browser, `/api/v1`, import, Slack forms, automation), and every change is an activity entry (ISSUE-007) with the previous and new value. Renaming a field or option changes no stored value; archiving an option keeps it on issues that have it but removes it from pickers; deleting a field (Owners and Admins only) removes its values after a confirmation that counts affected issues and is recorded in the audit log. PROJ-001 inherits project custom-field definitions, typed values, validation on every door and per-value project activity before project fields ship. *Acceptance:* a journey that creates a team single-select field "Severity" with three options, sets it on an issue through the browser and through `/api/v1`, finds a number value refused 400 for it, sees one activity entry per change, renames an option and finds the stored value unchanged, and a Member who does not own the team is refused 403 creating a team field under the default permission. *Status:* partial — `src/core/features/issues/repository.ts`, `src/core/features/issues/input.ts` (issues store an untyped key/value custom-field map and its changes reach activity through `src/core/features/issue-edit/plan.ts`; typed definitions, scopes, project fields, validation and the role rule are absent).
- **FIELD-002 — Custom fields in views, search, API and imports.** Every custom field is available as a list column, a board card property (VIEW-008), a filter with type-appropriate operators (text contains, number ranges, select is / is not / any of, date ranges, person is me, checkbox), a group-by for single-select, person and checkbox fields, and a sort; it is searchable (SEARCH-004), included in exports (DATA-001, REPORT-005), webhooks (WEBHOOK-002) and the API (API-003) with its definition, and usable in automation conditions and actions (AUTO-002). Imports map source fields onto custom fields with an explicit mapping and create missing select options only after review: GitHub Projects single-select, iteration, number, text and date fields (DATA-012), Jira custom fields of select, multi-select, number, text, date, user and URL types (DATA-013), and Linear custom properties where present (DATA-014); unmappable types and values are reported as omissions (DATA-007). PROJ-001 inherits project custom-field columns, filters, search, API, exports and reviewed import mapping before those project capabilities ship. *Acceptance:* a journey that filters and groups a list by the Severity field of FIELD-001, saves it as a view and finds the expected issues, and a GitHub API fixture whose Projects single-select "Priority" and number "Points" fields import into a single-select and a number custom field with every value matched in the reconciliation report. *Status:* missing.

### Target: Relations

- **REL-007 — Related issues.** Members can mark two issues as related across teams, with no blocking effect; the link shows on both issues, survives team moves, imports from Jira "Relates" and Linear "related" links when both issues are in the same migration (DATA-017), and is removed when either issue is deleted. *Acceptance:* a journey that relates two issues, sees both sides, and a Jira import fixture whose in-run Relates link becomes a related link. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/related-issues.test.ts`, `tests/worker/jira-apply.test.ts`, `tests/worker/linear-api-import.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/core-related-journey.mjs`.

### Target: Intake and triage

- **TRIAGE-001 — Triage responsibility.** Each team names who triages (set under its *Who manages team settings* permission, ROLE-009): a fixed person, a weekly rotation, or the current on-call person from Oncall or PagerDuty. The responsible person is notified of each new triage item (NOTIFY-007), sees a triage count in navigation, and hands over at rotation time with untouched items listed. Inherited from NOTIFY-001: a new triage item for the responsible person notifies once in the inbox, never the actor; the receiving journey verifies this before the event ships. *Acceptance:* a journey that sets a two-person weekly rotation, advances the workspace clock past the handover and sees new items notify the second person. Inherited from NOTIFY-001: a new triage item for the responsible person notifies once in the inbox, never the actor; the receiving journey verifies this before the event ships. *Status:* partial — native weekly rotation and once-only Inbox intake; see the Status evidence and remaining Gap.
- **TRIAGE-002 — Triage routing rules.** Owners and Admins define workspace-wide routing rules, and a team's rules are defined by its team owners or, while its *Who manages team settings* permission is All team members (ROLE-009), by all its members; Guests never do (role matrix). Rules are ordered, read intake properties (source, label, customer tier, text match, form field) and set team, labels, priority, assignee or template, or send straight to Backlog for trusted sources. Every routed item records which rule acted; a rule never declines or deletes. *Acceptance:* a journey with two rules that routes a Sentry item and a Zendesk item to different teams and shows the acting rule on each, and a non-owner Member's team rule refused 403 under the default permission. *Status:* partial — `src/core/features/issues/mutations.ts` (manual acceptance chooses team, status and assignee; rules are absent).
- **TRIAGE-003 — Agent triage suggestions.** When an item enters triage, a triage agent proposes likely duplicates and related issues (by meaning, not only words), team, assignee, labels and priority, each with a one-line reason and the records it compared. The triager accepts, edits or dismisses each suggestion; accept-all is a single explicit action. Acceptance and dismissal rates are reported per team so the policy can be tuned. Agents never decline, merge or close by themselves. Inherited from INTAKE-004: an agent assertion names the model, the records it read and when, is shown as a suggestion until accepted, and the triage journey shows its provenance and applies it only on acceptance. Inherited from ROLE-001: Owners and Admins, and Members in the member's teams, accept, edit or dismiss an agent's suggestion; Guests are refused (role matrix). *Acceptance:* a journey with a seeded near-duplicate that the agent proposes with reason and evidence, the triager accepts the duplicate decision, and the rate report counts it; the model call is faked only at the provider edge. Inherited from INTAKE-004: an agent assertion names the model, the records it read and when, is shown as a suggestion until accepted, and the triage journey shows its provenance and applies it only on acceptance. *Status:* missing.
- **TRIAGE-004 — Request forms.** Owners, Admins and a team's owners — or all its members while its *Who manages team settings* permission is All team members (ROLE-009) — publish structured request forms for that team (bug report, feature request, access request) with required fields, help text and a template (ISSUE-016). Forms are available in the app, in Slack and Teams (SLACK-002, TEAMS-001) and through `/api/v1`; anyone who can create issues in the team, including a Guest in an invited team, can submit one, and each submission becomes a triage item with its answers as structured fields. A form can include a Customer field, prefilled for a submitter whose email domain maps to a customer (CUST-006), and the submission then carries that customer's request (CUST-008). *Acceptance:* a journey that builds a bug form with a required severity field, submits it from the app and from a Slack shortcut fixture, and finds two triage items with the answers, and a Guest's form edit refused 403 while the same Guest's submission is admitted. *Status:* partial — `src/core/features/operator-intake/input.ts` (one fixed internal intake form; configurable forms are absent).
- **TRIAGE-005 — Email intake.** Each team can enable a private intake address (under its *Who manages team settings* permission, ROLE-009); mail forwarded from Gmail, Outlook or a shared inbox becomes a triage item with the sender, subject, body, inline images and attachments (within BASELINE file limits), threading later replies as comments and refusing senders outside an allow-list. Mail is received through Cloudflare Email Routing or a SendGrid Inbound Parse webhook. The sender needs no seat: they are recorded as the item's requester (and as a contact of the customer their domain maps to under CUST-006, which records the mail as that customer's request) and get no sign-in. A team member replies to them by posting a comment marked "Reply by email" (role matrix): Vector sends it once from the team's intake address through the declared transactional-email external (SendGrid), to the sender and the addresses the original mail copied, with `In-Reply-To` and `References` naming the original message so it lands in the sender's existing mail thread, embedded images attached to the mail rather than linked, and shows the delivery outcome (accepted or bounced) on the comment; the sender's answer threads back as a reply in that comment's thread, so the conversation continues over email. Internal comments, other fields and status never leave Vector, and an optional team setting sends the sender one acknowledgment with the identifier. *Acceptance:* an inbound-mail fixture at the provider edge that creates one item with two attachments and threads a reply as a comment; plus a member's "Reply by email" comment that makes exactly one SendGrid Mail Send request to the sender carrying the original `Message-ID` in `In-Reply-To`, an internal comment that makes none, the sender's answer to that mail landing in the same comment thread, and a Guest's "Reply by email" refused 403. *Status:* partial — `tests/worker/collaboration-mail.test.ts`, `tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/integration-settings-journey.mjs`.

### Customer requests

- **CUST-001 — Customer requests on issues.** An issue's Customers section lists each customer (CUST-005) who asked for it, with every request: its source and link — a manual request (CUST-008), a Penny request, a HappyToHelp conversation, a Zendesk, Intercom or Front ticket, a Salesforce or HubSpot opportunity, an Orbit account, a Gong call excerpt, a Slack message (SLACK-002), an intake email (TRIAGE-005) or a Spool error — the requester, and the requester's own words, with the customer's tier, status and annual revenue in USD from the customer record, whether entered by hand or synced from a linked source. Each request is attached to a customer by the identity rule of CUST-006, and one customer is counted once per issue however many requests or requesters link it. Requests move with duplicates (REL-003) and survive merges of issues and of customers (CUST-006). Owners, Admins and Members see customers and requests; Guests see neither (role matrix). *Acceptance:* a journey that creates the customer Acme by hand with the domain `acme.example`, $120,000 annual revenue and the Enterprise tier, adds a manual request to an issue, then delivers a Penny request from `jane@acme.example` and a HappyToHelp conversation from `bob@acme.example` to the same issue through signed fixtures, and finds one customer, Acme, with three requests from three sources and the USD revenue; a fourth request from a `gmail.com` address adds a requester with no company customer; and a Guest's read of the issue's customers is refused 403. *Status:* missing.
- **CUST-002 — Customer-weighted planning.** Issue lists, projects and the roadmap can filter, sort and group by requesting-customer count, total revenue of the requesting customers (CUST-005, each customer counted once per issue or project), customer tier and newest request, and show these as columns; a saved view such as "Most-requested bugs from Enterprise" is shareable. *Acceptance:* a view journey that sorts three issues by their requesting customers' revenue, entered by hand on the customer records, and matches the expected order, with one customer's two requests on an issue counted once. *Status:* missing.
- **CUST-003 — Close the loop with requesters.** When an issue with customer requests completes, ships in a release (RELEASE-001) or is verified (PUSH-004), Vector notifies each linked source: it marks Penny requests shipped with the release, adds an internal note to the HappyToHelp, Zendesk, Intercom or Front ticket, and offers the support owner a drafted customer reply (PUSH-005). Nothing is sent to a customer from Vector without a person approving it. *Acceptance:* a journey that completes an issue with a Penny request and a Zendesk ticket and finds the Penny state change and the Zendesk internal note in the signed outbound fixtures, with no customer-visible message. *Status:* missing.
- **CUST-004 — Penny keeps the board.** Vector never hosts a public feedback board, voting portal, public roadmap or public changelog; it links to Penny for those, and a Penny request linked to an issue keeps Penny as its source of truth for the customer's text, votes and status. *Acceptance:* the public route inventory in `seed.json` lists no public feedback, voting or roadmap route; a Penny link shows Penny's state. *Status:* done — `tests/e2e/core-runtime.mjs` (the public surface is the homepage, sign-in and declared auth routes, and unknown public paths answer 404 in the RENDER-404 check; the Penny link itself is CUST-001).
- **CUST-005 — Customer records.** A customer is a record of the workspace with a name, one or more domains (each belonging to at most one customer), a logo (uploaded, or taken from a linked source), an owner who is a workspace member, a status and a tier chosen from ordered, colored select values the workspace defines (defaults: status Active, Trial and Churned; tier Enterprise, Growth and Starter), a size (employee count), annual revenue in whole US dollars, the external account IDs of linked sources (CUST-006), contacts (name and email, gathered from requests or added by hand), and values for workspace custom fields that apply to customers (FIELD-001). Owners, Admins and Members create a customer by hand — from the command palette's Create new customer, the Customers list (CUST-007) or inline while adding a request (CUST-008) — and edit its fields; Owners and Admins define the status and tier values and merge or delete customers (role matrix). An Owner or Admin can map chosen fields — owner, revenue, size, status and tier — to fields of a linked Salesforce, HubSpot, Orbit, Zendesk, Intercom, Front or HappyToHelp account; a mapped field shows "Synced from" its source with the last sync time, is read-only in Vector while mapped, and returns to manual entry, keeping its last value, when the mapping is removed. Every change is a customer activity entry. Customers and their requests are in the portable export (DATA-001) and restore exactly (DATA-005), are served by `/api/v1` (API-003) and webhooks (WEBHOOK-002), and Linear customers with their domains, owners, statuses, tiers, sizes, revenue and requests import through DATA-014. A customer record is internal and never sent to the customer. *Acceptance:* a journey that creates Acme from the palette with the domains `acme.example` and `acmecorp.example`, a logo, an owner, the Enterprise tier, the Active status, size 250 and $120,000 revenue, edits the revenue and finds one activity entry, and finds a second customer claiming `acme.example` refused 409; maps revenue and tier to a HubSpot company fixture and finds both shown as synced and refused for manual edit, then removes the mapping and edits revenue by hand; a Member's change to the tier values refused 403; an export and restore that brings back Acme with its fields and requests; and a Linear GraphQL fixture whose customers and requests import with their domains, revenue and tier. *Status:* missing.
- **CUST-006 — Customer identity and merging.** Every request from a linked source, Slack (SLACK-002), email intake (TRIAGE-005) or a request form (TRIAGE-004) is attached to a customer by one rule, in this order: (1) the source's account ID where the source has one — an Orbit account, Salesforce Account, HubSpot company, Zendesk organization, Intercom company, Front account or HappyToHelp organization ID — matching an external ID stored on a customer; (2) otherwise the requester's email domain, lower-cased, matching one of a customer's domains exactly or as a subdomain of it (`eu.acme.example` matches `acme.example`), with a Slack author's email read through `users.info` under the `users:read.email` scope; (3) otherwise, while the workspace setting Create customers from new company domains is on (the default), a new customer named after the domain, holding that domain and the source's account ID. Free-mail and consumer domains never match or create a customer — a maintained list covering at least gmail.com, googlemail.com, outlook.com, hotmail.com, live.com, msn.com, yahoo.com, icloud.com, me.com, aol.com, proton.me, protonmail.com, gmx.com and zoho.com — and neither does any domain on the Admin's block list (for example the workspace's own domain or a reseller's); such a request keeps its requester as a contact under No customer, and a member can assign it to a customer by hand. A request whose rule-1 ID and rule-2 domain point at different customers attaches by the ID and is flagged for review. Owners and Admins merge two customers in one action after a counts preview: every request, domain, external ID, contact and favorite moves to the surviving customer, the actor chooses each conflicting field's value, every issue and project still counts the survivor once (CUST-001), the merged customer's page and API ID redirect to the survivor, and one audit entry records it. *Policy: `customer.identity.v1`; default: as stated, with new-domain creation on.* Inherited from ROLE-001: only Owners and Admins define customer status and tier values, map customer fields, set the domain block list and new-domain setting, and merge or delete customers (role matrix). *Acceptance:* a fixture suite through the signed source routes that attaches a Zendesk ticket with organization ID `org-9` to the customer holding that external ID although the requester writes from `acmecorp.example`; attaches `jane@acme.example` and `pat@eu.acme.example` to the customer with the domain `acme.example`; creates `globex.example` as a new customer with the setting on and leaves it under No customer with the setting off; gives a `gmail.com` address and an address on a blocked domain no customer; and flags a request whose ID and domain disagree; plus a journey that merges Acme Inc (two requests on issue A) into Acme (one request on issue A and one on project P), finds issue A with three requests counted as one customer, the request on P kept, the old customer page redirecting and one audit entry, and a Member's merge refused 403. *Status:* missing.
- **CUST-007 — Customers list and customer pages.** A Customers list, opened from the sidebar, the palette or the shortcut G then Q, shows every customer with name, logo, domains, owner, status, tier, size, revenue, request count, open-issue count and newest request, and sorts and filters by revenue, tier, status, owner, size, request count and newest request, with the same save, share and display model as issue views (VIEW-002, VIEW-009). Customers can be favorited (VIEW-010), opened from the palette (CMD-001) and found by name and domain in search (SEARCH-001). A customer page shows its fields (CUST-005), contacts and activity, every request with the requester's words, source, date and linked issue or project, and the issues and projects it asked for grouped by status, with totals; for a request on an issue in a private team (ROLE-002), a non-member sees only that a request exists, never the issue's title. The list and the page have a 390px layout and appear in the mobile apps (MOBILE-001). *Acceptance:* a browser journey that opens the list with G then Q, sorts three customers by revenue and finds the expected order, filters by the Enterprise tier, saves the view and opens it from the palette, opens Acme's page and finds its three requests with their issue and project and the requester's words, favorites Acme, and passes a 390px check of the list and the page; plus a member outside a private team who sees Acme's private-team request as a count only. *Status:* missing.
- **CUST-008 — Adding customer requests by hand.** Owners, Admins and Members add a request to an issue or a project from its Customers section, from a customer page, from the palette (Add customer request) or with the shortcut Control-R (Alt-Shift-R on Windows and Linux): they pick a customer or create one inline (CUST-005), paste or write the requester's words (required, Markdown), and may name a contact by name and email and add a source link (a call recording, a Gong or Zoom link, a document). The request is attributed to the member who added it, is an activity entry on the issue or project and on the customer, can be edited by its author, moved to another issue or project, and deleted by its author or an Owner or Admin (role matrix). A project's Customers section lists its own requests and its issues' requests, each customer counted once. When a member files an issue from a Slack message (SLACK-002), the dialog offers a Customer picker prefilled by CUST-006 from the message author's email domain, and the message becomes that customer's request in the author's words; a request form (TRIAGE-004) can include a Customer field; and an intake email (TRIAGE-005) becomes a request of the customer its sender's domain maps to. Manual requests count everywhere CUST-001 and CUST-002 do. *Acceptance:* a journey that adds a manual request for Acme to an issue with Control-R and to a project from the project's Customers section, creates Initech inline while adding a second request, moves one request to another issue, and finds each on the customer page and in the issue's and project's activity; plus a Slack Events and interactivity fixture that files an issue from a message by `bob@acme.example` with the Customer picker prefilled to Acme and finds the request on Acme with the message as its words, and a Guest's request refused 403. *Status:* missing.

### Target: My work

- **WORK-004 — Created, Subscribed and Activity tabs.** Beside the assigned queue of WORK-001, My work has three tabs: Created (every issue the member created, newest first), Subscribed (every issue the member follows, NOTIFY-001, with an Unfollow action on each row) and Activity (the member's own recent changes and comments, newest first, each linking to its issue). Created and Subscribed take the same filters and display options as issue views and can be saved as views (VIEW-002); every tab applies OPS-001 and ROLE-002, has a 390px layout and appears in the mobile apps (MOBILE-001). ROLE-002 inherits private-team narrowing and its lost-access acceptance cell before private teams ship; MOBILE-001 inherits the mobile-app tabs journey. No private teams exist today. *Acceptance:* a journey in which a member creates two issues, follows a third created by someone else and comments on a fourth, then finds the two in Created, the third in Subscribed and gone after Unfollow from its row, and the comment first in Activity; ROLE-002 owns the inherited lost-private-team-access check in every tab. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/my-work-tabs.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/core-my-work-tabs-journey.mjs`; `evidence/my-work-tabs/WORK-004/` (Created, Subscribed with a row Unfollow, and Activity are implemented at desktop and 390px, Created and Subscribed take the issue-view filters, display options and Save view, and every tab reads only the member's workspace; ROLE-002 inherits private-team narrowing and MOBILE-001 the mobile-app tabs journey).

### Target: Projects, initiatives and roadmaps

- **PROJ-010 — Project updates.** The project's lead and members (PROJ-014), Owners and Admins post dated updates (Guests cannot, role matrix) with a status of On track, At risk or Off track and a Markdown body; Vector prefills the draft with computed health (PROJ-005), progress since the last update, completed and newly added scope, and slipped milestones. Updates appear in the project, roll up into initiatives (INIT-002), notify project followers, and post to the project's Slack or Teams channel (SLACK-003). Leads are reminded on the project's cadence (AUTO-005). Computed health and the lead's stated status are shown side by side, never merged. Inherited from NOTIFY-001: a followed project or initiative update notifies once in the inbox, never the actor; the receiving journey verifies this before the event ships. Inherited from SLACK-003: each project update posts to its bound Slack or Discord channel once, using the shared durable delivery and minimal-message rules; its acceptance journey verifies the bound post. *Acceptance:* a journey that posts an At risk update, finds it in the project, the initiative roll-up and a Slack fixture, and a follower notification. Inherited from NOTIFY-001: a followed project or initiative update notifies once in the inbox, never the actor; the receiving journey verifies this before the event ships. Inherited from SLACK-003: each project update posts to its bound Slack or Discord channel once, using the shared durable delivery and minimal-message rules; its acceptance journey verifies the bound post. *Status:* missing.
- **PROJ-011 — Milestones.** A project has ordered milestones with names, optional target dates and descriptions; issues belong to at most one milestone of their own project. Members create, edit, reorder, complete, reopen and delete milestones on the project page. A milestone shows count-based progress by PROJ-004 (Canceled and Duplicate excluded). It slips when its target date is before today in the workspace timezone and it has open countable issues, including when the milestone itself was marked complete. Changing an issue's milestone records one activity entry under ISSUE-007; leaving its project clears the milestone in that same issue change. Copy, persisted Undo, bulk project changes, deletion preimages and native archive restore carry the assignment; deleting a milestone keeps its issues and clears their assignment. Milestones hold no person data, so erasure is unchanged. DATA-013 and DATA-014 inherit the sentence "Jira fix versions and Linear milestones import to milestones or releases (RELEASE-001) by explicit mapping" in their source importers and acceptance journeys; generic mapped issue imports do not yet offer milestone mapping. VIEW-008 inherits grouping by milestone, API-003 inherits the API field and milestone object coverage, and CAL-001 inherits the feed events. *Acceptance:* `tests/e2e/admin-project-cycle-journey.mjs` creates two milestones, edits and reorders them, assigns issues, completes one, sees the other flagged past its date, moves an issue to another project and sees its milestone clear, then deletes a milestone without deleting its issues, at 1440 and 390. `tests/worker/project-fields.test.ts`, `tests/worker/issue-copy.test.ts` and `tests/worker/data-http.test.ts` cover progress, assignment activity, bulk/Undo, copy and exact archive restore. *Status:* done — `src/core/features/projects/milestones.ts`, `src/core/features/administration/project-milestones.ts`, `src/core/features/projects/ui/project-milestones.tsx`, `evidence/milestones/PROJ-011/`.
- **PROJ-012 — Project templates.** Owners and Admins save a project as a workspace template, and a team's own project templates are saved by its team owners or, while its *Who manages cycles and templates* permission is All team members (ROLE-009), by all its members; Guests never do (role matrix). A template holds description, milestones, labels, default issues and update cadence, and anyone who can create projects creates new projects from it with relative dates. *Acceptance:* a journey that creates a launch project from a template with two milestones and four issues offset from a chosen start date, and a Member's workspace project template refused 403. *Status:* partial — `tests/worker/project-fields.test.ts` (project planning and ordered milestones; exerciseProjectTemplates), `tests/worker/data-http.test.ts` (portable template structure), `tests/worker/project-fields-upgrade.test.ts` (populated upgrade), `tests/e2e/admin-project-cycle-journey.mjs` (`tests/e2e/project-templates-journey.mjs`: projectTemplatesJourney and nativeProjectTemplatesJourney at 1440 and 390; configured and zero-membership native Owners use native issue-team choices, and created projects retain milestones/issues after template deletion). Gap: Update cadence is stored and shown; its effect awaits PROJ-010/AUTO-005. Issue templates await ISSUE-016. `tests/e2e/admin-project-cycle-journey.mjs` (projectTemplateAccessCells) exercises current private-template visibility and Viewer creation/edit/application refusals at 1440/390 and HTTP. Default ADOPTED: team templates are visible only to their members; deletion preserves created projects. Member applications skip unavailable project labels and report their names. Application uses native services in order; an interrupted creation names the partial project for inspection before retry.
- **PROJ-013 — Project documents and specs.** Projects, issues, initiatives (INIT-001) and teams can hold Markdown documents (specs, PRDs, decision records, team handbooks and runbooks) written in the ISSUE-026 editor, with inline images and video (ISSUE-024), checklists (ISSUE-025), revision history, mentions, comments, attachments and search; a spec document can be linked as an issue's specification (PUSH-001). A team's documents are listed on the team's page and an initiative's on the initiative; a document under a private team (ROLE-002) is visible only to its members. Documents export with their project, issue, initiative or team. Inherited from ROLE-001: Owners, Admins and Members write, edit and comment on documents under what they can see, and Guests only under invited teams' issues and projects (role matrix); this clause's journey checks those cells. Inherited from SLACK-005: a Vector document link pasted in Slack unfurls with the document's identifier and title (a document has no status, assignee or priority) only for linked members allowed to see that document; others, including a linked member who cannot see a private team's document, see the link alone. *Acceptance:* a journey that writes a spec under a project, links it to an issue, edits it twice and finds both revisions and a search hit; plus one document under an initiative and one under a team, each listed there and found in search, and the team document refused 404 to a non-member once the team is private. plus, inherited from SLACK-005, two signed unfurl fixtures through the Slack events door for one document under a private team: a linked member who can read it receives exactly one preview with its identifier and title and no body, and a linked non-member whose native read of that same document is refused receives none, with storage unchanged apart from the identifier-only receipt. *Status:* partial.
- **PROJ-014 — Project members and teams.** A project lists its lead, members and contributing teams; members follow it, appear in its filters, and receive its updates. Projects can span teams, and a team's project list shows every project with that team's issues. PROJ-001 inherits choosing and changing a primary team, defaulting to the lead's first contributing team, with explicit same-category status mapping under PROJ-016 before primary teams ship. Inherited from ROLE-007: a project's contributing teams follow team membership; Owners, Admins and Members edit its members and contributing teams, Guests never (role matrix). *Acceptance:* a journey that adds two teams' issues to a project and finds the project in both teams' lists with both teams as contributors. *Status:* partial — `tests/worker/project-membership.test.ts` (spanning projects appear in each contributing team list without exposing a private contribution after access is withdrawn) and `tests/e2e/core-project-membership-journey.mjs` (contributingTeamsJourney: both actual team project lists, contributor names and current private-team access). Derived contributors and existing project subscriptions are available; member management, editable contributing-team membership, member filters and project-update recipients remain absent.
- **PROJ-015 — Project dependencies.** A project can be marked *blocked by* or *blocking* another project directly, from the project page, the projects list, the roadmap timeline (INIT-003) and `/api/v1`, independently of issue dependencies; a project cannot block itself and a dependency that would close a cycle is refused. The timeline draws direct dependencies as solid connectors and those derived from issue dependencies (an issue in one project blocks an issue in another) as dashed ones, and flags both projects when the blocked project starts before the blocking project's target date. Each change is a project activity entry and notifies both leads (NOTIFY-001); Linear project dependencies import onto it (DATA-014). Inherited from ROLE-001: Owners, Admins and Members set or remove project dependencies; Guests are refused (role matrix). *Acceptance:* a journey that sets project B blocked by project A in the browser and through `/api/v1`, finds the reverse dependency refused 400 as a cycle, sees one solid connector and one dashed derived connector on the timeline, sees the date-conflict flag after moving B's start before A's target, and finds one activity entry on each project. *Status:* missing.
- **PROJ-016 — Project statuses.** Project statuses are named, ordered, colored values, each with an optional description, inside six fixed categories in this order: Backlog, Planned, Started, Paused, Completed and Canceled. The workspace set's defaults are Backlog, Planned, In Progress (Started), Paused, Completed and Canceled; an installation upgraded from the fixed four-value lifecycle names its Started default Active and maps every existing project onto the default of its category, so no project's displayed status changes. Owners and Admins add, rename, recolor, describe and reorder workspace statuses within a category (for example Idea and Discovery in Backlog); a status never changes category, and every category keeps at least one status. A team can instead use its own project status set, managed under its *Who manages team settings* permission (ROLE-009); a project uses the set of its primary team (chosen on the project, defaulting to the lead's first contributing team, PROJ-014) or the workspace set when that team has none, and changing the primary team to one with another set maps the project's status, in an explicit step, to the first status of the same category. Removing a status requires a destination status in the same category and atomically moves every project to it, as TEAM-004 does for issue statuses, recording the mapping in the audit log. Categories drive behavior everywhere: health is computed only for Started projects (PROJ-005); a Paused project shows as paused, with no health, no slip and no update reminder (AUTO-005), and keeps its dates; portfolio counts and filters (PROJ-007) offer both status and category; the roadmap timeline styles bars by category and draws Paused bars hatched with a pause marker (INIT-003); and initiative roll-ups and leadership digests count projects by category and never report a Paused project as slipping (INIT-002, INIT-004). Linear project statuses, with their names, colors and types (`backlog`, `planned`, `started`, `paused`, `completed`, `canceled`), import onto statuses of the matching category through DATA-014 after an explicit mapping step that proposes matches by name and creates the missing statuses, and Jira epics imported as projects map by status category (DATA-013). Where a clause says a project's *lifecycle*, it means its status. PROJ-001 inherits configurable project statuses, the default Planned status, workspace/team status sets and explicit same-category mapping when a primary team changes before configurable statuses ship. *Acceptance:* a browser and HTTP journey that adds an Idea status in Backlog, renames and recolors it, and finds a Member's attempt refused 403; moves an overdue Started project to the default Paused status and finds no At risk health, no slip in the initiative roll-up, no update reminder in the next scheduled run, and the paused style on the timeline; filters the portfolio by the Paused category and by the Idea status with matching counts; removes Idea with Backlog as the destination and finds its project moved and one audit entry, and finds removing the last Paused status refused 409; gives a team its own set and finds a project with that primary team offered only that set; a Linear GraphQL fixture with Backlog, Paused and a custom Started status named Beta that imports each project to the mapped status with the reconciliation counts matching; and an upgrade test that migrates a populated installation's four-value lifecycles with every project keeping its displayed status. *Status:* partial — `src/core/features/administration/schema.ts`, `tests/e2e/admin-project-cycle-journey.mjs` (a fixed lifecycle of Planned, Active, Completed and Canceled set by hand; named, ordered and colored statuses, the Backlog and Paused categories, team sets, removal with a destination and status import are absent).
- **PROJ-017 — Recoverable project deletion.** Mirroring ISSUE-020, a confirmed project deletion removes it from active lists, search and roadmaps and places it in Recently deleted projects for 30 days with actor and time; the deleting session gets Undo for 10 seconds, including Command/Ctrl-Z outside editable controls. Restore through Undo or Recently deleted keeps the same project ID, fields, milestones, updates, documents, relations and history, and reattaches surviving issues only where their membership has not since been replaced; issues are never deleted (PROJ-003). Restore is version-checked, refuses stale Undo without writes and records one restore activity entry. Owners/Admins delete or restore any project; after this recoverable flow ships the project's Member lead may delete it and restore their own deletion. Until then deletion remains Owner/Admin only because it is irreversible. Guests and Viewers never delete or restore. Only Owners/Admins delete permanently before retention ends; the scheduled purge completes a person-started deletion once after retention, never deletes its issues, and leaves an opaque audit reference. Owners alone configure retention from 7 to 90 days, default 30, as for ISSUE-020. *Acceptance:* A browser journey at 1440 and 390 plus HTTP coverage that deletes a project with two issues, milestones and an update, restores it with Undo within 10 seconds and from Recently deleted after reload, and finds the same project ID, retained content and eligible issue memberships with one restore activity entry. Reassign one issue while the project is deleted and prove restore does not overwrite its new project. Refuse Member-lead deletion until recovery ships, then allow the lead to delete and restore their deletion while refusing another Member and all Guests/Viewers; refuse a Member's permanent delete. After 30 workspace days run purge twice and find one final deletion, 404 for the project and both issues intact; stale or expired Undo changes nothing. *Status:* missing.
- **INIT-001 — Initiatives.** An initiative groups projects (and sub-initiatives up to three levels) under an owner, description, target date and status (Planned, Active, Completed), across teams. A project can belong to several initiatives. Owners, Admins and Members create and edit initiatives and add projects to them; Guests cannot (role matrix). Inherited from SLACK-003: initiatives bind chosen events to a Slack or Discord channel and each event posts to its bound channel once, using the shared durable delivery and minimal-message rules; its acceptance journey verifies the binding and bound post. *Acceptance:* a journey that creates an initiative with a sub-initiative and three projects from two teams and sees them nested, and a Guest's initiative creation refused 403. Inherited from SLACK-003: initiatives bind chosen events to a Slack or Discord channel and each event posts to its bound channel once, using the shared durable delivery and minimal-message rules; its acceptance journey verifies the binding and bound post. *Status:* partial — `tests/worker/project-membership.test.ts` (exerciseInitiatives: persisted Planned readback and retained hierarchy, roles and API checks), `tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts` (every role-matrix row with a door), `tests/e2e/initiatives-journey.mjs` (initiativesJourney, 1440 and 390); `tests/worker/channel-notifications-scenario.ts` (initiativeChannels: public create then selected creation binding once at both provider edges, full private notice without actor or private text, chosen versus unselected events, each real provider edge once, replay, private content, actor access, revocation, uncertain and terminal results, restore holds); `tests/e2e/initiatives-journey.mjs` (initiativesJourney: rendered Planned state, Settings binding and bound initiative post, 1440 and 390); `tests/worker/initiatives-scenario.ts` (scopedInitiativeDoors: Members refused at the approval and Settings administration doors, Guests and Viewers refused at every scoped door, only this initiative’s approvals offered, approved Discord binding with chosen events, unapproved, display-name, stale and demoted refusals with no row, withdrawal by another editor); `tests/worker/channel-notifications-scenario.ts` (scopedInitiativeChannels: unapproved identities refused before any provider call, one Slack and one Discord post through the Member’s scoped binding with replay adding none, demoted and removed binders post nothing, a withdrawn approval drops the pending and later events, re-approval replays nothing until the Member binds again, approval and withdrawal audited; changed Slack workspace refuses saving without custody changes, stops pending and future posts, fresh approval replays nothing, and an explicit new binding permits subsequent work); `tests/worker/role-probes.ts` (channels.bind-initiative row through role-matrix.test.ts); `tests/e2e/initiatives-journey.mjs` (initiativesJourney: Settings approval control and the Member’s initiative-page binding with one bound post, 1440 and 390); `tests/e2e/data-integration-history.mjs` (populateIntegrationHistory and verifyIntegrationCategories through `tests/e2e/data-roundtrip.mjs`: an Owner-approved destination and the initiative binding saved through the scoped door retained in the portable archive, the sealed webhook excluded). ct judged af171d2d782a3787e43cd339867cc06dec89677f STAYS PARTIAL on 27 October 2026 under DONE-RIDERS rows 1, 6, 8 and 11; these added assertions address the three behavioral gaps, with fresh judgment and landing gates pending. *Gap:* **INIT-001 unproved:** assertion sensitivity for approval refusal when the connected Slack workspace changes. Candidate landing, exact-SHA Run site and complete clean publication-scan proof remain outstanding. INIT-002 roll-ups remain separately assigned. **CT policy ruling, 27 October 2026 — REJECT WITH ALTERNATIVE for INIT-001-BIND-ROW.** A current Member who may edit an initiative may manage that initiative’s selected events and binding through a scoped initiative capability, using only an existing destination explicitly approved by an Owner or Admin for the intended initiative and permitted audience. Owners and Admins retain integration installation, credentials, channel creation, destination approval and broader Settings administration. Guests and Viewers receive no binding authority. An approved destination is identified by its provider, workspace and immutable channel identity; approval is not inferred from a display name, another unrelated binding or possession of a credential. The scoped capability cannot approve a destination, widen its audience, expose private-team work, alter another subject’s binding or acquire integration administration. Recheck current editor authority, destination approval, binding version, source visibility and channel safety when saving and immediately before posting. Withdrawal stops pending and future posts; fresh authorization never replays held events automatically. INIT-001 retains its expressly named SLACK-003 durable delivery and minimal-message inheritance. Public workspace-visible events may show the readable actor. Private or restricted events use the required generic notice without actor or private text. Every offered selected initiative event requires a usable public flow and once-only delivery proof. This policy accepts no completion; INIT-001 remains partial.
- **INIT-002 — Initiative roll-up.** An initiative shows each project's latest update status, computed health, progress, target date and slip since the last update, and its own owner-written updates on a cadence; a Paused project (PROJ-016) shows as paused with no health and no slip, and the roll-up counts its projects by status category; the initiatives list sorts and filters by these for leadership. *Acceptance:* a journey that posts updates on two projects and sees the initiative roll-up reflect them in order, then pauses one past its target date and finds it shown as paused with no slip. *Status:* missing.
- **INIT-003 — Roadmap timeline.** A roadmap view shows initiatives and projects on a timeline by their start and target dates (PROJ-001) with milestones, dependencies between projects (set directly under PROJ-015, and derived from issue dependencies), today marker and zoom by week, month and quarter. Bars are styled by project status category (PROJ-016): a Paused project's bar is hatched with a pause marker and is never flagged as slipping. Placement is fixed for every combination of dates: a project with both dates is a bar from start to target; one with only a target date is a diamond on its target date; one with only a start date is a bar from its start to today (or to the start date when that is in the future) with an open, faded end; one with neither is listed in an Unscheduled group beside the timeline, never drawn at an invented date. An initiative spans the earliest start to the latest target of its projects, or shows its own target date as a diamond when none of them has dates. Leads drag a bar's body to move both dates, drag either end to change one date, and drag an Unscheduled project onto the timeline to set both; each drag is recorded as a project change. The view is keyboard-operable (arrow keys move a focused bar by one zoom unit) and has a list equivalent at 390px. The timeline inherits VIEW-011: it follows the member's Light, Dark or System theme, bars by status category, the Paused hatch, connectors and the today marker stay distinguishable in both, and its text and marks meet WCAG 2.2 AA contrast in both. PROJ-001 inherits editing project start/target dates, priority and labels on the roadmap, with one activity entry per change, before the roadmap ships. *Acceptance:* a browser journey with four projects — both dates, target only, start only, no dates — that finds a bar, a diamond, an open-ended bar and an Unscheduled entry respectively; drags the target-only project's diamond and the unscheduled project onto the timeline and finds each change in the project's activity; moves a bar by keyboard; and a 390px check of the list equivalent. *Status:* missing.
- **INIT-004 — Leadership digest.** Owners of initiatives and anyone subscribed receive a weekly digest by email, Slack or Teams of initiative and project status, slipped dates, new risks and shipped milestones (a Paused project is reported as paused, never as slipped, PROJ-016), linking back to the source records and respecting ROLE-002. *Acceptance:* a scheduled-run test that sends one digest per subscriber to the mail and Slack fixtures and omits a private team's project from a non-member's digest. *Status:* missing.

### Target: Cycles

- **CYCLE-007 — Automatic cycle schedule.** Owners, Admins and the team's owners set a team's cycle cadence, and so do all its members while its *Who manages cycles and templates* permission is All team members (ROLE-007, ROLE-009; proven by `tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts`). The cadence comprises a duration of one to eight weeks, a start weekday, optional cooldown between cycles and the number of upcoming cycles to keep; Vector creates future cycles on schedule so rollover (CYCLE-004) always has a next cycle, and records each creation as automation. Changing the cadence affects only cycles not yet started. *Acceptance:* a scheduled-run test that keeps three upcoming two-week cycles with a one-week cooldown and closes a cycle without a CYCLE-006 failure. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/cycle-schedule.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/cycle-schedule-runtime.mjs`.
- **CYCLE-008 — Automatic cycle membership.** A team can choose to add issues to the active cycle when they move to Started, and when they complete without a cycle, so the cycle reflects real work; each addition is recorded as an automation change. *Acceptance:* a journey that starts an uncommitted issue and finds it in the active cycle with the automation activity entry. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/cycle-membership.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/core-cycle-membership-journey.mjs`.

### Releases and deployments

- **RELEASE-001 — Releases.** Owners, Admins and Members define team or workspace releases (versions) — a Member a team's releases only for teams they can see, and Guests none (role matrix) — each with name, target date, status (Planned, In progress, Released) and description; issues and projects join a release; a release page shows scope, progress, blockers and linked PRs, and can be shared read-only inside the workspace. Jira fix versions import as releases. Inherited from SLACK-003: a release posts to its bound Slack or Discord channel once, using the shared durable delivery and minimal-message rules; its acceptance journey verifies the bound post. *Acceptance:* a journey that creates release 2.4, adds five issues, marks it Released and sees release state on each issue, and a Guest's release creation refused 403. Inherited from SLACK-003: a release posts to its bound Slack or Discord channel once, using the shared durable delivery and minimal-message rules; its acceptance journey verifies the bound post. *Status:* partial — Team/workspace releases have name, date, status and Markdown description; issues and projects form deduplicated scope with completed/total progress, open dependency blockers and linked PRs. Publishing records one activity per included issue and issue chips show release state. Native and scoped versioned doors use the existing release role row; Guests are refused on pages and every API door. A read-only workspace URL shares the visible scope. Evidence: `tests/worker/project-membership.test.ts` (only Owners and Admins delete a project; removal, archive and deletion preserve its issues; exerciseReleases), `tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts` (every role-matrix row with a door), `tests/worker/integration-api.test.ts` (the versioned schema is published through the real app), `tests/e2e/releases-journey.mjs` (releasesJourney, 1440/390). *Gap:* Slack/Discord release bindings are absent: the existing durable channel model binds issue-bearing team/project/view subjects. Jira fix versions import remains DATA-013. Conservative default: reopen a Released release before changing its scope.
- **RELEASE-002 — Deployment tracking.** Vector receives deployment events from GitHub Deployments, GitLab environments, CircleCI, Vercel, Netlify and Cloudflare Workers/Pages through signed webhooks or the API, and attaches to each issue whose merged commits are included which environments it has reached (for example staging, production) and when. A team rule can move an issue to a chosen status on production deploy. *Acceptance:* a signed GitHub Deployments fixture for a merged commit linked to an issue shows the issue deployed to production and triggers the team rule once on replay. *Status:* partial — Signed GitHub successful deployments attach environment and provider time by exact linked commit or merged-PR SHA, or ancestry from recorded non-forced push events. The team-selected production environment applies its chosen status once per issue/deployment; replay is inert. Evidence: `tests/worker/github-links.test.ts` (signed deliveries preserve links, automate team statuses and notify followers exactly once; exerciseGithubAutomation, exerciseGithubCommits, exerciseGithubDeployments), `tests/e2e/integrations/github-chain.mjs` (exerciseGithubChain, 1440/390). *Gap:* GitLab environments, CircleCI, Vercel, Netlify and Cloudflare Workers/Pages have no receiver or signed fixture. Unrecorded ancestry, missing non-forced push proof and unknown SHAs remain unproven and never attach an issue.
- **RELEASE-003 — Release notes.** For a release or a date range, Vector drafts internal release notes from completed issues grouped by label or project, using each issue's title and an optional "release note" field, which the release owner edits and publishes to the workspace, Slack, or hands to Penny as a changelog draft. Inherited from SLACK-003: publishing release notes posts the release event to its bound Slack or Discord channel once, without duplicating RELEASE-001’s post; its acceptance journey verifies the bound post. *Acceptance:* a journey that drafts notes for a release of six issues, edits one line, and sends the approved text to Slack and Penny fixtures. Inherited from SLACK-003: publishing release notes posts the release event to its bound Slack or Discord channel once, without duplicating RELEASE-001’s post; its acceptance journey verifies the bound post. *Status:* missing.

### Target: Search, views and commands

- **SEARCH-004 — Advanced filters.** Filters combine with AND/OR groups and negation over every issue field, custom field (FIELD-002), relation, customer request (CUST-002), Git state (GITHUB-005), release, milestone and date expressions ("due before next Friday", "updated in the last 7 days"); a typed filter bar accepts the same expressions with autocomplete, and any filter can be saved as a view. Jira JQL in imported saved filters is translated where every term maps and reported otherwise. *Acceptance:* a journey that builds an (A OR B) AND NOT C filter in the bar and the menu, matches the expected issues, and saves it. *Status:* partial — `tests/worker/boolean-filters.test.ts` (Boolean list and saved-view doors retain exact paged results, reject invalid expressions and enforce current reader authority); `tests/e2e/core-boolean-filter-journey.mjs` (booleanFilterJourney: typed and menu priority and blocked expressions, saved reload and current reader visibility).
- **SEARCH-005 — Meaning-based search.** Search also returns issues, documents and comments that match the meaning of a query, labeled as semantic matches and ranked after exact identifier and full-text hits, under the same access rules. *Acceptance:* a search for "checkout fails on Safari" that finds an issue titled "Payment page broken in WebKit" labeled as a semantic match, and does not return a private-team issue to a non-member. *Status:* missing.
- **VIEW-006 — Manual order.** Besides the derived orders, lists and boards offer a Manual order that members set by dragging or keyboard (move up, move down, move to top); the order is shared per team and view, imported from Jira rank and Linear sort order, and never changes Current's derived ranking (CURR-007). *Acceptance:* a journey that reorders three backlog issues by keyboard, reloads in a second session and finds the same order, while Current's order is unchanged. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/manual-order.test.ts`, `tests/worker/manual-order-import.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/core-manual-order-journey.mjs`, `tests/worker/data-http.test.ts`.
- **VIEW-007 — Timeline and calendar views.** Issue lists can be shown as a timeline (by the issue start date (ISSUE-022) and the due date, with dependencies drawn; an issue with only a due date is a diamond, one with only a start date is open-ended to today, and one with neither is listed as Unscheduled beside the timeline) and as a month calendar of due dates, cycles and milestones; dragging changes dates through the version-checked mutation. *Acceptance:* a journey that drags an issue's due date on the calendar and finds the change in its activity, and a timeline with one issue of each date combination placed as stated. *Status:* done — `tests/e2e/calendar-month-journey.mjs` (calendarMonthJourney: due-date drag, activity, keyboard edit, Undo, stale refusal and due issues, cycles, milestones and releases at 1440 and 390); `tests/e2e/timeline-journey.mjs` (timelineJourney: start-to-due interval, due-only diamond, start-only open interval to today, Unscheduled list, dependency lines and version-checked start and due drags with activity, Undo and stale refusal at 1440 and 390); `tests/e2e/calendar-access-cells.mjs` (calendarAccessCells: current private-team exclusion, Viewer read-only calendar and timeline, membership withdrawal and Guest refusal). All run through `tests/e2e/cycle-report-runtime.mjs` in Verify. `tests/worker/base-auth.test.ts` (calendar capability and shared month reader via exerciseCalendarCapability) retains feed authority and event projection proof.
- **VIEW-008 — Display options.** Members choose the properties shown on rows and cards, show sub-issues nested or flat, group lists and add board swimlanes by assignee, project, priority, cycle, label (one label group at a time, ISSUE-014), project milestone (PROJ-011), parent issue (REL-006) or a single-select or person custom field (FIELD-002), and drag cards between columns and lanes (the labeled selector of VIEW-005 remains the keyboard path). Display options save with a view. VIEW-008 inherits PROJ-011's milestone grouping in saved views. *Acceptance:* a journey that adds assignee swimlanes, drags a card across a lane and a column, and finds both changes in activity; plus a list grouped by a label group, by milestone and by parent issue, each with group counts matching the filtered result. VIEW-008 inherits PROJ-011's milestone grouping in saved views. *Status:* partial — `src/core/features/issues/ui/issue-board.tsx` (main’s current judgment: columns by status with a keyboard selector; swimlanes, drag and property choices are absent). Additional list-only evidence: `tests/worker/project-fields.test.ts` (milestone and parent grouping count complete filtered results and saved views obey current reader authority), via `tests/worker/issue-grouping-journey.ts` (issueGroupingJourney); `tests/e2e/core-grouping-journey.mjs` (groupingJourney), reached through `tests/e2e/core-keyboard-journey.mjs` in `tests/e2e/core-runtime.mjs` (milestone and parent grouping, full filtered counts over multiple pages, stable duplicate-name identities, unassigned groups, saved reload and current private-team, Guest and Viewer authority). Row and card property choices, nested versus flat subissues, label-group and custom-field grouping, board swimlanes, cross-column and cross-lane drag with activity, and remaining display-option persistence are still owed; label grouping requires ISSUE-014 and custom-field modes require FIELD-002.
- **VIEW-009 — Workspace-wide views.** Views can span all teams the member can see, and also exist for projects, initiatives, releases, customers (CUST-007) and customer requests with the same filter, sort, group and save model. *Acceptance:* a journey that saves a shared project view filtered by lead and health and a cross-team issue view, and opens both from the palette. *Status:* partial — `src/core/features/views/service.ts` (saved issue views; project, initiative and request views are absent).
- **VIEW-010 — Sidebar favorites.** Members pin issues, projects, initiatives, cycles, releases, documents, customers (CUST-007), saved views and teams to a Favorites section of the sidebar, reorder them by drag or keyboard, group them in named folders, and see each favorite's unread or count badge; favorites are per member, sync across browsers and the mobile apps (MOBILE-001), and a favorite whose subject is deleted or becomes invisible (ROLE-002) disappears without revealing its title. *Acceptance:* a journey that favorites an issue, a project and a view, moves the view into a folder, reloads in a second browser and finds the same order, then loses access to the project's private team and finds that favorite gone. *Status:* missing.
- **VIEW-011 — Light, dark and system themes.** Each member chooses Light, Dark or System under Settings → Appearance; System, the default, follows the device's `prefers-color-scheme` and switches live when it changes. The choice is stored per member on the installation, so every browser the member signs in to applies it, and the server renders it on the document so it applies before first paint, without a script and with no flash of the other theme. Both themes meet WCAG 2.2 AA contrast for text and controls: text at 1.4.3, and every enabled UI component a person must see to operate (buttons, checkboxes, radios and toggles, selects and text fields, tabs, focus indicators, and selected, current and hover states) at 3:1 against what surrounds it (1.4.11); status, priority, label, project-status and health colors and code highlighting (ISSUE-026) stay distinguishable in both. Signed-out pages, sign-in and the public homepage (HOME-001) keep the light palette and are unchanged. Charts (REPORT-001), the roadmap timeline (INIT-003) and the desktop and mobile apps (MOBILE-001, MOBILE-005) inherit this choice and its contrast rule before they ship. *Acceptance:* `tests/e2e/theme-runtime.mjs` (VIEW-011), a browser journey that emulates `prefers-color-scheme: dark` and finds the app dark on first paint with scripts disabled, switches to Light and finds it light after a reload and in a second browser signed in as the same member on a dark device, then sets System and follows an emulated scheme change without a reload; plus an automated contrast check (`tests/e2e/theme-contrast.mjs`: axe `color-contrast` for text, and `tests/e2e/theme-control-contrast.mjs` for 3:1 non-text contrast of every enabled button, link drawn as a control, tab, select, text field and disclosure at rest and hovered, native checkboxes and radios from their rendered pixels, selected, pressed and current states, and the keyboard focus indicator of every focusable control) of the issue list, issue page, board, projects list, project page and Settings in both themes at 1440 and 390 with no AA failure. *Status:* done — `tests/e2e/theme-runtime.mjs`, `tests/e2e/theme-contrast.mjs`, `tests/e2e/theme-control-contrast.mjs`, `src/core/app/ui/palette.css`, `src/core/app/app.css`, `src/core/features/settings/appearance.ts`.
- **CMD-004 — Keyboard-first editing.** Every issue property has a single-key shortcut on a focused or selected issue (S status, A assignee, P priority, L labels, E estimate, D due date, M move team, Shift-D duplicate decision, Command/Ctrl-. copy branch name), shortcuts work on multiple selected issues, and a shortcut help sheet (?) lists them. *Acceptance:* a keyboard journey that changes status, assignee and priority of two selected issues without a mouse. *Status:* done — `tests/e2e/core-stream-planning-journey.mjs`, `tests/worker/bulk-commands.test.ts`, `evidence/keyboard-planning/CMD-004/`.

### Speed, real time and mobile

- **SYNC-001 — Real-time sync.** Changes by any member, automation, integration or agent appear in every open view of every other member within one second (p95, measured) without a reload, through a live connection (Cloudflare Durable Objects WebSocket) scoped to what each member may see; the five-second CURR-008 window remains the worst case after a dropped connection. *Acceptance:* a two-browser journey that changes an issue in one and measures its appearance in the other under one second, then drops the socket and recovers within CURR-008. *Status:* partial — `tests/e2e/core-current-journey.mjs` (refresh within five seconds without reload; a live connection is absent).
- **SYNC-002 — Local-first speed.** The web app keeps a permission-scoped local cache of the member's teams so issue lists, issue detail, filters and the palette open from local data in under 100ms (p95) and edits apply optimistically, reconciling with server versions (ISSUE-006) and showing a conflict when one is rejected. Offline edits queue and replay on reconnect in order. *Acceptance:* a browser journey that measures issue open and list filter latency from cache, makes an offline edit, reconnects and sees it applied, and a conflicting edit shown. *Status:* missing.
- **SYNC-003 — Collaborative editing.** Two members editing the same description or document see each other's cursors and changes live and never lose text; the result is one revision per editing session in ISSUE-018 history. *Acceptance:* a two-browser journey that types into the same description concurrently and finds both edits in the saved text. *Status:* missing.
- **MOBILE-001 — iOS and Android apps.** Native apps for iPhone and Android, published by Run Edit Run as one "Vector" listing in the Apple App Store and Google Play (MOBILE-004), so a member installs them from the store with no developer account and no build step; on first launch the member enters their installation's URL (or scans a QR code from Settings → Mobile, or opens an invitation link), signs in through that installation's own sign-in (email link, SSO under ROLE-004) in the system browser, and may add several installations and switch between them. The apps let members read and act on their inbox, triage (accept, defer, decline, duplicate), view My work, Current, projects and cycles, create issues with photos, screenshots, screen recordings and voice-to-text, with the images and video shown inline (ISSUE-024), tick checklists (ISSUE-025), comment with mentions, change properties and search. They use the same `/api/v1` and permission rules, through a per-device OAuth token (API-004) that the member or an Admin can revoke from Settings. The apps inherit VIEW-011: they apply the member's saved Light, Dark or System choice from the installation. Inherited from WORK-004: the mobile-app journey exercises Created, Subscribed with row Unfollow and Activity using the same visibility, filters and display rules. *Acceptance:* a device-simulator journey on iOS and Android builds that signs in to a local installation by URL, accepts a triage item, creates an issue with a photo and finds both on the web, the photo shown inline in the description in the app and on the web; plus the MOBILE-004 distributed-build check, which runs the same sign-in and triage steps on the store-distributed build (TestFlight and the Play internal testing track) against a hosted test installation. Inherited from WORK-004: the mobile-app journey exercises Created, Subscribed with row Unfollow and Activity using the same visibility, filters and display rules. *Status:* missing.
- **MOBILE-002 — Push notifications.** Working hours gate every time-of-day push delivery under NOTIFY-010.  The mobile apps receive real-time push notifications through Apple Push Notification service and Firebase Cloud Messaging for the events a member enabled (NOTIFY-007), with quick actions (mark read, snooze, assign to me) that call the member's installation directly. The installation never holds Apple or Google push credentials: it sends each push to the Run Edit Run push relay (MOBILE-004), which forwards it to APNs or FCM. The payload the relay receives and forwards carries only the opaque device registration, the issue identifier, its title, the actor's display name, the notification kind and an installation-scoped notification ID — never descriptions, comments, customer data or attachments — and a member can choose "ID only", in which the title and actor are omitted and the app fetches them from the installation after it wakes. A push is sent within 5 seconds of the inbox notification (NOTIFY-001), except during the member's push quiet hours: outside their working hours and working days in their personal time zone (NOTIFY-010), unless they turn quiet hours off, the inbox entry appears at once and the push is held and sent once when their working hours resume; revoking the device token or signing out deregisters the device at the relay. *Acceptance:* a journey on a local installation configured with a relay fixture at the network edge that mirrors the relay's registration and send API: registering a simulator device, triggering one enabled and one disabled event, and finding exactly one relay request whose body has only the listed fields and no description text, then the "ID only" setting producing a request with no title; an enabled event outside the member's working hours in their personal time zone producing no relay request until their working hours begin and then exactly one; a quick action that marks the notification read on the installation; and the MOBILE-004 distributed-build check receiving one real push on a test device through the production relay. For the mobile push channel (MOBILE-002), the inherited NOTIFY-007 duty is quoted verbatim: "SLACK-004 delivers Slack direct messages for assignments and mentions now, the events NOTIFY-006 mails; the per-event choice for every other event, Admin defaults, digests, mobile push (MOBILE-002), desktop notifications (MOBILE-005) and Teams direct messages (TEAMS-001) remain this clause's." Scope: mobile push only. The member chooses this channel per event type; Admin defaults include it; it honors working hours and digests (NOTIFY-010), mutes (NOTIFY-004), actor exclusion (NOTIFY-003) and access (OPS-001), and never repeats a delivered notification. *Status:* missing.
- **MOBILE-003 — Responsive web.** Every web page works at 390px width with no horizontal scroll, touch targets of at least 44px and the same actions as desktop. *Acceptance:* the 390px viewport checks in the development journey plus a 390px pass over every new page. *Status:* partial — `tests/e2e/dev-runtime.mjs` (390px checks exist for current pages; new pages in this contract are open).
- **MOBILE-004 — Distributed apps and push relay.** Run Edit Run's release lane builds, signs and publishes the iOS app to the App Store and the Android app to Google Play under Run Edit Run's developer accounts, and the macOS and Windows apps of MOBILE-005, on every Vector release whose API version they support; each app declares the oldest installation version it works with and tells the member to upgrade an older installation rather than failing silently. Run Edit Run operates the push relay those apps need: an installation registers with the relay once from Settings → Mobile (an Owner confirms; the relay issues an installation key, stored encrypted under SEC-001), devices register through the installation, and the relay accepts sends only signed with a registered installation key and only for devices registered to that installation. The relay stores device registrations and installation keys, and keeps no notification payload after forwarding; its terms, data list and status page are linked from Settings → Mobile. An Owner can turn push off for the whole installation, and a buyer who wants their own branded build publishes it under their own Apple and Google accounts with their own push credentials, pointing their installation at their own relay deployment from the same source (docs). *Acceptance:* the release lane's distributed-build check on each release: install the TestFlight and Play internal-testing builds on test devices, sign in to a hosted test installation by URL, accept a triage item, and receive one real push through the production relay with only the MOBILE-002 fields; plus a relay test that refuses an unsigned send, a send signed by another installation's key, and a send to a device deregistered by sign-out. *Status:* missing.
- **MOBILE-005 — Desktop apps and desktop notifications.** Signed and notarized desktop apps for macOS and Windows (MOBILE-004 publishes them), and the installable web app (PWA) for any browser, open the member's installation by URL with its own window, keyboard shortcuts, a dock or taskbar unread badge and native operating-system notifications in real time for the events the member routes to desktop under NOTIFY-007. In a browser, Web Push delivers the same desktop notifications through the browser's push service with the MOBILE-002 payload limits, while the tab is closed. Clicking a notification opens the issue in the app. The apps inherit VIEW-011: they apply the member's saved Light, Dark or System choice from the installation. *Acceptance:* a desktop-shell journey on macOS and Windows CI runners that signs in by URL and shows one native notification for an assignment, and a browser journey that grants notification permission, closes the tab, triggers a mention and finds one Web Push request at the push-service fixture with no description text. For the desktop notification channel (MOBILE-005), the inherited NOTIFY-007 duty is quoted verbatim: "SLACK-004 delivers Slack direct messages for assignments and mentions now, the events NOTIFY-006 mails; the per-event choice for every other event, Admin defaults, digests, mobile push (MOBILE-002), desktop notifications (MOBILE-005) and Teams direct messages (TEAMS-001) remain this clause's." Scope: desktop notification only. The member chooses this channel per event type; Admin defaults include it; it honors working hours and digests (NOTIFY-010), mutes (NOTIFY-004), actor exclusion (NOTIFY-003) and access (OPS-001), and never repeats a delivered notification. *Status:* missing.

### Target: Notifications

- **NOTIFY-007 — Channels and digests.** Each member chooses per event type whether it reaches them in the inbox only, by email, in a Slack direct message, and can take email as an immediate message or an hourly or daily digest; Admins set workspace defaults. Working hours gate every time-of-day digest delivery. Digests follow the member's personal time zone and working hours (NOTIFY-010): a daily digest is sent once at the start of the member's working hours on each working day, and hourly digests are sent only within working hours, with anything collected outside them carried into the next digest. Every channel honors mutes (NOTIFY-004), actor exclusion (NOTIFY-003) and access (OPS-001), and never repeats a delivered notification. the per-event choice for push, desktop notifications and Teams direct messages is delivered with MOBILE-002, MOBILE-005 and TEAMS-001. *Acceptance:* a journey that routes mentions to Slack and comments to a daily digest, then checks the Slack and mail fixtures receive exactly one message each; plus a scheduled-run test in which a member in America/Los_Angeles with working hours from 09:00 on weekdays, in a workspace in America/New_York, receives that daily digest once at 09:00 Pacific on a weekday and none on a Saturday. *Status:* done — per-event email and Slack choices, workspace defaults, hourly/daily email digests and working-hours admission; evidence is mapped per acceptance point in the Status table. Mobile push, desktop notifications and Teams direct messages are inherited by their named clauses.
- **NOTIFY-008 — Snooze and reminders.** A member can snooze an inbox item until a chosen time or until the issue changes, and set a personal reminder on any issue; snoozed items return once at that time. *Acceptance:* a journey that snoozes a notification for one hour, advances the clock, and sees it return once. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/notification-followups.test.ts`, `evidence/issue-follow-up/NOTIFY-008/`.
