- **NOTIFY-009 — Subscriptions.** Members can subscribe to a team, project, initiative, label or saved view and receive notifications for new matching issues and status changes, with the same channel rules. Inherited from ROLE-007: a member's team notifications and team subscriptions follow team membership. *Acceptance:* a journey that subscribes to a label and gets one notification when a new issue gets that label. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/follower-events.test.ts` (line 406: the NOTIFY-009-tagged workflow-status-removal case calls exerciseSubjectSubscriptions in `tests/worker/subscriptions-scenario.ts`: ordinary Member Bob outside a public team is omitted from subjects, refused 403 `team_membership_required` with no row or new-issue notice; joining permits a subscription and one notice; leaving removes it, re-subscription is refused with no row or notice; a directly inserted pre-fix row is unavailable and delivers nothing), `tests/e2e/subscriptions-journey.mjs` (subscriptionsJourney at 1440/390: the Owner outside a public team has neither its picker option nor Subscribe to team; joining restores both and the picker subscribes successfully; each width restores its membership fixture), `tests/worker/project-membership.test.ts` ([PROJ-003 ROLE-001 RELEASE-001 INIT-001 NOTIFY-009] only Owners and Admins delete a project; removal, archive and deletion preserve its issues, calling `exerciseInitiatives` in `tests/worker/initiatives-scenario.ts`: a fresh initiative-only subscriber gets one creation notice, two after one status change, and still two after rereading), `tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts` (current role-matrix doors), `tests/worker/data-http.test.ts` (native archive HTTP), and `tests/e2e/initiatives-journey.mjs` (1440/390). Default ADOPTED: self-only readable subjects; team subscriptions require current membership at selection, creation and delivery; Guests use invited-team subjects. The Worker helper asserts workspace-removal cleanup and status-channel development delivery. Portable preferences and history retain a destination-only historical replay cutoff. Mapping completion to status fails the completed-causes assertion.  `tests/worker/follower-events.test.ts` (workflow status removal notifies followers once without replaying terminal completion, worker-3, loading exerciseSubjectSubscriptions): after genuine completion, remapping the completed status to another completed status adds exactly one status-only notice, no delivery or mail with status email off and completion immediate, and rereads retain all counts. The original (pre-0100) trigger fails at `tests/worker/subscriptions-scenario.ts` line 56 with completed instead of status; migration 0100 passes. *Gap:* None.
- **NOTIFY-010 — Personal time zone and working hours.** Each member can set a personal IANA time zone (defaulting to the workspace time zone of OPS-003) and working hours. Their due reminders (NOTIFY-005) arrive at the reminder time in their own time zone, and their snoozes (NOTIFY-008), email digests (NOTIFY-007), personal reminders and push quiet hours use it; workspace-level instants (cycle boundaries, SLAs, workspace calendar days) stay in the workspace time zone. Dates and times in the interface show in the viewer's time zone with the zone named where it differs from the workspace's. *Acceptance:* a scheduled-run test with a workspace in America/New_York, one member in America/Los_Angeles and one without a personal zone, both with an issue due today, that sends the Eastern member's reminder at 09:00 Eastern and the Pacific member's at 09:00 Pacific, each exactly once. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/personal-time.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/personal-time-runtime.mjs`, `src/core/app/viewer-time.tsx`, `src/core/features/settings/personal-time.ts` (members save a personal IANA zone or follow the workspace zone, including its later changes, plus working hours and working days; due reminders arrive at the workspace reminder time in each assignee's zone, exactly once; personal reminders, snoozes, deferral and expiry inputs and interface instants use the viewer's zone, named where it differs from the workspace's; workspace calendar days and date-only due and target dates are unchanged. Working hours are stored in `settings/personal-time.ts`; NOTIFY-007 and MOBILE-002 inherit their application to digest scheduling and push quiet hours before those capabilities ship).

### Automation and follow-through

Every background action in Vector has an accountable owner (the person or Admin who configured it), a due condition, durable progress, a visible failure state and an explicit stop. Repeated runs never duplicate issues, comments, notifications or customer messages.

- **AUTO-001 — Recurring issues.** A member creates a recurring issue from a template on a cadence (daily, weekdays, weekly, monthly on a date or weekday, every N weeks) in the workspace timezone, with assignee, team, project and due offset; a series can skip creation while the previous occurrence is still open, and can be paused, edited or ended. Inherited from ROLE-001: Owners and Admins, and Members in the member's teams, create, pause or end a recurring issue; Guests are refused (role matrix). *Acceptance:* a scheduled-run test that creates a weekly series, runs the scheduler twice in the same week and finds one issue, then skips while open. *Status:* missing.
- **AUTO-002 — Workflow rules.** Owners and Admins build workspace rules, and a team's rules are built by its team owners or, while its *Who manages team settings* permission is All team members (ROLE-009), by all its members; other Members and all Guests cannot (role matrix). A rule has a trigger (issue created or changed, status entered, comment, label added, PR event, deploy, due-date approaching, SLA warning, schedule), conditions over any field and actions (set fields, assign, move status or team, add to cycle or project, add a comment, apply a template, notify a person or channel, call a webhook, start an agent with AGENT-001). Each rule shows a dry run over recent issues before saving, a run log with every change it made, a monthly run budget, and a failure state that notifies its owner; rules cannot trigger themselves in a loop. *Acceptance:* a journey that builds "when label Security is added, set priority Urgent and notify #security", dry-runs it, triggers it, and finds one run-log entry, one change and one Slack fixture message; plus the AUTO-002 rows of the role-matrix journey: a non-owner Member's team rule is refused 403 under the default permission and admitted under All team members, and a Member's workspace rule is refused 403. *Status:* partial — `src/core/features/due-reminders/service.ts`, `src/core/features/intake-return/service.ts` (fixed due reminders and deferral return; configurable rules are absent).
- **AUTO-003 — Auto-close and auto-archive.** Per team, under Settings → Auto-close and auto-archive, an Owner, an Admin or the team's owners (and every team member while its *Who manages team settings* permission is All team members, ROLE-009) set an auto-close period (days without activity: no issue change, activity entry or comment) and an auto-archive period (days since the issue closed), each 1 to 3650 days or off, and preview exactly which issues the next scheduled run would close and archive. The scheduled run moves each open (Backlog, Unstarted or Started) issue past its period to the team's first Canceled status with a comment-equivalent automation entry explaining why, and archives each completed, canceled or duplicate issue past its period with an automation entry; both are guarded by the issue version and held during a restore. Bugs are exempt by default: until issue types exist (ISSUE-021), an issue carrying a label named Bug is never auto-closed while the team's exemption is on. Archived issues keep every row and link; they leave the issue list, board, saved views, My work and project issue lists and default search, open by their identifier with an Archived notice, appear in search with archived records included, stay in archive exports, and return to lists when another status is chosen. Who may change these periods follows ROLE-009's *Who manages team settings* (proven by `tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts`). The automation entry is the comment: it sits in the issue's own activity with its reason ("Closed automatically after 30 days without activity", "Archived automatically 90 days after it closed") for everyone who can read the issue, and the issue's followers (NOTIFY-002) receive the notification a manual status change gives them, once per automated change, naming the automation as actor: a cancellation for an auto-close and an archive notification (its own kind, mutable like the others) for an auto-archive. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/housekeeping.test.ts` (AUTO-003), a scheduled-run test on a fixed clock with a 30-day stale issue and a 90-day completed issue that closes one and archives the other, each with an automation reason entry that the owner, a follower and a non-following member all read, as the preview listed; each follower gets exactly one notification per change across two runs, with the same causes and mail as an operator's manual cancel, and a non-follower gets none; a signed-out visitor and a removed member are refused the issue and its entry; a stale Bug-labelled issue stays open; the archived issue leaves the completed list and default search, is found with archived records included and in the archive export, and returns on reopening; plus `tests/e2e/housekeeping-runtime.mjs`, which sets and previews the periods in Settings, runs the scheduler twice, finds each reason entry in the issue's activity as the owner and as a following teammate, and not signed out, finds one inbox notification per automated change beside the manual cancel's, and finds the archived notice and archived search at 1440 and 390. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/housekeeping.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/housekeeping-runtime.mjs`, `src/core/features/housekeeping/`, `src/core/features/notifications/events.ts`.
- **AUTO-004 — SLAs.** Teams set SLA targets by priority, label or customer tier (for example: Urgent bugs from Enterprise customers resolved in 24 hours, triage within 4 business hours in the workspace timezone); issues show time remaining, warn the assignee and triage owner before breach, and record breaches for reporting (REPORT-001). Inherited from NOTIFY-001: an SLA warning notifies once in the inbox, never the actor; the receiving journey verifies this before the event ships. Inherited from SLACK-003: an SLA warning posts to its bound Slack or Discord channel once, using the shared durable delivery and minimal-message rules; its acceptance journey verifies the bound post. Inherited from ROLE-009: a team's SLAs change under its *Who manages team settings* permission; Owners and Admins always may and Guests never (role matrix). *Acceptance:* a clock-advancing test that warns once before breach and records one breach. Inherited from NOTIFY-001: an SLA warning notifies once in the inbox, never the actor; the receiving journey verifies this before the event ships. Inherited from SLACK-003: an SLA warning posts to its bound Slack or Discord channel once, using the shared durable delivery and minimal-message rules; its acceptance journey verifies the bound post. *Status:* missing.
- **AUTO-005 — Update reminders.** Project leads and initiative owners are reminded on the chosen cadence (weekly on a weekday, every two weeks) to post an update, with a prefilled draft (PROJ-010); missing updates are flagged on the project and in the initiative roll-up. *Acceptance:* a scheduled-run test that reminds one lead once per cadence and flags a missed update. *Status:* missing.
- **AUTO-006 — Due-soon rules.** Rules on due dates can act before the due day (for example two days before: notify the assignee and move to the active cycle), complementing the fixed due-today and overdue reminders of NOTIFY-005. Inherited from ROLE-001: only Owners and Admins build or change workspace automation rules (role matrix). *Acceptance:* an AUTO-002 rule with a due-in-two-days trigger acting once per issue and date. *Status:* partial — `src/core/features/due-reminders/service.ts` (due-today and overdue notifications only).

### Reporting

- **REPORT-001 — Insights.** Members chart any filtered set of issues by count or estimate over time and by any field: created vs completed, throughput, cycle time and lead time (from Started or created to Completed, with percentiles), time in each status, triage time, SLA breaches, bug inflow and open bug age, grouped by team, project, label, assignee, priority or customer tier. Each chart drills down to its issues and exports as CSV. Every chart inherits VIEW-011: it follows the member's Light, Dark or System theme, its series stay distinguishable in both, and its text and marks meet WCAG 2.2 AA contrast in both. *Acceptance:* a journey on a seeded data set whose cycle-time median and throughput per week match hand-computed values, and whose drill-down lists the counted issues. *Status:* partial — `tests/worker/cycle-report.test.ts` (Insights, shared dashboards and daily burn-up); `tests/e2e/insights-journey.mjs` (insightsJourney), run by `tests/e2e/cycle-report-runtime.mjs`. *Gap:* Triage time (TRIAGE-001), SLA breaches (AUTO-004), customer tier (CUST-*), issue types and unavailable historical data; `tests/e2e/insights-journey.mjs` (insightsAccessCells at 1440/390 and HTTP) exercises Member omission and Viewer chart/count/drill/CSV reads after explicit membership. Bug series require a current Bug label. No /api/v1 series read. Default ADOPTED: Owners, Admins and Members read only visible issues; Guests refused.
- **REPORT-002 — Cycle and project charts.** Each cycle shows a burn-up of scope and completion by day, with added and removed scope marked, and a final report (completed, carried over, added mid-cycle, by assignee); each project shows scope and completion over time with the projected completion date. At each workspace-zone day close, per-project and per-cycle daily scope and completion counts are recorded as counts only, without issue identifiers, titles, assignees or people. Later issue deletion does not rewrite those closed days; days before recording starts say history not recorded. Projected completion uses open issues divided by completions recorded in the last 28 closed workspace days / 28, rounded up to whole workspace calendar days. A day counts issues entering Completed while in the project, including creation in Completed; same-day events are deduplicated, and a reopened issue completed on a later day counts again. Today enters the rate only after day close. Fewer than three recorded completions shows Not enough history; zero remaining issues has no projected date. Completed, canceled and archived projects retain their last aggregate state; reopening resumes live history. Membership events retain identifiers and timestamps only, and erasure is unchanged. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/cycle-report.test.ts` and `tests/e2e/cycle-report-runtime.mjs` retain cycle proof; `tests/worker/project-report.test.ts` follows adds, moves, deletions and reopenings at a fixed clock, advances scheduled day closes, proves aggregate-only history, projection and terminal states; `tests/e2e/project-report-runtime.mjs` uses live and frozen reports at 1440/390; `tests/worker/data-http.test.ts` retains report history through archive restore. *Status:* done — `src/core/features/project-report/`, `tests/worker/project-report.test.ts`, `tests/worker/cycle-report.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/project-report-runtime.mjs`, `tests/worker/data-http.test.ts`.
- **REPORT-003 — Dashboards.** Members compose dashboards of saved charts, counts and issue lists for a team or for leadership, share them inside the workspace, and filter the whole dashboard by team, date range or project. *Acceptance:* a journey that builds a three-widget dashboard, shares it, and opens it as another member who sees only what they may access. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/cycle-report.test.ts` (Insights, shared dashboards and daily burn-up), `tests/worker/dashboard-proof.ts` (exerciseDashboards), `tests/worker/data-http.test.ts` (portable definitions), and `tests/e2e/dashboards-journey.mjs` (dashboardsJourney, loaded by cycle-report-runtime in the runtime Verify shard). Each light/dark theme at 1440/390 selects both projects across chart, count and list: two exact small-project identifiers; 26 large-project matches with exactly 25 preview rows and the native full-list link showing all 26; matching project-filtered HTTP results; All projects restores the 28 owner-created matches. The added 26 issues are due outside 2026-11-02..05, preserving the existing one-issue date-range and member-created-scope assertions. dashboardAccessCells proves private omission at both widths and Viewer mutation refusals; after private-team admission, positive Viewer counts and identifiers are HTTP-asserted, rather than UI content assertions at both widths. Default ADOPTED: Owners/Admins manage any dashboard, Members their own, Guests refused; team sharing requires membership. Only definitions are stored; every read uses the viewer. The global date range filters issue due dates and intersects every saved widget; issue-list widgets show 25 with a link to the native full list.
- **REPORT-004 — Workload.** For a cycle or date range, a workload view shows each person's assigned estimate and issue count against their stated capacity, including delegated agent work, so a lead can rebalance; it never ranks people by output. *Acceptance:* a journey with three assignees whose committed points match the view and over-capacity is marked. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/workload.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/core-workload-journey.mjs`.
- **REPORT-005 — Warehouse and spreadsheet export.** Owners and Admins (role matrix) schedule incremental exports of issues, history, cycles, projects and customer links as CSV or JSON to an S3-compatible bucket (Amazon S3, Cloudflare R2), expose a read-only export API that Fivetran and Airbyte connectors can page through, and sync a saved view to a Google Sheets spreadsheet through the Sheets API (`spreadsheets.values.batchUpdate`), refreshed on a schedule of at least hourly with one row per issue and one column per visible view property. *Acceptance:* a scheduled export to an R2 fixture whose second run carries only changed rows, a paged export API read, and a Google Sheets API fixture at the network edge to which a saved view syncs: after one issue in the view changes and another leaves it, the next sync updates the first row and removes the second. *Status:* missing.

### Connecting integrations

Vector runs on each buyer's own Cloudflare account, so there is no shared Slack, GitHub or Teams app that reaches every installation, and Run Edit Run runs no event relay for them: each installation owns its provider apps, created for it by the provider's own app-creation flow, so its events and tokens never pass through a third party. Run Edit Run publishes only the apps whose provider requires a store listing and that call the installation by URL: the mobile and desktop apps with their push relay (MOBILE-004) and the Zapier and Make apps (CONNECT-001).

- **INTEG-001 — Guided connection from Settings.** Settings → Integrations lists every integration with its state (not connected, connecting, connected, needs attention). An Owner or Admin connects one through the setup path in the table below: Vector generates every value the provider needs — this installation's request, event, OAuth redirect and sidebar URLs, least-privilege scopes, event subscriptions, a fresh signing secret, and any app package or plugin manifest — and either submits them through the provider's app-creation API or manifest flow, or prefills the provider's creation page, so the Admin never types or edits a scope, URL or manifest by hand; the only values an Admin copies are the credentials a provider shows once, which Vector stores encrypted (SEC-001). Each path ends with a live check: Vector calls the provider with the new credentials and asks the Admin to perform one real action (send `@Vector` a message, open a PR), and marks the integration connected only when that event arrives signed. From then on every member uses it without further setup, linking their own provider identity where the integration acts as them (SLACK-001, DOCS-001). Disconnecting revokes tokens, deletes the provider webhook where the provider's API allows it, and lists anything the Admin must remove in the provider. *Acceptance:* one setup journey per provider in the table against a provider fixture at the network edge that mirrors that provider's real app-creation, OAuth and event APIs (for example Slack's `apps.manifest.create` and `oauth.v2.access`, GitHub's manifest redirect and `POST /app-manifests/{code}/conversions`), in which an Admin completes setup from Settings → Integrations without entering any scope, URL or manifest text, the first real event (an `@Vector` message, a PR opened) creates or links an issue, and a Member's attempt to open the setup is refused 403; plus a hosted connection run for Slack and GitHub against the real providers on a test workspace and organization, recorded as hosted evidence. *Status:* partial — `src/core/features/integrations/ui/github-setup.tsx`, `src/core/features/integrations/ui/source-setup.tsx`, `src/core/features/integrations/ui/copy-field.tsx`, `src/core/features/integrations/test-webhook.ts` (Settings → Integrations exists for signed sources, GitHub repository webhooks and outbound destinations, with copyable payload URLs, reveal-once secrets and a test delivery; provider app creation, manifest flows, OAuth installs and the live first-event check are absent).
- **INTEG-002 — Integration health and repair.** Each connected integration shows its last received event, last successful call, failure count and token expiry; a failing integration notifies the Admins who connected it and shows the provider's error. When the installation's origin changes or a provider rotates a secret, Settings repairs the integration in place — updating the request URLs through the provider's API where one exists (Slack `apps.manifest.update`, GitHub `PATCH /app/hook/config`, GitLab hook update) or re-running the setup path with the existing app otherwise — without losing links, synced threads or identity mappings. After a restore (SEC-001) each integration shows "needs attention" until its credentials are re-entered. *Acceptance:* a journey that changes the installation origin in a fixture run, repairs Slack and GitHub from Settings and finds each provider fixture's request URL updated and the next event received, with existing links intact. *Status:* partial — `src/core/features/integrations/reenter.ts`, `src/core/features/integrations/ui/delivery-setup.tsx` (credential re-entry after restore and outbound delivery state; health for inbound providers and in-place repair are absent).

**Setup path per provider** (INTEG-001). "Manifest flow" means the provider creates the app from a definition Vector generates; "API registration" means Vector registers webhooks itself with a token the Admin pastes once.

| Provider | Clause | Whose app | Setup path an Admin follows |
|---|---|---|---|
| Slack | SLACK-001 | the buyer's own Slack app | Settings creates the app through Slack's App Manifest API (`apps.manifest.create`, using a one-time app configuration token the Admin generates at api.slack.com/apps) or, without a token, opens Slack's "create app from manifest" page prefilled with the generated manifest; the Admin then clicks Install, which completes OAuth v2 back to the installation. |
| Microsoft Teams | TEAMS-001 | the buyer's own bot and Teams app | The Admin creates a bot in the Teams Developer Portal (Bot management) with the messaging endpoint Vector shows and pastes its app ID and client secret; Vector generates the complete Teams app package (manifest with bot ID, valid domains and resource-specific consent permissions, plus icons) for the Admin to upload in the Teams admin center as a custom app. |
| GitHub (Cloud and Enterprise Server) | GITHUB-006 | the buyer's own GitHub App | GitHub App manifest flow: Settings posts the generated manifest to the user's or organization's "new GitHub App" page, GitHub redirects back with a code, Vector converts it (`POST /app-manifests/{code}/conversions`) into the app ID, private key and webhook secret, then sends the Admin to the app's install page to choose repositories. |
| GitLab (.com and self-managed) | GITLAB-001 | a buyer group or project access token | API registration: the Admin pastes a group or project access token with `api` scope; Vector creates a project webhook on each chosen project (and a group webhook where the GitLab tier provides one) with a generated secret token. |
| Bitbucket Cloud | BITBUCKET-001 | a buyer workspace access token | API registration of repository webhooks with a generated secret. |
| Zendesk | SUPPORT-001 | a private Zendesk app | Vector generates a private Zendesk app package (ticket sidebar pointing at this installation) for upload in Zendesk Admin Center, and, with an API token the Admin pastes, creates the Zendesk webhook and trigger through the Zendesk API. |
| Intercom | SUPPORT-001 | the buyer's own Intercom app | The Admin creates an app in the Intercom Developer Hub; Vector shows the Inbox app (Canvas Kit) initialize and submit URLs and the webhook topics as copy fields and takes the app's access token and client secret. |
| Front | SUPPORT-001 | the buyer's own Front app | The Admin creates an app in Front's developer settings; Vector shows the plugin (sidebar) URL and webhook URL as copy fields and takes the app's API token and signing secret. |
| Sentry | ERROR-001 | a Sentry internal integration | The Admin creates an internal integration in Sentry with the webhook URL, scopes and resource subscriptions Vector shows, and pastes its token and client secret. |
| Datadog | ERROR-001 | a Datadog webhook | API registration: with an API and application key the Admin pastes, Vector creates the Datadog webhook with a secret header. |
| CircleCI, Vercel, Netlify, Cloudflare | CI-001, RELEASE-002 | provider webhooks | API registration with a provider token the Admin pastes once, or copy fields where the provider offers no webhook API. |
| Figma | DESIGN-001 | the buyer's own Figma OAuth app and plugin | The Admin registers a Figma OAuth app with the callback URL Vector shows; members connect their Figma accounts for previews. Vector generates the Figma plugin manifest with this installation in its allowed network domains, which an Admin publishes as a private organization plugin or members import from the manifest. |
| Notion, Google Drive, Microsoft OneDrive and SharePoint, Dropbox, Confluence | DOCS-001 | the buyer's own OAuth clients | The Admin registers an OAuth client in each provider's developer console with the redirect URL Vector shows (Google as an internal app of the buyer's Google Workspace); members then connect their own accounts. |
| Zapier, Make | CONNECT-001 | Run Edit Run's published apps | No Admin setup in the provider: a member adds the Vector app in Zapier or Make, enters the installation URL, and authorizes it by OAuth (API-004) on the installation; triggers register webhooks (WEBHOOK-002) on that installation. |
| Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, Copilot | AGENT-009 | the buyer's own provider accounts and runners | As AGENT-009's provider table states. |

### Target: Code host and delivery

- **GITHUB-003 — Branches and status automation.** Each issue offers a copyable branch name (for example `vec-123-fix-checkout`); Vector links a PR whose branch, title or body names an issue, understands closing words ("Fixes VEC-123") and contributing words ("Part of VEC-123"), and moves the issue by team rules: draft PR opened → In Progress, review requested → In Review, merged → Done (or the status chosen per rule, including on deploy with RELEASE-002). Each move is recorded as Git automation and never moves an issue backwards out of Completed without a rule saying so. Inherited from NOTIFY-001: a Git status/completion event, naming the Git automation as actor notifies once in the inbox, never the actor; the receiving journey verifies this before the event ships. The inherited merged-PR fixture completes a followed issue and its follower receives exactly one notification naming Git automation; the actor receives none and replay adds none. Inherited from ROLE-009: a team's Git automation rules change under its *Who manages team settings* permission (role matrix). Provider-backed "create branch" is tracked in GITHUB-008. *Acceptance:* A signed-fixture journey that opens a draft PR, requests review and merges it, and finds three status moves with automation attribution, then replays the deliveries and finds no duplicates. Inherited from NOTIFY-001: a Git status/completion event, naming the Git automation as actor notifies once in the inbox, never the actor; the receiving journey verifies this before the event ships. The inherited merged-PR fixture completes a followed issue and its follower receives exactly one notification naming Git automation; the actor receives none and replay adds none. *Status:* done — Copyable issue branch suggestions; branch/title/body references and closing/contributing words; version-checked per-team draft/review/merge/deploy rules; Git automation status activity and follower notifications commit with delivery replay receipts. Evidence: `tests/worker/github-links.test.ts` (signed deliveries preserve links, automate team statuses and notify followers exactly once; exerciseGithubAutomation: contributing-only PR link, follower Inbox outcomes and replay with exact recipients; exerciseConfiguredPrIsolation: non-default draft, review and merge outcomes for two teams and unchanged unconfigured work; exerciseGithubCommits, exerciseGithubDeployments), `tests/e2e/integrations/github-chain.mjs` (exerciseGithubChain: Owner rule saves, stale concurrency, branch copy and signed lifecycle at 1440 and 390), `tests/e2e/integrations/github-team-permissions.mjs` (exerciseGithubTeamPermissions: team-owner and ordinary Member saves, next-request revocation, visible stale and permission errors, Guest and Viewer refusals, personal Integrations), `tests/worker/role-probes.ts` (githubRuleProbes, registered by the role-matrix journey: filtered GET and native PATCH under both settings values). *Gap:* Provider-backed create branch is tracked in GITHUB-008.
- **GITHUB-004 — Commits.** Push events link commits whose message names an issue, showing author, message and link on the issue; closing words in a commit on the default branch act like a merged PR under the team rules. *Acceptance:* a signed push fixture with two commits naming one issue shows both on the issue and applies the closing rule once. *Status:* done — Signed push events retain commit author text, first message line and a validated commit URL; non-default branches only link; default-branch closing words share the PR rule engine and apply once per issue/event and commit SHA. Evidence: `tests/worker/github-links.test.ts` (signed deliveries preserve links, automate team statuses and notify followers exactly once; exerciseGithubAutomation, exerciseGithubCommits, exerciseGithubDeployments), `tests/e2e/integrations/github-chain.mjs` (exerciseGithubChain, 1440/390).
- **GITHUB-005 — Review and checks on the issue.** A linked PR shows its review state (review requested, approved, changes requested, with reviewers), required check status from GitHub Actions or other CI, mergeability and stacked-PR parent; issue lists can filter by these, and reviewers get a notification in Vector when their review is requested on a linked PR. Inherited from NOTIFY-001: a Git review request notifies once in the inbox, never the actor; the receiving journey verifies this before the event ships. *Acceptance:* signed review and check-run fixtures that change the issue's PR badge from checks failing to approved and passing. Inherited from NOTIFY-001: a Git review request notifies once in the inbox, never the actor; the receiving journey verifies this before the event ships. *Status:* missing.
- **GITHUB-006 — GitHub App.** Each installation creates its own GitHub App through the GitHub App manifest flow from Settings → Integrations → GitHub (INTEG-001's table): the Admin chooses a personal account or organization (or a GitHub Enterprise Server host), approves the prefilled app on GitHub, and Vector converts the returned code into the app's ID, private key and webhook secret, then sends the Admin to choose repositories. The app has least-privilege scopes on chosen repositories (GitHub.com and GitHub Enterprise Server), posts a linkback comment on linked PRs, reads PR and check state for enrichment, and maps GitHub users to members; tokens are short-lived installation tokens stored encrypted, and uninstalling stops all calls. The webhook-only mode of GITHUB-001 remains available without a token. Inherited from SEC-001: credentials are sealed, shown once, least-scoped, excluded from archives, re-entered on restore, rotated on demand and revoked on removal; an archive-then-restore journey proves no credential is exported and the restored connection needs re-entry. *Acceptance:* an app-install journey against a GitHub API fixture at the network edge that mirrors the manifest redirect, `POST /app-manifests/{code}/conversions` and the installation callback: the Admin connects without editing scopes or URLs, the first PR opened in a chosen repository links its issue and posts one linkback, and calls stop after uninstall; plus the INTEG-001 hosted connection run on a real test organization. Inherited from SEC-001: credentials are sealed, shown once, least-scoped, excluded from archives, re-entered on restore, rotated on demand and revoked on removal; an archive-then-restore journey proves no credential is exported and the restored connection needs re-entry. *Status:* missing.
- **GITHUB-007 — GitHub Issues sync.** A team can sync with one or more GitHub repositories' Issues: new GitHub issues appear in triage (or Backlog by rule), and title, description, state, labels, assignee and comments sync both ways with stable source identity; conflicts take the latest change and are shown in activity. Sync can be one-way for public open-source repositories. *Acceptance:* a fixture journey that creates an issue on each side, edits a comment on each side and closes on GitHub, and finds both sides consistent with no duplicate. *Status:* missing.
- **GITHUB-008 — Provider-backed create branch.** Each issue offers a copyable branch name (for example `vec-123-fix-checkout`) and a "create branch" action. *Acceptance:* the offered "create branch" action creates the branch through the installation’s GitHub App (GITHUB-006). *Status:* missing.
- **GITLAB-001 — GitLab.** For GitLab.com and self-managed GitLab, Vector links merge requests, branches and commits that name issues, moves status by the same team rules as GITHUB-003, shows approvals and pipeline status, and receives environment deployments for RELEASE-002, through signed webhooks and a least-privilege access token. Setup is INTEG-001's API registration: the Admin pastes a group or project access token with `api` scope, chooses projects, and Vector creates each project webhook (or a group webhook where the tier provides one) with a generated secret token, without the Admin entering a URL. *Acceptance:* a setup journey against a GitLab API fixture in which pasting a token and choosing two projects creates two webhooks with the installation's URL and secret, then signed GitLab fixtures for MR opened, approved, merged and a production environment deployment that move one issue as configured. *Status:* missing.
- **GITLAB-002 — GitLab Issues sync.** A team can sync with GitLab project issues with the same behavior as GITHUB-007. *Acceptance:* the GITHUB-007 journey against GitLab fixtures. *Status:* missing.
- **BITBUCKET-001 — Bitbucket.** Bitbucket Cloud pull requests, branches, commits and pipelines link to issues and drive the same team rules. *Acceptance:* signed Bitbucket fixtures for PR opened and merged that move one issue. *Status:* missing.
- **CI-001 — CI and deploy providers.** CircleCI, GitHub Actions, GitLab CI, Vercel, Netlify and Cloudflare deployments report build and deploy results for linked commits through signed webhooks or the API, shown on the issue and usable as rule triggers. *Acceptance:* one signed or API fixture per named provider — a CircleCI workflow-completed webhook, a GitHub Actions `workflow_run` event, a GitLab pipeline hook, a Vercel deployment webhook, a Netlify deploy notification and a Cloudflare Workers or Pages deployment — each appearing on the linked issue and firing an AUTO-002 rule exactly once when replayed. *Status:* missing.

### Chat

- **SLACK-001 — Slack app.** Each installation has its own Slack app, created from Settings → Integrations → Slack through Slack's app manifest (INTEG-001's table): with a one-time app configuration token Vector calls `apps.manifest.create` with the generated manifest (this installation's event, interactivity, slash-command, unfurl and OAuth redirect URLs and least scopes) and receives the app's credentials; without a token, Vector opens Slack's create-from-manifest page prefilled and the Admin pastes the signing secret, client ID and client secret Slack shows. The Admin then clicks Install, which completes OAuth v2 to the company's Slack workspace (or an Enterprise Grid org) and stores the bot token encrypted. Members link their Slack identity to their Vector member; uninstalling or unlinking stops all calls and removes stored tokens. Inherited from SEC-001: credentials are sealed, shown once, least-scoped, excluded from archives, re-entered on restore, rotated on demand and revoked on removal; an archive-then-restore journey proves no credential is exported and the restored connection needs re-entry. SLACK-003 already covers the sealed bot-token paste path and its archive/re-entry proof; SLACK-004 covers member-confirmed linking by the member's own sign-in email, the pasted signing secret and the interactivity door; the manifest/OAuth installation, Events API, @Vector issue creation, explicit Admin mapping and uninstall remain this clause’s acceptance. *Acceptance:* an install journey against a Slack API fixture that mirrors `apps.manifest.create`, `oauth.v2.access` and the Events API: from Settings → Integrations → Slack an Admin connects through the manifest flow without hand-editing scopes or URLs, the first `@Vector` message in a channel creates an issue, one encrypted bot token is stored and one member is linked, then uninstalling stops all calls; the same journey through the prefilled-page path; and the INTEG-001 hosted connection run on a real test Slack workspace. Inherited from SEC-001: credentials are sealed, shown once, least-scoped, excluded from archives, re-entered on restore, rotated on demand and revoked on removal; an archive-then-restore journey proves no credential is exported and the restored connection needs re-entry. app_uninstalled and tokens_revoked remove stored Slack keys and member links and disable destinations without a provider call. `tests/e2e/integration-runtime.mjs`, loading exerciseSlackAppInstallation from `tests/e2e/integrations/slack-app-installation.mjs` and exerciseSlackAppArchive from `tests/e2e/integrations/slack-app-archive.mjs` (Verify hosted-artifact), exercises generated/prefilled Settings installation, explicit mapping, first-mention issue and actor, local removal, independent restored empty credential fields and provider uninstall followed by a scheduled tick with no further Slack calls at 1440/390, with overflow, serious/critical axe and hydration checks. `tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts` (current private and invited teams scope native, bearer, search, project, notification and document reads; Viewers remain read-only; Verify worker-2), through `tests/worker/role-viewer-census.ts`, supplies valid seeded inputs for both new mapping mutation doors and requires unchanged Viewer storage. Admin mapping riders in the named mapping phase (Verify worker-1): Admin mapping to another Admin or Owner is refused with unchanged links and audit; self, active Member and Guest mappings are accepted; targets read their Slack user/workspace IDs and revoke through personal Settings, including Guest/Viewer targets; Admin revoke and every map, re-map or unmap each add exactly one actor/target audit entry; a foreign Slack workspace is refused. The named Events phase (Verify worker-1) proves a mapped Guest receives Slack 403 with unchanged storage and native uninvited-team 404 with unchanged issues/receipts; demotion or public-door removal stops the next signed event without any storage write. *Gap:* Hosted real-workspace installation remains unproved, pending the owner's Slack app console steps.
- **SLACK-002 — Issues from Slack with synced threads.** From any message, a shortcut, an emoji reaction or `@Vector` creates an issue (or adds the message to an existing one) with the message, author, attachments and a link, using a template or request form (TRIAGE-004), with a Customer picker prefilled from the message author's email domain so the message becomes that customer's request (CUST-006, CUST-008); the Slack thread then syncs both ways with exactly one Vector comment thread (ISSUE-008): the originating message becomes that thread's root comment, each Slack reply becomes a reply in it, a Vector reply in that thread posts once to the Slack thread, resolving the Vector thread adds a resolved marker in Slack, and comments outside that thread never post to Slack; a second Slack thread linked to the same issue maps to its own comment thread. Status changes post into every linked Slack thread. Request channels can be configured so every top-level message becomes a triage item. Files in Slack messages and replies become attachments shown inline in the mapped comment (ISSUE-024); an image or video embedded in a Vector reply in that thread is uploaded into the Slack thread once through Slack's external upload API (`files.getUploadURLExternal`, then `files.completeUploadExternal`), and a checklist (ISSUE-025) posts as text. A Slack user with no Vector seat and no linked identity (SLACK-001) — someone in sales or support — can file in the same ways in any channel the Vector app is in: Vector records them as the issue's requester by Slack user ID and display name (and as a contact of the customer their email domain maps to under CUST-006), never as a member, and gives them no sign-in and no access to Vector; they receive the confirmation with the identifier and title, and the issue's status changes and the Vector replies in the mapped thread reach them in that Slack thread like anyone in it. For an issue in a private team (ROLE-002), the thread receives only the identifier and status changes. *Acceptance:* a Slack Events fixture that creates an issue from a message, posts a thread reply that becomes a reply in the mapped comment thread, a Vector reply in that thread that posts back to the Slack thread once, a separate top-level Vector comment that posts nothing to Slack, and a second linked Slack thread whose replies land only in its own comment thread; plus a thread reply carrying a PNG that shows inline in the mapped reply, a Vector reply with an embedded image that calls `files.completeUploadExternal` exactly once for that thread, and a Slack user with no seat and no linked identity who files from a message, is recorded as the requester with no member created, and receives the issue's next status change in the thread; and a filing whose Customer picker is prefilled from the author's domain and adds the message as a request on that customer. *Status:* missing.
- **SLACK-003 — Channel notifications.** Teams, projects, initiatives and saved views can post chosen events (new triage item, new issue, status change, project update, release, SLA warning, agent completion) to a Slack channel, respecting ROLE-002 so private-team content only posts to channels the Admin bound to that team. A team or project can create its own Slack channel when it is created (`conversations.create`, named from its identifier or name) and bind it automatically, and a Discord channel can receive the same events through a Discord webhook URL. An Admin enters a Slack bot token in Settings → Integrations once; auth.test validates it, sealSecret/openSecret seal it per workspace, settings never reveal it again, archives exclude it, restored installations need attention until re-entry, and removal calls auth.revoke, deletes local custody and disables bindings (a provider error refuses removal visibly). Owners and Admins connect Slack and bind channels in Settings → Integrations, and only they may have the Slack bot create a channel in the customer's Slack workspace, which a team or project created with it binds automatically; a Member who may create a project may bind it to a channel an Owner or Admin already bound or allowed, and post to it (role matrix, ROLE-001); no private teams exist, so every team is workspace-visible and bindable, and ROLE-002 inherits the private-team channel restriction before private teams ship. Existing new-triage, new-issue and status-change events post through the shared native outbound claim, bounded-retry, uncertain-result and visible Settings failure machinery, once per event per binding. Saved-view membership uses the same list predicates at dispatch. Messages carry identifier, title, readable actor, old → new status and the canonical /app/issues/:identifier link, never description or comment bodies. Unbinding, issue deletion and operator removal stop future posts; already-posted Slack or Discord messages cannot be recalled by local erasure (SEC-003). Channel creation makes one conversations.create call with a lowercase hyphenated identifier or name of at most 80 characters, public channels only; name_taken and other Slack errors are shown without a retry loop and refuse object creation. Discord accepts only HTTPS discord.com or discordapp.com URLs whose path starts /api/webhooks/, seals the URL and refuses redirects; this fixed-host rule does not depend on arbitrary resolved-address egress. PROJ-010, RELEASE-001/RELEASE-003, AUTO-004, AGENT-002/AGENT-003 and INIT-001 inherit their future objects’ bound-channel events and acceptance before shipping. Slack channel selection and each post explicitly check conversations.info: bot membership and is_channel true, archived/shared/external-shared/org-shared/pending-external-shared false, and not frozen; the held Slack conversation object omits is_frozen, recorded as an assumption that an absent is_frozen means not frozen (revisable if a provider run contradicts it). Unsafe channels and archived/frozen/not-joined post refusals suspend routing visibly until an Admin confirms a fresh binding; held events are not replayed. Channel IDs are explicitly mapped by the Admin, never inferred from email or name. Actor display names are allowed for current workspace-visible items (ct, 20 Oct 19:12Z); ROLE-002 inherits generic notices without actor or text, or a DM to a permitted explicitly mapped member, for private/restricted items before they ship. *Acceptance:* a journey that binds a project to a channel and finds one formatted Slack message per update, a project created with "create Slack channel" that finds one `conversations.create` call and the binding, and a Discord webhook fixture that receives one message per bound event. Tests [SLACK-003] in tests/worker/outbound-broken-destination.test.ts and tests/e2e/integration-runtime.mjs exercise Settings, native mutations, one formatted post per event, channel creation and Discord delivery at the network edge; tests/worker/data-http.test.ts retains bindings without credentials through restore. *Status:* done — `src/core/features/integrations/channels/`, `tests/worker/outbound-broken-destination.test.ts`, `tests/worker/data-http.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/integrations/channel-settings.mjs`, `tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts`. Strict channel-safety fixtures, the built settings and create-channel journey at 1440/390, and the real-provider run passed; dated provider and cleanup transcripts are in evidence/slack-003/SAFE_PROVIDER.md, REAL_PROVIDER.md and CHANNEL_SAFETY.md. The role-matrix journey admits Owners and Admins and refuses Members and Guests 403 when a project created with Create Slack channel asks the bot for a channel, with no Slack call. The registry-driven `tests/worker/role-matrix.test.ts` case "every role-matrix row with a door" admits Owner, Admin and Member project bindings, refuses an unapproved channel before any Slack call, and refuses Guest. `tests/worker/outbound-broken-destination.test.ts` (channel notifications scenario) proves a Member binds an already-bound channel and its issue/status events post once. `tests/e2e/integrations/channel-settings.mjs` (`exerciseChannels`) proves the approved-channel choice and bound state at 1440 and 390, with overflow and serious/critical axe checks.
- **SLACK-004 — Personal Slack notifications.** Members can receive their notifications as Slack direct messages under NOTIFY-007 with actions (assign to me, change status, open). A signed-in member chooses Link Slack in Settings → Notifications; Vector calls `users.lookupByEmail` (scope users:read.email) with that member's own Vector sign-in email, shows the Slack account it found and links it only when the member confirms; no match shows "No Slack account found for this email" and stores nothing, and a lookup error is shown and not retried. There is no silent or background lookup, no lookup by an Admin for someone else's email, no bulk directory read (`users.list`) and no matching on names (ct, 20 Oct 19:12Z); explicit Admin mapping remains SLACK-001's. The link is shown, audited (`slack.member_linked`, `slack.member_unlinked`) and revocable with Unlink; unlinking, member removal, suspension (ROLE-011 inherits) and removal of the workspace's Slack credential delete it and stop every direct message. A linked member turns on Slack direct messages separately for assignments and for direct mentions, the events Vector mails today (NOTIFY-006); the option is unavailable, with the reason shown, while the workspace has no valid Slack credential or the member has no link, and NOTIFY-007 keeps the choice for every other event. Each qualifying notification creates at most one direct-message intent per recipient in the same transaction as its source mutation, in the native outbound ledger (no second queue); immediately after commit, like immediate mail, and before every later attempt Vector rechecks mutes (NOTIFY-004), actor exclusion (NOTIFY-003), the active recipient, issue access (OPS-001) and the member's link and choice, then calls `conversations.open` (im:write) and `chat.postMessage`. A delivered message is never repeated: rate limits and an unanswered `conversations.open` (which posts nothing) retry at most three attempts, after one and then five minutes; an unknown `chat.postMessage` result is uncertain and never resent; failed and uncertain messages are listed to the recipient in Settings → Notifications. A message carries only the event kind, issue identifier and title, actor display name, status change, due date and the authenticated canonical `/app/issues/:identifier` link, in plain-text blocks with `unfurl_links` and `unfurl_media` false — never a description, comment or mention body, free text or AI output; private or restricted items (none exist until ROLE-002) get a generic notice with no actor or text under ROLE-002. Open is the canonical link button; Assign to me and Change status (an inline select of the team's workflow states the issue-edit door accepts) appear only while the workspace's Slack signing secret is stored, and Settings says they are off until it is entered (pattern 3). Slack's interactivity requests reach `POST /api/integrations/slack/interactive`, which verifies `X-Slack-Signature` (HMAC-SHA256 over `v0:<timestamp>:<raw body>`) in constant time, refuses a timestamp more than 5 minutes from the Worker clock and a replayed request key, maps the Slack user to the Vector member only through the confirmed link and runs the change through the version-checked issue-edit door with that member's own authority and the issue version the message carried. A changed issue is refused with a threaded Slack reply carrying Open and is never overwritten; an applied change refreshes the message with the new version. The door answers before Slack's 3-second limit and replies afterwards. An unlinked, removed or suspended Slack user, a bad or missing signature, a stale timestamp and a replay change nothing. An Admin enters the Slack app's signing secret beside the bot token in Settings → Integrations; it is sealed with sealSecret/openSecret per workspace, never shown again, excluded from archives with the member links (SEC-001: after a restore it is entered again and members re-link) and removed with the credential. docs/INTEGRATIONS.md names the Interactivity Request URL the Admin sets in their Slack app until SLACK-001's manifest sets it, and what leaves the deployment in a direct message. *Acceptance:* a mention routed to Slack produces one DM fixture with working actions. [SLACK-004] in `tests/worker/outbound-broken-destination.test.ts` (with `tests/worker/slack-dm-scenario.ts`), `tests/worker/data-http.test.ts` and `tests/e2e/integration-runtime.mjs` (with `tests/e2e/integrations/slack-dm-settings.mjs`) proves own-email lookup and confirmation, no-match and lookup-error handling, exactly one DM with the three actions and only the allowed fields, signed Assign to me and Change status each applied once, refusal of unsigned, wrongly signed, stale-timestamp, replayed and unlinked requests, the stale-version reply, no DM for a muted event, the actor, a removed or natively inactive member, a turned-off choice or a removed credential, and an archive round trip without the signing secret or links. *Status:* done — `src/core/features/integrations/slack-dm/`, `tests/worker/outbound-broken-destination.test.ts`, `tests/worker/data-http.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/integrations/slack-dm-settings.mjs`. The dated real-provider leg in evidence/slack-004/REAL_PROVIDER.md covered `users.lookupByEmail` and the message format only: no real direct message to a person and no live button click (pattern 3).
- **SLACK-005 — Link previews.** Vector issue, project and document links pasted in Slack unfurl with title, status, assignee and priority only for linked members allowed to see them; others see the link alone. *Acceptance:* two unfurl fixtures for a private-team issue, one for a member and one for a non-member. *Status:* partial.
- **TEAMS-001 — Microsoft Teams.** A Microsoft Teams app offers SLACK-001 to SLACK-005's behavior in Teams: install and identity linking, issues from messages with synced threads, channel notifications, personal notifications and link previews. Each installation has its own bot and Teams app (INTEG-001's table): the Admin creates a bot in the Teams Developer Portal with the messaging endpoint Vector shows and pastes its app ID and client secret, Vector generates the complete Teams app package with that bot ID, valid domains and resource-specific consent permissions, and the Admin uploads it in the Teams admin center as a custom app for the organization; no manifest is edited by hand. *Acceptance:* a setup journey against Bot Framework and Graph fixtures in which an Admin pastes a bot ID and secret, downloads a package whose manifest carries that bot ID and this installation's domain, and the first message to the bot in a channel creates an issue; then the Slack journeys run against Microsoft Bot Framework and Graph fixtures. For the Teams direct message channel (TEAMS-001), the inherited NOTIFY-007 duty is quoted verbatim: "SLACK-004 delivers Slack direct messages for assignments and mentions now, the events NOTIFY-006 mails; the per-event choice for every other event, Admin defaults, digests, mobile push (MOBILE-002), desktop notifications (MOBILE-005) and Teams direct messages (TEAMS-001) remain this clause's." Scope: Teams direct message only. The member chooses this channel per event type; Admin defaults include it; it honors working hours and digests (NOTIFY-010), mutes (NOTIFY-004), actor exclusion (NOTIFY-003) and access (OPS-001), and never repeats a delivered notification. *Status:* missing.

### Support, errors, design and knowledge

- **SUPPORT-001 — Zendesk, Intercom and Front.** Support agents create or link a Vector issue from a Zendesk ticket, Intercom conversation or Front message through the provider's app sidebar; the ticket shows the issue's status and release, and the issue shows the ticket and customer as a customer request (CUST-001). Status changes add internal notes on the ticket (CUST-003). Setup follows INTEG-001's table: Vector generates a private Zendesk app package (a ticket-sidebar app pointing at this installation) for upload in Zendesk Admin Center and creates the Zendesk webhook and trigger through the Zendesk API with an API token the Admin pastes; for Intercom and Front the Admin creates an app in the provider's developer settings from the copy fields Vector shows (Intercom Inbox app URLs and webhook topics; Front plugin and webhook URLs) and pastes its token and signing secret. Support agents then use the sidebar with no setup of their own, linking their Vector identity on first use. *Acceptance:* a setup journey per tool against provider fixtures that mirror Zendesk's private-app upload format, webhook and trigger APIs, Intercom's Canvas Kit initialize request and Front's plugin context, in which the Admin connects without editing a URL and the first sidebar action links a ticket; then signed provider fixtures that link one ticket from each tool and receive one internal note each when the issue completes. *Status:* partial — `src/core/features/integrations/source-ingest.ts` (signed generic source intake with replay identity; provider apps, linking and status sync are absent).
- **SUPPORT-002 — HappyToHelp.** HappyToHelp conversations link to Vector issues with the same behavior as SUPPORT-001 through the sibling's API, so a team running Run Edit Run products needs no third-party support tool. *Acceptance:* a journey across local Vector and HappyToHelp installations that links a conversation and returns the completion note. *Status:* missing.
- **ERROR-001 — Sentry and Datadog.** A Sentry issue or Datadog case or monitor alert can create or link a Vector issue with the error, frequency, affected users, release and stack-trace link; resolving the Vector issue can resolve the Sentry issue, and a regression reopens or comments on the Vector issue by team rule. Setup follows INTEG-001's table: a Sentry internal integration created from the webhook URL, scopes and subscriptions Vector shows, and a Datadog webhook Vector registers through the Datadog API. *Acceptance:* a Sentry setup journey in which pasting the internal integration's token and secret admits the first signed Sentry webhook, then signed Sentry fixtures that create an issue, resolve it back, and a regression event that reopens it once. *Status:* done — `tests/e2e/integrations/error-providers.mjs` (exerciseErrorProviders, invoked by the existing test:integrations push Verify journey) saves the Sentry token and secret in Settings, admits the first signed issue with error details and counts plus release metadata read from the Sentry event API, completes it through the native intake and status doors, resolves Sentry at the provider edge, and reopens once on signed regression with replay unchanged. `tests/worker/error-provider-journey.ts` (exerciseErrorProviders, loaded by the signed source publication test in `tests/worker/source-ingest.test.ts`) covers existing-issue linking, Datadog API registration and mapped monitor and case events, once-per-payload comments including changed request IDs, atomic rollback, rotation, member refusal, revocation and deleted-issue replay. `tests/worker/data-http.test.ts` (native archive HTTP journey) proves safe profile retention, credential exclusion, restored ingress refusal and fresh credential re-entry. Explicit Owner or Admin resolution follows native completion; regression rules are chosen per connected team. Provider transport uses fixed destinations and refuses redirects. No live provider acceptance is claimed.
- **ERROR-002 — Spool.** Spool error groups link to Vector issues with live counts, first and last seen, affected users and releases, and a Spool regression after the issue's fix deploys reopens it for PUSH-004. *Acceptance:* a journey across local Vector and Spool installations that links an error group and sees its counts on the issue. *Status:* missing.
- **DESIGN-001 — Figma.** Figma files, frames and prototypes linked in an issue or document show a live preview and last-modified time; a Figma comment can create an issue through the Figma plugin with a link back. Setup follows INTEG-001's table: the Admin registers the installation's Figma OAuth app from the callback URL Vector shows and members connect their own Figma accounts for previews; Vector generates the plugin manifest with this installation in its allowed network domains, published by an Admin as a private organization plugin or imported by members from the manifest. *Acceptance:* a Figma API fixture that mirrors Figma's OAuth and file-image endpoints, in which a connected member's issue renders a frame preview and an unconnected member sees the link alone, a generated plugin manifest that names this installation's origin as its only allowed domain, and a plugin request that creates one issue. *Status:* missing.
- **DOCS-001 — Notion, Confluence and Google Drive.** Links to Notion pages, Confluence pages, Google Docs and Drive files, Microsoft OneDrive and SharePoint, and Dropbox files (through OAuth clients the Admin registers from the redirect URLs Vector shows, INTEG-001) show title, owner and last-updated previews for members who connected that account, and specs written there can be linked as an issue's specification (PUSH-001). Loom and YouTube video links embed as players through their public oEmbed endpoints, with no account connection. *Acceptance:* one fixture per provider at the network edge — Notion, Confluence, Google Drive, Microsoft Graph for OneDrive and SharePoint, and Dropbox — each rendering a preview with title, owner and last update only for the member who connected that account; and oEmbed fixtures for Loom and YouTube that each render an embedded player with no account connection. *Status:* partial — `src/core/features/links/preview-route.ts`, `tests/worker/link-cascade.test.ts` (named video links load only authorized oEmbed players and comment deletion erases their sources atomically), `tests/e2e/video-players-journey.mjs` (exerciseVideoPlayers, reached by integration-runtime.mjs): explicit public Loom and YouTube players use network-edge fixtures without account connections. OAuth document previews and their remaining integration duties are absent.
- **CAL-001 — Calendar feeds.** Each member and team has a private calendar feed (ICS) of due dates, cycles, milestones and releases for Google Calendar, Outlook and Apple Calendar; revoking the feed URL stops it. CAL-001 inherits PROJ-011's milestone events in calendar feeds. *Acceptance:* a feed request that returns the expected events and a revoked feed that returns 404. CAL-001 inherits PROJ-011's milestone events in calendar feeds. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/base-auth.test.ts` (removal revokes calendar capabilities), `tests/worker/calendar-feed-proof.ts` (exerciseCalendarCapability), `tests/worker/data-http.test.ts` (credential exclusion and restore), `tests/e2e/calendar-feeds-journey.mjs` (calendarFeedsJourney), run by `tests/e2e/cycle-report-runtime.mjs`.  `tests/e2e/calendar-access-cells.mjs` (calendarAccessCells), loaded by `tests/e2e/calendar-feeds-journey.mjs` through `tests/e2e/cycle-report-runtime.mjs`, asserts team-release name inclusion, workspace-release name and release-description exclusion, and team-feed 404 after authority loss; after private-team membership removal it asserts native release read 404 and personal feed 200 with the workspace release retained and the unreadable release name and description absent. Viewer month controls are checked at 1440 and 390; personal-feed rotation and revocation are checked over HTTP. `tests/worker/calendar-feed-proof.ts` (exerciseCalendarCapability), called by the existing tagged `tests/worker/base-auth.test.ts` entry, now asserts team-feed issue due-date, cycle and milestone events with their names, UIDs and dates; the mutation “Drop team due dates, cycles and milestones while retaining releases” fails these assertions. Target-dated releases are included by current creator visibility: personal feeds carry own-team and readable workspace releases, and private-team releases require membership at each request. Team feed authority loss returns 404. Default ADOPTED: 256-bit creator-bound capabilities, only SHA-256 hashes stored, shown once, current creator visibility, 404 after rotation/revocation or authority loss, no cookie and no-store. Credentials are excluded from archives; create new feeds after restore. Personal feeds include assigned work, own-team cycles, milestones for led or assigned projects and currently readable own-team or workspace releases; team feeds carry that team's releases and use contributing projects for milestones.
- **CONNECT-001 — Zapier and Make.** A Zapier app and a Make app expose triggers (issue created, status changed, comment added, project update) and actions (create issue, update issue, comment) over API-003 and WEBHOOK-002 so buyers can connect tools Vector does not integrate natively. Run Edit Run publishes both as public apps in the Zapier and Make directories; each connection asks for the installation URL, authorizes by OAuth (API-004) against that installation, and registers its triggers as REST-hook webhooks (WEBHOOK-002) on it, so no buyer builds or hosts a private app. *Acceptance:* the app definitions' trigger and action tests run against a local installation, including a connection test that enters the installation URL, completes the OAuth consent and registers one webhook on that installation. *Status:* partial — `src/core/features/integrations/api.ts`, `src/core/features/integrations/outbound.ts` (the API and webhooks they need exist in part; the apps are absent).

### Coding agents

- **AGENT-001 — Delegate an issue to an agent.** A member can delegate an issue to a coding agent — Claude Code, OpenAI Codex, Cursor's background agent, GitHub Copilot coding agent or any agent registered through AGENT-006 — from the issue, a shortcut, Slack or a rule (AUTO-002). The human assignee stays accountable; the issue shows the agent as delegate beside the assignee. Delegation sends the issue, its spec (PROJ-013), linked evidence and the team's agent guidance (AGENT-008), scoped to what the delegating member may see, through that provider's own delegation mechanism as AGENT-009 names it — Vector drives each provider's API or the buyer's runner itself, because provider agent apps built for hosted trackers cannot reach a self-hosted installation. Inherited from ROLE-001: Owners and Admins, and Members in the member's teams, delegate to agents allowed under AGENT-004; Guests are refused (role matrix). *Acceptance:* for each named provider, a journey that delegates an issue to a fixture at the network edge that mirrors that provider's real delegation API as AGENT-009's table names it (request paths, payload shapes, status values and webhook signatures), and sees the delegate, the human assignee and the exact context payload the fixture received; plus AGENT-009's hosted run per provider. *Status:* missing.
- **AGENT-002 — Agent session on the issue.** Each delegation opens an agent session shown on the issue, fed by the provider mechanism of AGENT-009, with live progress (plan, steps, files touched, commands run, elapsed time and cost in USD when the provider reports it), questions the agent asks a person (answered in the issue or Slack), and a cancel action that stops the session within one minute and records who stopped it. Where a provider has no mid-run question channel, the session says so and a member's answer starts a follow-up run on the same branch (AGENT-009). Inherited from NOTIFY-001: a delegated agent question or completion notifies once in the inbox, never the actor; the receiving journey verifies this before the event ships. Inherited from SLACK-003: agent completion posts to its bound Slack or Discord channel once, using the shared durable delivery and minimal-message rules; its acceptance journey verifies the bound post. *Acceptance:* per provider, a journey against that provider's mirrored fixture (AGENT-009) where the agent posts a plan and progress, asks a question (or ends a run with one, for providers without a question channel), the member answers and the answer reaches the fixture in the provider's own form (Cursor follow-up, PR comment, runner callback), then cancels, and the session shows the question, answer, cancellation, actor and the provider's stopped status within one minute. Inherited from NOTIFY-001: a delegated agent question or completion notifies once in the inbox, never the actor; the receiving journey verifies this before the event ships. Inherited from SLACK-003: agent completion posts to its bound Slack or Discord channel once, using the shared durable delivery and minimal-message rules; its acceptance journey verifies the bound post. *Status:* missing.
- **AGENT-003 — Reviewable output, human completion.** An agent's work lands as a branch and pull request linked to the issue (GITHUB-003), with the tests it added or ran and a summary; the issue moves to In Review, never to Completed, until a person reviews and merges the PR and the team rule or a person completes the issue. An agent may not merge, deploy, change priority, due date or assignee, or message a customer. Inherited from SLACK-003: reviewable agent completion posts to its bound Slack or Discord channel once, without duplicating AGENT-002’s completion event; its acceptance journey verifies the bound post. *Acceptance:* a journey where the agent fixture opens a PR, the issue moves to In Review only, and the agent's attempt to complete the issue through the API is refused 403. Inherited from SLACK-003: reviewable agent completion posts to its bound Slack or Discord channel once, without duplicating AGENT-002’s completion event; its acceptance journey verifies the bound post. *Status:* missing.
- **AGENT-004 — Agent permissions and budgets.** Admins choose which agents may be delegated to, by which teams, on which repositories, with monthly cost and session budgets and a concurrency cap; every agent call uses a scoped token (ROLE-006), and a budget or permission stop is visible on the issue and to the delegating member. Inherited from SEC-001: credentials are sealed, shown once, least-scoped, excluded from archives, re-entered on restore, rotated on demand and revoked on removal; an archive-then-restore journey proves no credential is exported and the restored connection needs re-entry. *Acceptance:* a journey that exhausts a test agent's session budget and sees the next delegation refused with a visible reason. Inherited from SEC-001: credentials are sealed, shown once, least-scoped, excluded from archives, re-entered on restore, rotated on demand and revoked on removal; an archive-then-restore journey proves no credential is exported and the restored connection needs re-entry. *Status:* missing.
- **AGENT-005 — MCP server.** Vector exposes a Model Context Protocol server (streamable HTTP, OAuth 2.1) so Claude, ChatGPT, Cursor, VS Code and other clients can search, read, create and update issues, comments, projects, cycles and documents as the signed-in member, with the same access rules and audit attribution as the app. *Acceptance:* an MCP client journey that lists the tools, creates an issue and comments on it, and a refused read of a private-team issue for a non-member. *Status:* missing.
- **AGENT-006 — Agent platform.** Third-party agents register as Vector apps (OAuth with an `app` actor), receive delegation and mention events by signed webhook, post session activities (thought, action, question, result) and request input through a documented API, so new coding or ops agents plug in without changes to Vector. The same API is the callback channel for the runner-based providers of AGENT-009 (Claude Code and Codex on the buyer's runners), each run holding a token scoped to its one session. *Acceptance:* a sample agent registered from the docs that receives a delegation webhook and posts three activity types that render on the issue. *Status:* partial — `src/core/features/integrations/api.ts`, `src/core/features/integrations/outbound.ts` (scoped tokens and signed outbound events; app actors, delegation events and activity types are absent).
- **AGENT-007 — Ask Vector.** Members ask questions in plain English ("what is blocking the 2.4 release?", "which Enterprise customers asked for SSO?") and get answers with cited issues, projects and comments drawn only from records they can see; when the answer implies a change (create issues, update statuses), the assistant proposes the exact changes for the member to confirm. *Acceptance:* a journey on seeded data whose answer cites the right blocking issues, and a proposed bulk change that applies only after confirmation. *Status:* missing.
- **AGENT-008 — Agent guidance.** Teams and members keep reusable agent instructions (repository, coding conventions, test commands, definition of done) and saved prompts that apply to delegations and triage agents in their scope, versioned and audited. *Acceptance:* a journey that edits team guidance and finds the new version in the next delegation payload. *Status:* missing.
- **AGENT-009 — Provider delegation protocols.** Vector delegates to each named coding agent through the mechanism below, configured by an Admin in Settings → Agents with the buyer's own provider account, keys and runners (INTEG-001); every mechanism supports the same five steps — start with the AGENT-001 context, progress into the AGENT-002 session, a question and its answer, cancel, and a PR returned and linked (GITHUB-003) — and the session records which provider call carried each step. Repositories must be connected through GITHUB-006 or GITLAB-001 first. *Acceptance:* one fixture journey per provider in which the fixture mirrors that provider's real API or runner protocol as the table names it and the journey drives all five steps end to end, including a signed provider webhook or callback and a refused unsigned one; plus a hosted run per provider on a test repository with the buyer-side setup from the docs, delegating one small issue and receiving one PR, recorded as hosted evidence. *Status:* missing.

| Provider | Start | Progress | Question and answer | Cancel | PR returned |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| GitHub Copilot coding agent | Vector mirrors the issue to a GitHub issue in the chosen repository (GITHUB-007) with the context and assigns it to Copilot through GitHub's issue-assignment API (GraphQL `replaceActorsForAssignable` with the Copilot actor) | Copilot's draft PR events, commits and check runs through the GitHub App webhooks | Copilot asks in its PR; a member's answer posts as a PR comment mentioning `@copilot`, which resumes work | the provider's stop where GitHub's API offers one, otherwise unassigning Copilot and closing its draft PR; the session records which | Copilot's PR, linked by its branch and issue reference |
| Cursor background agent | Cursor Background Agents API `POST /v0/agents` with the prompt, repository, ref, auto-create-PR and a webhook URL and secret | signed `statusChange` webhooks and `GET /v0/agents/{id}` and `/conversation` | the agent's conversation; a member's answer is sent with `POST /v0/agents/{id}/followup` | `POST /v0/agents/{id}/stop` | the PR URL in the agent's target, linked to the issue |
| OpenAI Codex | the buyer's GitHub Actions runner: Vector dispatches the repository's Codex workflow (`openai/codex-action` running `codex exec`) through `POST /repos/{owner}/{repo}/actions/workflows/{id}/dispatches` with the session ID and a per-session callback token; or, for Codex cloud, an `@codex` comment on the mirrored GitHub issue | workflow run and job status through GitHub webhooks, plus AGENT-006 activities the run posts | `codex exec` runs non-interactively, so a run that needs input ends with a question activity; the answer dispatches a follow-up run on the same branch | `POST /repos/{owner}/{repo}/actions/runs/{id}/cancel` | the PR the workflow opens, naming the issue |
| Claude Code | the buyer's GitHub Actions runner: Vector dispatches the repository's Claude workflow (`anthropics/claude-code-action`) the same way; or a Claude Agent SDK runner on the buyer's own infrastructure that receives the AGENT-006 delegation webhook | AGENT-006 activities the runner posts, plus workflow status | the runner posts a question activity and waits; the answer reaches it by the AGENT-006 webhook | workflow run cancel, or the AGENT-006 cancel event that the SDK runner obeys within one minute | the PR the runner opens, naming the issue |
| Any other agent | AGENT-006 delegation webhook | AGENT-006 activities | AGENT-006 question activity and answer event | AGENT-006 cancel event | AGENT-006 result activity naming the PR |

### Target: API and platform

- **API-003 — Complete public API.** A documented, versioned API covers every object a member can see or change — issues, comments, attachments, relations, labels, teams, workflow states, cycles, projects, milestones, initiatives, releases, documents, views, customers, customer requests, notifications and members — with cursor pagination, filtering, idempotency keys, rate limits with `Retry-After`, and the same version checks and access rules as the app. API-003 inherits PROJ-011's milestone field and complete public milestone object operations. *Acceptance:* an API journey that creates a project, cycle, milestone and three issues and pages through them, and a refused private-team read; plus a schema-coverage check that reads the published API schema and fails unless every object named in this clause has list (with cursor pagination and filters), get, create, update and delete operations, or a stated read-only reason for an operation it omits (for example creating a notification). API-003 inherits PROJ-011's milestone field and complete public milestone object operations. *Status:* partial — `schema/api-v1.json` is published at `/api/v1/schema` and drives the explicit route/scope admission table. `tests/worker/integration-api.test.ts` validates the published route inventory and reports operation gaps; full schema-coverage acceptance remains owed. Native comment list/get/edit/delete, project cursor lists and deletion, full milestone operations and inherited issue assignment, cycle list/get/create, workspace label list and detail reads under the explicit labels:read scope, actor/filter-bound cursors and per-token 300/minute limits with Retry-After are implemented.
