The planning journey creates a project, cycle, milestone and three issues, pages them, and tests comment authorship, erasure aliases, scopes and invalidated cursors. Comment reads: `{id, issueIdentifier, body, version, author:{id, displayName}, createdAt, editedAt, url, maxBodyLength:100000, truncated:false}`; IDs are stable, times are ISO-8601 (`editedAt` nullable), the canonical URL opens the issue, bodies are complete, and native author aliases (including Removed operator after erasure) apply. Only comments on readable issues appear; deleted comments are absent, never content tombstones. Suspended-member display remains owed with the native product state under ROLE-012/OPS-004. *Gap:* Idempotency keys and their concurrency/retention proofs; issue deletion through the native receipt/policy door; cycle update/delete; complete list/get/create/update/delete coverage for attachments, relations, labels, teams, workflow states, views, notifications and members; attachment bytes excluded by API-001; initiative and release deletion; documents PROJ-013, customers CUST-005 and customer requests CUST-001 not built; real private-team refusal under ROLE-002; full schema-coverage acceptance. API-001 published wording is retained pending a valid next-release amendment representation: the issued RELEASE.json fails its pinned validator and the schema has no NEXT envelope.
- **API-004 — OAuth apps.** Developers register OAuth 2.0 applications with chosen scopes; members authorize them from a consent screen; Admins can see and revoke every authorized app. Archives exclude vector_oauth_apps, vector_oauth_codes, vector_oauth_grants and integration_tokens. Safe token profiles, usage receipts and audit history remain portable as history only and confer no authority. After restore, an Owner or Admin registers the application again and each member consents again; a pre-restore OAuth token returns 401. Inherited from ROLE-001: Owners, Admins and Members authorize an OAuth app for themselves; Guests are refused (role matrix). *Acceptance:* an authorization-code journey that grants read-only scope, a refused write, and an Admin revocation that ends the next call. *Status:* partial. *Evidence:* `tests/worker/integration-api.test.ts` (oauthAuthorizationJourney: registration and private secret, Owner/Admin/Member consent, Guest/Viewer refusal, exact redirect and PKCE exchange, read-only access, personal-token isolation, Admin revocation, replay and member removal); `tests/worker/role-probes.ts` (probes: OAuth management and personal-consent role cells); `tests/worker/role-viewer-census.ts` (prepareViewerCensus: the three OAuth mutation doors); `tests/e2e/integrations/oauth-apps.mjs` (exerciseOAuthApps: show-once secret, Member consent, read-only access, personal-token isolation, browser recovery, in-view registration refusal, Settings section route and Admin revocation); `tests/e2e/data-roundtrip.mjs` (seedOAuthArchive and verifyOAuthArchive: credential exclusion, retained safe history, pre-restore token refusal and fresh registration/member consent restoring access). *Gap:* ct’s exact-head re-judgment and the independent UI gate remain outstanding. Follow-ups: a Member-owned authorized-app revoke surface, and uip’s untouched N1–N4 and Observations. API-004 stays PARTIAL.
- **API-005 — SDK and docs.** A TypeScript SDK generated from the API schema and public developer documentation with examples for intake, sync and agents ship with each release. *Acceptance:* the SDK's example scripts run against a local installation in CI. *Status:* missing. *Evidence:* `tests/worker/integration-api.test.ts` (sdkIntakeJourney: generated client intake and exact persisted readback, cursor paging, typed comment reads, input, scope, revocation and stale-write errors); `tests/worker/api-rate-limit.test.ts` (SDK preserves 429 and Retry-After); `tests/e2e/integrations/sdk-intake.mjs` (exerciseSdkIntake through integration-runtime.mjs: compiled intake runner against a real local Worker and D1, exact title and identifier, unchanged-storage refusals); `sdk/generate.mjs`, `sdk/build.mjs`, `docs/API.md`. *Gap:* Main's current missing judgment is retained pending review of this SDK evidence. Sync and agent examples and their local-installation CI execution; complete public developer documentation and release packaging and delivery remain owed. Generated types cover only published schema declarations and retain unspecified shapes as unknown; API-003 schema and object-operation gaps and API-004’s independent consent and UI gate remain unchanged.
- **WEBHOOK-002 — Webhooks for every object.** Webhook destinations can subscribe to create, update and delete events for issues, comments, reactions, projects, project updates, cycles, initiatives, releases, labels, customers, customer requests and agent sessions, filtered by team, with the delivery guarantees of WEBHOOK-001. *Acceptance:* a destination subscribed to project updates and comments receives exactly those signed events. *Status:* partial — Owner and Admin configuration routes persist implemented event selections and public-team filters. Native issue-comment roots and replies, including email-intake replies with an external author label, produce stable identifier-only create, update and delete events transactionally through the durable signed-delivery path. Settings subscription controls, their independent Claude Opus gate, the real PROJ-010 project-update producer and the other object and issue-event families remain owed; see the Status row evidence and Gap.

### Target: Import, export and portability

- **DATA-012 — GitHub Issues and Projects import.** An Owner or Admin imports from GitHub through a fine-grained token or the GitHub App (GITHUB-006): issues and pull-request references, comments with authors and times, labels, assignees, milestones (to milestones or cycles by mapping), Projects (v2) items, single-select, iteration and number fields (to status, cycles and custom fields), sub-issues and issue types, with attachment bytes fetched from GitHub's asset hosts, the same preflight, loss review, staged apply, reconciliation and rollback as DATA-006 and DATA-007, and an optional continuing sync (GITHUB-007). An API import is one migration (DATA-017): it pages the whole organization or chosen repositories itself, splitting into runs as needed. *Acceptance:* a run against a GitHub API fixture of a repository with a Project, milestones and comments whose reconciliation report matches source counts, and the DATA-017 scale run for a GitHub fixture of 50,000 issues across five repositories. *Status:* missing.
- **DATA-013 — Jira Cloud and Data Center API import.** An Owner or Admin imports directly from Jira Cloud (API token or OAuth) or Jira Data Center: projects, issue types, workflows mapped to statuses, epics to projects (their Jira status category To Do, In Progress or Done mapped onto project statuses in an explicit step, PROJ-016) or parents, sprints to cycles, fix versions to releases, components to labels, story points, custom fields, comments, worklogs reported as omissions, attachments with bytes, links (Blocks, Duplicates, Relates) and users matched by email, through the same reviewed staged runs as one migration (DATA-017), re-runnable so a second pass imports only issues created or changed in Jira since the first. DATA-013 inherits PROJ-011's sentence "Jira fix versions and Linear milestones import to milestones or releases (RELEASE-001) by explicit mapping": the Jira importer reviews that choice, assignments and reconciliation before shipping. *Acceptance:* a run against a Jira REST fixture with two projects, sprints and attachments whose reconciliation matches, and the DATA-017 scale run for a Jira fixture of 50,000 issues across six projects with cross-project Blocks and epic links. DATA-013 inherits PROJ-011's sentence "Jira fix versions and Linear milestones import to milestones or releases (RELEASE-001) by explicit mapping": the Jira importer reviews that choice, assignments and reconciliation before shipping. *Status:* partial — `src/core/features/data/import/jira.ts` (All Fields CSV file profile; the live API, attachment bytes, versions and Data Center are absent).
- **DATA-014 — Linear API import.** An Owner or Admin imports directly from Linear with an API key: teams, workflow states, issues, comments, attachments with bytes, projects, project updates, milestones, initiatives, cycles, labels, relations, customers with their domains, owners, statuses, tiers, sizes and revenue, customer requests, project statuses mapped by category (PROJ-016) and templates, through the same reviewed staged runs as one migration (DATA-017), paging Linear's GraphQL API within its rate limits and resuming after a limit or interruption. PROJ-001 inherits Linear project start and target dates, priority and project labels onto the native fields, checked in reconciliation, before Linear project import ships. DATA-014 inherits PROJ-011's sentence "Jira fix versions and Linear milestones import to milestones or releases (RELEASE-001) by explicit mapping": the Linear importer reviews that choice, assignments and reconciliation before shipping. *Acceptance:* a run against a Linear GraphQL fixture whose reconciliation includes projects, initiatives, attachment bytes, customers with their requests and each project's mapped status, and the DATA-017 scale run. DATA-014 inherits PROJ-011's sentence "Jira fix versions and Linear milestones import to milestones or releases (RELEASE-001) by explicit mapping": the Linear importer reviews that choice, assignments and reconciliation before shipping. *Status:* partial — `src/core/features/data/import/linear.ts`, `src/core/features/data/import/api-file.ts` (workspace CSV plus an owner-produced API file; direct API, bytes, initiatives and updates are absent).
- **DATA-015 — Plane, Huly and Shortcut import.** Exports from Plane (CSV or API), Huly (JSON export) and Shortcut (CSV or API) import with documented field mappings through the reviewed staged run. *Acceptance:* one fixture per source whose reconciliation report matches. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/plane-huly-shortcut-import.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/data-staged-cli.mjs`, `tests/e2e/data-profile-journey.mjs`.
- **DATA-016 — Transition sync.** During a migration, a team can keep a Jira project or Linear team in one-way or two-way sync for issues, status, comments and assignee for a bounded period, with every unsynced field listed and an explicit end that freezes the source mapping. *Acceptance:* a Jira fixture sync that mirrors a status change each way and stops at the configured end. *Status:* missing.
- **DATA-017 — Whole-workspace migration.** An Owner or Admin starts one migration per source workspace (a Linear workspace, a Jira site, a GitHub organization, or a set of files from one of them). A migration has one identifier and holds any number of reviewed runs — file runs the owner adds (DATA-002 to DATA-010) or runs the API importers create as they page the source (DATA-012 to DATA-014) — and is sized for at least 50,000 issues with their comments, attachment bytes and history. Across every run of a migration: a relation (blocks, duplicate, related, parent, epic) whose other end is in an earlier or later run is held as pending and bound as soon as both issues exist, in whichever order the runs arrive, so no cross-run relation is lost; source people, teams, statuses, labels, projects, cycles, milestones and custom-field options are mapped once for the migration and reused by every run; and a source record lands exactly once even when it appears in two runs. Re-running the migration (or adding a newer export) imports only records that are new or changed at the source since the last run and skips everything already imported, with no duplicates. The migration has one reconciliation report and CSV (DATA-007's contents for all runs together) that also lists every relation still pending because its other end was never supplied, and an Owner or Admin finishes the migration only by explicitly accepting that list, which converts each into a reported omission. Rollback acts per run as today or on the whole migration. *Acceptance:* a Linear GraphQL fixture of 50,000 issues across six teams with comments, attachment bytes and 5,000 blocks, duplicate and parent links of which at least 2,000 cross run boundaries, imported as a migration of at least thirteen runs delivered out of order, whose single reconciliation report matches source counts by team, status, project, cycle and assignee, binds every cross-run relation and reports zero pending; a second pass after the fixture changes 100 issues imports exactly those 100; and a file-based migration whose last run is never supplied finishes only after the owner accepts the pending list. *Status:* partial — `src/core/features/data/import/ids.ts`, `src/core/features/data/import/runs.ts`, `tests/e2e/data-staged-cli.mjs` (stable per-source import identities, staged runs with resume, repeat and rollback, and in-run relations; migrations, cross-run relation binding, the migration report, incremental re-runs and the 50,000-issue run are absent).
- **DATA-018 — Old identifiers and links after a move.** Every imported issue keeps its source key (for example `ENG-123` from Linear or `PROJ-45` from Jira) as an alias that stays resolvable after cutover: pasting a source URL (`linear.app/<workspace>/issue/ENG-123/...`, `<site>.atlassian.net/browse/PROJ-45`, a GitHub issue URL) into search, the command palette or a Vector link opens the imported issue; `/alias/ENG-123` redirects to it for bookmarks; and a branch name, PR title or body, or commit message that names an old key ("Fixes ENG-123") links and moves the imported issue under GITHUB-003 and GITHUB-004 exactly as its Vector identifier would, while the owner keeps the alias active for each source prefix. An alias that collides with a native Vector identifier never shadows it: the native issue wins and the alias is reported in the migration report. *Cites:* BASE-ACCESS-002, BASE-ACCESS-004, BASE-DATA-003, BASE-OPS-003 and BASE-OPS-005. *Acceptance:* after a Linear fixture migration, a journey that pastes a `linear.app` issue URL into search and opens the imported issue, follows `/alias/ENG-123` to it, and a signed GitHub fixture for a PR on branch `eng-123-fix-login` with "Fixes ENG-123" in its body that links and completes the imported issue once; plus a native `VEC-1` whose number collides with an alias and is never shadowed. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/linear-import.test.ts` ("documented-format Linear CSV imports through reviewed native mapping, retains originals and restarts without duplicates or effects", invoking importAliasScenario in `tests/worker/import-alias-scenario.ts`: Linear source URL search, authenticated bookmarks, signed old-key PR completion once, native collisions and reports; Jira and mapped GitHub source URLs, commit messages, backlinks, Owner prefix controls, current privacy, revocation and erasure replay); `tests/e2e/import-alias-journey.mjs` (exerciseImportAliases, invoked by `tests/e2e/integration-runtime.mjs` in push Verify: all three source URL forms in search and the palette, Vector bookmarks, linked completion, prefix controls and collision reports; browser proof at 1440 and 390).

### Security, privacy and agents' data

- **SEC-001 — Integration credentials.** Every integration and agent credential (GitHub App keys, Slack and Teams tokens, provider API tokens, OAuth refresh tokens) is stored encrypted, shown once, scoped to the least access the feature needs, excluded from archives, re-entered on restore, rotated on demand and revoked when the integration is removed. The Slack bot-token paste path (SLACK-003), the Slack signing secret and member-confirmed Slack links (SLACK-004) and Discord webhook URLs are covered now. SLACK-001’s manifest/OAuth install, Events API, @Vector issue creation and explicit identity linking beyond the member-confirmed email link remain SLACK-001’s. SLACK-001, GITHUB-006 and AGENT-004 inherit these credential rules and their provider archive/re-entry acceptance cells before they ship. *Acceptance:* the SEC-001 archive-then-restore HTTP journey excludes source signing and webhook URL secrets, repository and outbound webhook secrets API tokens, the Slack paste-path token, the Slack signing secret, member Slack links and Discord webhook URLs; restored settings require credential re-entry or token replacement, and members re-link Slack. The future manifest/OAuth Slack install, Events API, @Vector issue creation and explicit identity-linking cells remain SLACK-001’s; GitHub App and agent cells belong to SLACK-001, GITHUB-006 and AGENT-004. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/data-http.test.ts`, `tests/worker/outbound-broken-destination.test.ts`.
- **SEC-002 — Model and agent data handling.** Admins choose the model provider for triage suggestions, semantic search, Ask Vector and drafting (for example Anthropic, OpenAI, Amazon Bedrock, Google Vertex AI or a self-hosted model), see exactly which data categories each feature sends (declared in `seed.json` externals), can turn each feature off per workspace or team, and private-team content is never sent for members who cannot see it. Every model provider entry in `seed.json` externals names the provider's commercial or API terms URL, states that customer content is not used for training under those terms, and lists the data categories and retention; Settings shows that entry beside the provider choice, and a provider with no such entry (including a self-hosted model the Admin adds) cannot be enabled until an Owner records its terms URL and confirms its no-training status, which is audited (ROLE-005). *Acceptance:* a journey that turns off triage suggestions for one team and finds no model call for that team's items at the provider fixture; a check that every enabled model provider in `seed.json` externals carries a terms URL, a no-training statement and data categories; and a journey in which enabling a self-hosted model provider is refused until an Owner records its terms and confirms no-training, with the confirmation found in the audit log. *Status:* missing.
- **SEC-003 — Erasure across integrations.** Erasing a member or an issue (ROLE-012, ISSUE-010, DATA-011) also unlinks their Slack, Teams, GitHub and agent identities, removes their content from synced threads Vector posted, deletes cached previews and embeddings, and records which external copies (in GitHub, Slack or a ticketing tool) Vector cannot recall. *Acceptance:* an issue deletion with a synced Slack thread and an embedding that removes both and lists the GitHub comment it cannot recall. *Status:* partial — `src/core/features/issue-deletion/service.ts` (native, file and webhook erasure; posted Slack channel messages, already-sent Slack direct messages and Discord messages cannot be recalled by local erasure; member removal deletes SLACK-004 Slack links; synced-thread erasure, other external identities and embeddings are absent).

### Agent surface (Base-delivered)

#### BASE-AGENT-001 — One MCP door per seed

*Tier:* Agent surface

**Requirement.** Every seed, hosted or self-hosted, serves one remote MCP endpoint at `/mcp` over Streamable HTTP. It speaks the current revision (2026-07-28) and accepts 2025-11-25 clients through the twelve-month deprecation window. It implements `server / discover`, tools, resources and prompts. Lists are deterministic and carry `ttlMs` and `cacheScope: "private"`. Roots, Sampling and Logging are not served.

**Acceptance.**
1. `server / discover` returns the supported versions, capabilities, `serverInfo` and instructions.
2. A 2026-07-28 request works with no handshake, and a 2025-11-25 client completes `initialize` and lists the same tools.
3. An unsupported version returns `UnsupportedProtocolVersion`.
4. Two `tools / list` calls return the same order, with cache fields.
5. A foreign Origin returns 403, and a header and body method mismatch returns `HeaderMismatch`.
6. An oversized body returns 413 with no effect.

#### BASE-AGENT-002 — The seed's jobs are tools, through the UI's own actions

*Tier:* Agent surface

**Requirement.** Each Core job a person does in the product is a tool. Where one is not, the Agent surface says why: it runs on a device, it is a person's interactive screen, it belongs to a connector or the import door, or it is an installation fact. Risk is never the reason: a risky job is a tool with its class (BASE-AGENT-006). Tools are named `<seed>.<area>.<verb>` and declare input and output schemas and truthful annotations. Each calls the same server action as the UI, so validation and authorization are written once, and the seed's embedded agents use the same registry. A business refusal returns `isError` with the seed's stable error code.

**Acceptance.**
1. The Core clause ids in each tool's `_meta`, with the not-a-tool reasons that `server / discover` serves, cover every Core clause in the contract, and no reason is risk; the harness and `rer check` fail on a gap.
2. Each tool's fixture result validates against its `outputSchema`.
3. Read tools declare `readOnlyHint`; tools that can carry destructive declare `destructiveHint`, and tools that can carry external declare `openWorldHint`.
4. A refusal returns the same error code the UI gets for the same action.
5. The UI action and its tool, on the same fixture, give the same record state and the same audit event type.
6. The seed's embedded agents appear in the audit as calls to the same tools.

#### BASE-AGENT-003 — Records are resources

*Tier:* Agent surface

**Requirement.** Each primary record type has a URI template, and tools return `resource_link`s to the records they touch. A read returns exactly the fields the caller's role sees in the UI. File bytes count against any byte budget on the grant. A record the caller cannot open reads as not found, identical to a missing id. Text from customers, imports or files carries an untrusted marker in `_meta`.

**Acceptance.**
1. `resources / templates / list` names every record type in the Agent surface.
2. Reads as two roles return exactly what the UI shows each.
3. An out-of-scope id and a missing id give identical responses.
4. A write's `resource_link` reads back the new state.
5. Bytes over budget return `budget_exhausted` and no partial content.
6. Customer-sourced text carries the untrusted marker.

#### BASE-AGENT-004 — Business identity and short-lived, audience-bound tokens

*Tier:* Agent surface

**Requirement.** The door is an OAuth 2.1 resource server. Its RFC 9728 metadata names the authorization servers this installation trusts. That is the paired Business when Business provisioned it, verified with the owner-claim trust the installation already holds for its import door; otherwise it is the seed's own sign-in. Access tokens are RFC 9068 JWTs with `aud` set to the seed's canonical MCP URI, `sub` the sponsoring person, `act` the agent (RFC 8693) with its specialist, scopes per tool family, and a lifetime of 15 minutes or less (5 by default). The policy scopes rules on the sponsor, agent and specialist the token names. The consent behind the tokens can last longer and is revocable. Tokens travel only in the Authorization header, and there is no passthrough.

**Acceptance.**
1. An unauthenticated call returns 401 with `resource_metadata`, and the metadata names the trusted servers and scopes.
2. A Business token for this seed works, and the same token sent to a sibling seed returns 401.
3. An expired token returns 401, and a token with a lifetime over 15 minutes is refused.
4. A read-only token calling a write tool returns 403 `insufficient_scope` with every needed scope in one challenge.
5. A token in a query string is refused and not logged.
6. Revoking consent in the seed or in Business refuses the next call within 60 seconds.
7. After unpairing, Business tokens are refused and the seed's own sign-in still works.

#### BASE-AGENT-005 — An agent acts as a person in a role, never more

*Tier:* Agent surface

**Requirement.** Every call is authorized as the sponsoring person's current role and access (BASE-ACCESS-004), intersected with the grant's resources, actions and budget. A grant only narrows. No call widens the calling agent's own authority. A call that would widen its own grant or its sponsor's role, or change the company policy or Pause, is refused with `self_escalation`, whatever the policy says; narrowing its own grant is allowed. Other access and security changes are ordinary actions in the access and security classes. An agent never acts after its sponsor is suspended. Anything an agent reads (records, files, emails, imports, other seeds' results) is data and cannot change its scope, its targets or a call's class.

**Acceptance.**
1. For each role fixture, every tool's allow or deny through MCP equals the UI's for the same action.
2. A one-folder, read-only grant holds, and widening through arguments is refused.
3. Downgrading the sponsor changes the next call's result, and suspending the sponsor returns 401.
4. Calls that widen the agent's own grant or its sponsor's role, or change the policy or Pause, return `self_escalation` and change nothing, even under a policy with every class autonomous; dropping a scope from its own grant runs.
5. An injection fixture yields no out-of-grant call and no change of class: record text asking for a wider action, or claiming that a recipient is internal.

#### BASE-AGENT-006 — Every action declares its risk class

*Tier:* Agent surface

**Requirement.** Classes come from the fleet list in RISK-POLICY.md: standard, external, money, commitment, accounts, gated, access, security, destructive, sensitive and live. In the registry, each tool declares the classes it can carry and a server-side classifier that decides them per call from the arguments, current records and, for gated, the provider's answer. The classifier also supplies each class's facts: the measure (items, recipients, amount and currency, people or records), the counterparty (internal, known, new or public), the direction for access, the provider and step for gated, and the data kind for sensitive. A classifier never reads a flag, label or claim supplied by the agent or by record text. A seed cannot add a class; it proposes one for the fleet list.

**Acceptance.**
1. Every tool in `tools / list` carries its possible classes in `_meta`, equal to the Agent surface table.
2. For each tool, fixture calls cover every class it can carry, and the computed classes and facts equal the fixture's expected values.
3. An argument or record text asserting a class or a confirmation (`internal: true`, `confirmExternal: true`) leaves the computed classes unchanged.
4. Money calls carry amount and currency, external calls their counterparty and recipient count, access calls their direction, and destructive calls their item count.
5. No tool in `tools / list` declares a class outside the fleet list, and `rer check` fails on one.
6. A refused call's audit row still records its computed classes.

#### BASE-AGENT-007 — The company's policy decides, at one chokepoint

*Tier:* Agent surface

**Requirement.** Every agent door calls one decision function: MCP, the integration API with agent tokens, and the seed's embedded agents. It evaluates the call's classes against the company's policy, as above. The seed ships no dispositions and fills no gap with one: agent access opens only under a complete policy that setup has asked for (RISK-POLICY.md), and before then every agent call returns `policy_unset` with the setup link and changes nothing. *Autonomous* runs the call. *Review* runs it and adds it to the review feed. *Approval* saves a pending action with server-computed facts and returns `input_required`, with a URL-mode elicitation to the seed's approval page and a pending handle. Each person the rule names approves the exact saved revision in a signed-in browser, and changed inputs need a new approval. The retried call returns the result and a receipt. A rejection returns `approval_rejected`, and a lapse past the wait the rule sets returns `approval_expired`. *Blocked* returns `policy_blocked`, naming the class, rule and revision.

**Acceptance.**
1. The Base harness runs each tool under each disposition for every class it can carry, and sees it run, run and enter the review feed, wait with no effect, or be refused.
2. Before setup is complete, the setup page lists every class the seed's tools carry with examples and no answer pre-selected, an agent call returns `policy_unset` with the setup link and changes nothing, and an incomplete revision cannot be confirmed; a `*` rule completes it, and a tool an upgrade adds with an unanswered class appears in `tools / list` once that class is answered, while the other tools keep running.
3. Under "money autonomous up to $500, approval up to $10,000, blocked above", calls of $400, $600 and $20,000 run, wait and are refused; a per-day cap sends the call that crosses it to the next rule.
4. A call carrying two classes takes the stricter disposition.
5. An approval shows server-computed facts and an expiry, returns the URL elicitation and a handle, and completes once after the named approver approves; a changed input needs a new approval, and a rejection or lapse leaves no effect.
6. The same call through MCP, the integration API and an embedded agent gets the same decision.
7. A policy change applies to the next call within 60 seconds, and the audit cites the new revision.
8. A policy document with a bad signature or another Business's id is rejected, and the door keeps the last valid revision.

#### BASE-AGENT-008 — Truthful attribution

*Tier:* Agent surface

**Requirement.** Every agent act is recorded and shown as `"<agent> for <person>"`, and mail and messages an agent sends outside name it that way as their sender. An approval, a review mark, a confirmation (a warning dialog, a typed-field confirmation, a delivery confirmation), a decision or a signature attributed to a person comes only from that person's signed-in session. When an agent does the equivalent act, the record names the agent, and the act carries its own class; a binding decision or a signature is commitment. Agent doors ignore arguments that stand for a person's confirmation. No tool solves a bot check, or enters a person's own password or 2FA code at an outside service. A provider step that demands a person goes to a person, whatever the policy says; the gated class marks such steps before they run.

**Acceptance.**
1. An agent call carrying a person-confirmation flag is classified and decided exactly as without it.
2. Approving, marking reviewed or answering a policy question with an agent or device token returns 403 and changes nothing.
3. A form-mode elicitation "accept" never completes an approval.
4. Where an agent's decision, signature or field confirmation runs without a person, it reads `"<agent> for <person>"` in the record, the activity view, exports and any certificate, never as the person's own act.
5. After a person approves an agent's prepared action, the record shows the person as approver and the agent as preparer.
6. Activity and exports at 1440 and 390 show `"<agent> for <person>"` for every agent act.
7. Mail an agent sends to an outside address names `"<agent> for <person>"` as its sender.

#### BASE-AGENT-009 — Every agent action is audited with its class and decision

*Tier:* Agent surface

**Requirement.** Extending BASE-OPS-005, each call that reads private data or changes anything records the agent client, name and specialist, and the run or task id (`traceparent` when sent); the sponsoring person, the grant, the tool and the targets; the classes and their facts, and the policy revision and rule; the decision (autonomous, review, approval, blocked, unset or self_escalation) and the outcome (done, refused, pending or failed); the approval or review id and person, and the idempotency key. For a period, the seed's Agents page shows actions by class and decision with the notable ones, the review feed (Mark reviewed, Flag, and Undo where reversible), pending approvals, steps waiting for a person and setup questions. Business shows the same across seeds.

**Acceptance.**
1. One write leaves a complete audit row.
2. Refused, pending, unset and self-escalation calls are audited.
3. The Agents page's counts by class and decision equal the audit rows on a fixture week.
4. The review feed lists exactly the calls decided as review; Mark reviewed records the person, and Undo restores a reversible one.
5. Filtering by agent or class lists only those rows, and export includes every field.
6. Removing the agent keeps its rows and its name.
7. The Agents page works at 1440 and 390 with no serious or critical axe findings.

#### BASE-AGENT-010 — Agents can follow changes

*Tier:* Agent surface

**Requirement.** The seed emits a typed event for every record change and for every approval, review and policy decision. Each event carries a stable id, a type, the record URI, the actor (a person, or an agent acting for a person) and a time. Events never carry secrets or more than the subscriber may read. Agents can read events three ways: `subscriptions / listen`, an `events.list` tool with a cursor, and signed webhooks from Base `integrations` using the same ids. Every channel delivers at least once and filters by access at delivery.

**Acceptance.**
1. A subscribed record changed in the UI produces a notification within 10 seconds.
2. `events.list` returns the same event id the webhook carries.
3. Replaying from a cursor returns the same ids in order.
4. After access is revoked, no channel delivers events about that record.
5. A failed webhook is visible and replays without duplicate effects.
6. `approval.pending`, `approval.decided`, `review.flagged` and `policy.adopted` carry the class and decision and nothing the subscriber may not read.

#### BASE-AGENT-011 — Limits are policy, and Pause stops everything

*Tier:* Agent surface

**Requirement.** Business limits are rules in the company's policy: per-call and per-day thresholds on any class's measure, such as money moved, outside recipients or items destroyed; spend caps on provider cost (models, SMS, email) per seed, specialist or agent, which setup asks with the classes; "no cap" is an answer. Tools with provider cost declare an estimate before they run, and an exhausted cap returns `budget_exhausted` before any partial effect. The seed's capacity limits (request rate, body and byte sizes) protect the installation: they bound rate and size, never daily business volume, are documented and are not policy. A grant carries budgets only when the person granting it sets them. A **Pause agents** control in the seed, and Pause all agents in Business when paired, refuses every agent token within 60 seconds until a person resumes; browser sessions and devices keep working.

**Acceptance.**
1. A rate overrun returns 429 with `Retry-After`.
2. A per-day cap of three outside recipients sends the fourth to the rule that applies past the cap.
3. An exhausted spend cap returns `budget_exhausted` with no partial effect and shows the spend.
4. Under the answer "no cap", a spending call runs and its spend shows in usage; a call that spends nothing is unaffected by any cap.
5. The usage view shows calls, decisions by class, money moved and provider spend per agent.
6. Pause and Pause all agents each stop the next call within 60 seconds, and resume restores access.
7. An agent token cannot resume.

#### BASE-AGENT-012 — Writes are idempotent and conflict-safe

*Tier:* Agent surface

**Requirement.** Every mutating tool requires an idempotency key and takes an optional expected revision. The same key with the same arguments returns the first outcome for at least 24 hours; the same key with different arguments returns `idempotency_conflict`; a stale revision returns `revision_conflict` with the current revision. Long work returns a job handle and a status tool, with no dependence on the Tasks extension.

**Acceptance.**
1. Two calls with one key give one effect and equal results.
2. A retry with a new JSON-RPC id and the same key, after a dropped stream, returns the recorded outcome.
3. The same key with different arguments returns a conflict and no effect.
4. A stale revision returns a conflict naming the current revision.
5. A job handle used by another person's token reads as not found.

#### BASE-AGENT-013 — Manifest, docs and the conformance journey

*Tier:* Agent surface

**Requirement.** `server / discover` instructions and an on-site `/docs/agents` page are generated from the action registry. The page lists tools by job, with each tool's classes, the test that adds each computed class, its scopes, resource templates, event types, capacity limits and error codes, and the specialist role. `prompts / list` carries the specialist's jobs. Base ships the conformance journey, a harness that `rer verify` runs against `rer dev` and seeds run in CI. It is an MCP client; a signed-in test person takes a person's steps (setup answers, approvals, review marks, resume) through the URLs the door returns, and the same browser leg runs the UI-parity actions and the page checks at 1440 and 390. It runs every acceptance point of BASE-AGENT-001 to 012 and of the seed's SURFACE clauses under two company policies: *all autonomous* (`*`), and *held* (standard review, every other class the seed declares approval, which the test person grants), plus the single-class variants a point names. A point that names no disposition passes under both.

**Acceptance.**
1. The docs list exactly the names in `tools / list` and `resources / templates / list`.
2. Each tool's classes, and the test behind each computed one, are documented.
3. The harness fails on any of: a tool without a schema; an effect under approval or blocked, or before setup is complete; a computed class that differs from its fixture; a flag that changes a class or completes a person's step; a role-parity mismatch; a wrong-audience token being accepted; a duplicate effect on retry.
4. Every acceptance point runs under both policies, every decision matches the policy in force, and under all autonomous no call waits for a person or is refused by policy.
5. A stranger agent given only the seed URL completes the specialist journey through MCP.

## Push further

Agents serve the whole job beyond today's up-and-comers: an accepted issue becomes a spec, a tested PR and a verified deployment, and the issue closes only when the evidence shows the user-facing problem is gone. A named person stays accountable at every step.

- **PUSH-001 — Issue to spec.** For an accepted issue, an agent drafts a spec: the problem in the reporter's and customers' words (CUST-001), the evidence (Spool errors, tickets, measurements), acceptance criteria written as checkable statements, the code areas likely affected (from repository search through GITHUB-006 or GITLAB-001), open questions and a test plan. The assignee edits and approves it; only an approved spec is attached as the issue's specification (PROJ-013) and used for delegation. *Acceptance:* a journey on a seeded bug with two Spool errors and a Zendesk ticket where the draft cites both, the assignee edits one criterion and approves, and the approved revision is attached. *Status:* missing.
- **PUSH-002 — Spec to tested PR.** When an issue with an approved spec is delegated (AGENT-001), the agent must return a PR whose tests map to the spec's acceptance criteria; Vector shows each criterion with the test that covers it, the CI result (GITHUB-005) and any criterion left uncovered, and refuses to mark the session done while required checks fail. A reviewer approves the PR in the code host. *Acceptance:* an agent fixture that returns a PR covering two of three criteria with passing checks, and the issue shows one uncovered criterion and the session not done. *Status:* missing.
- **PUSH-003 — Verified deployment.** After merge, Vector follows the merged commit through CI and deployments (RELEASE-002, CI-001) and records when it reached production; if it does not deploy within the team's expected window, the assignee is asked why. *Acceptance:* a merge fixture followed by a production deployment fixture that marks the issue deployed, and a second merge with no deployment that raises one question after the window. *Status:* missing.
- **PUSH-004 — Outcome verification before close.** After production deploy, a verification agent watches the issue's evidence for a team-set window (default 72 hours): linked Spool or Sentry error groups (rate and regressions in the new release), Uptime checks, HappyToHelp, Zendesk or Intercom tickets reopening, Penny requests and the spec's measurable criteria. It then proposes "Verified — problem gone" with the before and after evidence, or "Not fixed" with what it saw. The assignee confirms; only then does the issue reach a Verified state (or reopen). Issues without linked evidence say so instead of claiming verification. *Acceptance:* a journey with a linked Spool error group whose rate drops to zero after the deploy fixture, producing a Verified proposal that the assignee confirms, and a second issue whose error persists and is proposed Not fixed. *Status:* missing.
- **PUSH-005 — Customer follow-through.** When an issue with customer requests is verified, an agent drafts a reply for each linked ticket and request, in the customer's context and without private engineering details, and queues them for the support owner, who edits, approves or skips each; sends go through HappyToHelp, Zendesk, Intercom, Front or Penny, never directly from Vector, and are never sent twice. *Acceptance:* a verified issue with two tickets yields two drafts; approving one sends one fixture message, skipping the other sends none. *Status:* missing.
- **PUSH-006 — Blocked-work follow-up.** An agent watches dependencies, overdue work, stale reviews and unanswered questions; it identifies the missing decision or evidence, asks the accountable person a precise question in Vector or Slack, and revisits at the time they promise, within the team's reminder budget. It never changes priority, due dates or assignees and stops when the owner says so. *Acceptance:* a blocked issue whose blocker owner is asked once, answers "Friday", and is asked again only after Friday; a stop instruction ends follow-ups. *Status:* partial — `src/core/features/dependencies/repository.ts`, `src/core/features/due-reminders/service.ts` (waiting state and due reminders; questions, promised times and Slack are absent).
- **PUSH-007 — Plans with uncertainty.** For a project, release or cycle, an agent uses historical throughput (REPORT-001), current scope, dependencies, unestimated work and people's capacity to propose two or three feasible scope/date options with confidence ranges and the assumptions behind them. The lead chooses; the chosen plan is recorded and later compared with what happened. It never ranks individuals. *Acceptance:* a seeded project where the forecast's 50% and 85% dates match a reference calculation, and the chosen option is recorded with its assumptions. *Status:* partial — `src/core/features/projects/service.ts` (explainable health and capacity risk; forecasts and options are absent).
- **PUSH-008 — Incident follow-up loop.** When an Oncall incident or a PagerDuty incident resolves, an agent drafts follow-up issues from the timeline and postmortem, links them to the incident, proposes owners and due dates for the incident commander to approve, and tracks them to completion with weekly status in the incident channel. *Acceptance:* a resolved-incident fixture that yields drafted follow-ups; approving two creates two linked issues and a weekly status post. *Status:* missing.
- **PUSH-009 — Standups and cycle summaries.** Each morning and at cycle end, an agent writes a short summary per team of what moved, what is blocked, what slipped and what shipped, citing issues and PRs, posted to the team's inbox and Slack channel; members can correct it, and corrections are kept. *Acceptance:* a seeded day of activity whose summary cites every completed and newly blocked issue and no private-team issue in a public channel. *Status:* partial — `src/core/features/cycle-closure/read.ts` (numeric closed-cycle summaries; written summaries are absent).
- **PUSH-010 — Agent accountability ledger.** Every agent action — suggestion, question, draft, delegation step, verification proposal — is recorded with the agent, model, inputs read, the person accountable, cost in USD and outcome (accepted, edited, rejected). Admins see per-team acceptance rates and cost, and can pause any agent everywhere at once. *Acceptance:* a journey that runs a triage suggestion and a spec draft, finds both in the ledger with costs, then pauses all agents and sees the next suggestion skipped. *Status:* missing.

## Honest boundaries

Vector does not do these jobs, and buyers get them as follows (US-first):

- **Code hosting, CI runners and production deploys.** Vector links to GitHub, GitLab or Bitbucket and the buyer's CI and deploy platform (GitHub Actions, CircleCI, Vercel, Cloudflare); it never hosts repositories, runs builds or deploys.
- **Running coding agents.** Agents run in their providers' sandboxes (Claude Code, OpenAI Codex, Cursor, GitHub Copilot) or the buyer's own runners; Vector holds delegation state, context and evidence, never source checkouts or production credentials inside its Worker.
- **Customer feedback board, public roadmap and changelog.** Penny.
- **Support inbox, CRM, error tracking, uptime checks, paging.** HappyToHelp, Orbit, Spool, Uptime and Oncall, with [Porthole](https://github.com/just-every/run-porthole) as the Run Edit Run observability sibling, or Zendesk, Intercom, Front, Salesforce, HubSpot, Sentry, Datadog and PagerDuty through the integrations above.
- **Cross-functional work management for marketing, operations and agencies.** [Pegboard](https://github.com/just-every/run-pegboard) is the Run Edit Run sibling for this job; buyers may also use Asana, monday.com or ClickUp. Vector serves software delivery.
- **Wiki and design tools.** Project documents and specs are a target (PROJ-013, missing), alongside links to Notion, Confluence, Google Drive and Figma; Vector does not take on the company wiki or design-tool job.
- **Timesheets, billing and capitalized-labor reports.** Not provided; buyers use Harvest, Toggl or Clockify linked by issue identifier, and REPORT-005 exports for finance.
- **Enterprise service management (help-desk ITSM, change approval boards) and regulated validation.** Not provided; an issue status is not a legal approval or a compliance record. Buyers use Jira Service Management or ServiceNow alongside.
- **Perpetual parity with every incumbent field and app.** Imports report every omission, and transition syncs (DATA-016) are bounded; Vector does not promise ongoing two-way parity with Jira or Linear.
- **Buyer-branded mobile apps.** Members install Run Edit Run's store-listed apps and receive push through Run Edit Run's relay (MOBILE-001, MOBILE-002, MOBILE-004). A buyer who wants apps under their own name and developer accounts builds them from source and publishes them with their own Apple and Google push credentials; Vector does not publish to a buyer's store accounts for them (Linear does not offer branded apps either).

## Installation and lifecycle

### Current local lifecycle — owner decision, 15 October 2026

For local entry under BASE-ACCESS-001, `rer dev` prints `RER_SIGNED_IN` and a private `RER_OPEN` link. Opening `RER_OPEN` automatically enters as the configured owner with a real, key-gated Base session; legacy `--login` uses the same automatic owner entry. `--as` selects another declared dev persona or signed-out entry. Development has no badge, toolbar or captured-mail UI. Preview serves production output and prints its ready address without captured-mail sign-in. Existing mail storage, erasure guards and applied migrations remain intact. Production requires declared outbound email or an approved Auth SSO setup dependency; Vector’s documented path uses SendGrid, with `rer signin` retained for Cloudflare-credential owner recovery.

### Installation and authority

BASE-ACCESS-001 uses ordinary Base operator-auth for local and hosted sessions. Local development uses automatic owner entry through the private checkout key, exact origin/Host and a valid persisted SESSION_SECRET. Test local entry by opening `RER_OPEN` and reviewing `/setup`, then select a teammate or signed-out entry with `rer dev --as` in that browser. For hosted email sign-in, a confirmation GET does not consume a token; only POST confirms. There is no secret-free or vector.test auth dialect. BASE-ACCESS-002 retains independent document/API/generated-function/private-file admission and the captured actor/workspace/native session through the final SQL projection. BASE-OPS-002 preserves all applied SQL and proves populated 0018→0019→0020→0021 transitions; expiring obsolete transient development captures neither replays mail nor changes delivery ledgers.

The shared installer and terminal local setup preserve configured FIRST_OWNER bootstrap and saved settings. Owner-account deployment and the separate artifact controller retain distinct credential contracts. Local archive export/restore uses Base portability with destination access/effect review; mapped imports, scoped native API tokens (including project list/detail/create/update), registered signed source ingestion and GitHub PR links are implemented. Project start/target dates, priority and enabled workspace labels are editable from the project page, projects list and scoped API, with date validation and per-field activity. Portfolio filters for lifecycle, health, lead, contributing team, target-date range and label keep counts and rows aligned; roadmap editing and Linear project-field import remain requirements. Outbound dispatch requires Settings to identify the hosting account’s excluded domains for the built-in Base enforcing transport, or a deployment-owned enforcing RPC transport binding. Missing configuration remains visible; a failed send never switches transports.

Signed source ingestion implements INTAKE-001/002/003/008/009 and the source registration, rotation and revocation behavior of INTAKE-011 using Base's source audience signature and one native D1 publication transaction. Source/revision identities, exact-repeat results, terminal decisions, observed/receipt order and field-level changes survive restart. INTAKE-011 source management is accepted for today's Owners; ROLE-001/ROLE-003/ROLE-008 inherit the Owner/Admin-only restriction before additional roles ship. See docs/API.md and docs/INTEGRATIONS.md for routes, wire protocols, secret re-entry and the owner-held hosting/live boundary. The Jira file importer is not a live Jira integration: it makes no Atlassian requests and never fetches attachment URLs (DATA-013 is the target API importer).

- **OWNER-CLAIM-001 — Signed owner claim.** An explicitly configured signed installation claims its initial owner through Base native operator-auth using a five-minute signed claim bound to trusted issuer, installation, app origin and canonical owner email. Confirmation GET/HEAD is inert; native same-origin POST admits exactly once. No request provides configuration or setup secrets. Existing initialization and permanent admission ledgers refuse all subsequent claims. Ordinary local configured-owner setup remains the default without RER claim configuration. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/owner-claim.test.ts`, `tests/worker/owner-claim-upgrade.test.ts`. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/owner-claim.test.ts`.
- **EDITOR-001 — Owner-gated editor.** The optional same-origin Base editor is admitted only for a recorded installation owner with a current native Base owner session, active mapped Vector identity and restored-installation grant where applicable. Its config endpoint returns a no-store non-secret lease of at most thirty seconds and exact trusted management origin. Logout/authority loss removes the mount; lease expiry bounds silent revocation. Each hydrated destination controls compiled-edit readiness. Application CSP preserves unrelated directives and permits framing only by self and that exact management origin. A hosted management session and independently registered frame-ancestors remain required; source adoption is not hosted acceptance. *Acceptance:* `tests/worker/editor.test.ts`. *Status:* done — `tests/worker/editor.test.ts`.
